  - **A forced show skips the introduction and the paint barrier.** That's the trade: once time is up, the tab the user asked for comes up as it is (possibly dark until it paints), instead of nothing and then the notice. The arrival cover is kept. How often it fires on Tirth's Mac isn't measured; the switch log's `forced` shows it.
- **Verified after the review's fixes:** unit tests 489, all passing (`npm test` exit 0). The six new tests fail on the build before them. `run.sh all` (load average 3–5): everything passed except `press` (fails on HEAD too, as before), `slowunloaded` (passes alone, as does HEAD's build), and host crashes in `behindbox` and `behindfocus` (both pass alone, on this build and HEAD's). `stalled` passes alone; HEAD's build fails it.

## The kick comes back as a foreground wake, and the paint barrier after a kick is shorter (2026-11-03)

- **Asked:** switching to a sleeping tab as fast as plain Safari, without bringing back the dark flashes. Tirth's switch log for 11:43–12:17 on build 2760b61: 68 switches to loaded tabs, `shownAfter` a median 0.8 s, p90 1.7 s, max 2.3 s. Plain Safari takes 0.3–0.5 s, with a dark flash. No dark flashes except two `capped` wakes.
- **Why:**
  - Background wakes are slow. A wake is an extension script, and WebKit resumes a suspended process for it at background priority (`WebFrame_GetFrameInfo`). In Safari's log for the same session, that resume to the woken process running the script took a median 436 ms, p90 1.7 s, max 5.1 s (n=114). A foreground resume (the tab's view in the window) drew the page a median 110 ms after it (n=9). Round 3's kicks matched that: `wakeMs` 69–203 ms.
  - The kick was taken out this morning on round 4's reading that it showed the other tab for 11–148 ms. Today's log says flips don't reach the screen. In all 55 flips today (29 introductions, 26 restores), the page in front never had a state change: it never left its window, went hidden or lost focus. WebKit runs the update for a view that leaves its window after the next CoreAnimation commit, and that commit came 23–51 ms after the page in front was back. So no frame was committed with the other tab in front. Round 4 probably took the kicked tab's in-window state as visible.
  - The paint barrier after a resume ran to its 400 ms limit (about 520 ms with the timers late) on 23 of the 68 switches. None of those switches was dark.
- **Changed** (`background/tabs.js`, `background/diagnostics.js`):
  - **The kick, inside the wait.** A switch to a tab that may be asleep still waits for its wake with the page before in front. Now, if the wake hasn't answered 60 ms after its scripts went out, the tab is kicked once: brought to the front and the page in front straight back, the same two updates as an introduction (`forwardAndBack`), not counted as one. That's 40 ms for a tab hidden 8 s or more (Safari's median suspend delay). A wake whose scripts haven't gone out by 400 ms (a permission check slow while Safari starts) is kicked at once. A wake taken on from a hover or a press that hasn't answered is kicked at once too.
  - After the kick: the tab's wake stands, and only its hold (`keepAwake`) is sent again, as its instant in the window ended the one that had reached the page. The preparation sent beside the wake is called off, and a new one goes out under its own token. The page then draws (paint barrier) before the show. Nothing is shown before the wake answers, except at the 1.6 s cap, as before. There's no early "stuck" show. A wake that answers within 60 ms of its scripts going out was running after all: shown as any awake tab, with no barrier (`kicked.outcome: 'awake'`).
  - **When there's no kick:** introductions off for the session (Safari left a tab in front, or the page in front blinked: `pageBlinked` covers kicks too), a tab kicked in the last 1.8 s, under 300 ms of the wait left (another switch's flip under way in the window: the kick waits for it), a tab not known to have had a page (not left in this Safari session, or a keep-ready hold found none), a tab never shown (it's introduced once it answers, as before), a tab keep-ready holds, a loading tab, and a held key passing through (those tabs don't go through `focusTab`). Hover, press, reveal and space wakes are unchanged.
  - If Safari leaves the kicked tab in front, it's shown as it is and its preparation is called off. If the kick turns out to have started a load (the tab's process had gone), the tab is waited for behind the covered page, as a restore.
  - **The paint barrier after a kick:** 150 ms at most (130 in the page, 20 for the answer), down from 400: the page drew in the window for the kick. A tab resumed behind before the switch (a hover or a press woke it), introductions, restores and tabs never shown keep 400 ms. The host evidence for those still stands: shown after one rendering update, a tab never shown was the empty view for 23–30 ms in 4 of 6 runs. A tab whose wake answered in time, process running, gets no barrier, as before.
  - The cover for a switch whose page doesn't cover itself now goes up at 150 ms exactly, on a timer of its own. It used to go up at the next 50 ms look after it.
  - **Switch log (DEVELOPMENT DIAGNOSTICS):** `kicked: { at, kickMs, wakeMs, outcome }`. `at` is ms from the ask. `kickMs` is Safari's answer to the two updates: about 110 is normal, near 250 means WebKit held its window for the tab's paint. `wakeMs` is the wake's answer, ms after Safari answered the kick (0: during it). `outcome` is `resumed`, `awake`, `capped`, `loading`, `no page`, `in front`, `not back`, `left` or `hurried`, and `under way` for a switch shown at its page's deadline during the kick (its `kickMs` is null then). A switch forced at the deadline carries its kick too.
- **Risks:**
  - **A kick can freeze Safari's window for up to 250 ms.** WebKit holds the update that brings back a view that was in a window before (`waitForDidUpdateActivityState`) until the page paints, up to 250 ms. A tab resumed in the foreground should paint long before that. A heavy tab under swap may not, and the whole window then waits. The switch log's `kickMs` shows this. In the WebKit host, a busy page made the kick take 254–261 ms every time.
  - **The flip proof is weaker for a kick.** Today's log has 7 flips of tabs that had been in a window (a never-shown mark left on them: the kick's case). In none did the page in front leave its window. But in 3 of the 7, the tab's own content was drawn (unhidden) while it was the one selected, which none of the 22 flips of tabs never in a window had. So for a kick nothing hides the tab from the screen except Safari committing no frame between the two updates. WebKit's 250 ms hold makes the forward leg last until the tab paints, the worst case for that, and none of those 7 had a hold (forward legs 14–55 ms). Round 4 read real kicks as showing the other tab for 11–148 ms. TESTING.md asks for Safari's log on the same day, to check that the page in front never gets an out-of-window update during a kick; `pageBlinked` turns kicks off for the session if the page in front notices.
  - The kicked page sees visible, then hidden: its visibility handlers run. A site may count it as a view, as with introductions.
  - A 150 ms barrier gives a kicked page less time to draw before its show. That rests on its page having drawn in the window for the kick, which isn't measured: a kick whose `kickMs` is near 250 (WebKit's hold ran out) may not have. Such a page then relies on WebKit's own 250 ms hold at the show.
  - A tab awake after all but slow to answer gets a kick it didn't need: a flip and a second preparation, and, if it answers 60–100 ms after its wake went out, a barrier too. One answering within 60 ms is shown as awake, with no barrier, but still waits for Safari's answer to the kick.
- **Verified:**
  - Unit tests: 497 passing (`npm test` exit 0), 8 new in `tests/wake.test.js`:
    - a kick 40 ms after the wake (hidden 8 s), with a second wake, a new preparation and a barrier before the show, and the kick's activations left out;
    - a kick at 60 ms for a tab hidden 2 s, and none for one answering in 30 ms;
    - no kick with introductions off, for a tab not known to have had a page, or with too little time;
    - a kick that doesn't resume the tab: shown only at 1.6 s;
    - another switch after a kick;
    - Safari leaving the kicked tab in front;
    - a blink during a kick turning kicks off;
    - the barrier: 150 ms after a resume, 400 ms for an introduction.
  - Three existing tests changed to expect a kick: the first switch after a background start, a slow permission check, and a late "no page" for a tab left in this session (the kick starts its load, then it's waited for as a load). Seven wait tests now run with introductions off, so they still test the wait alone.
  - 10 of the new and changed tests fail on HEAD's code; the no-kick guard test passes there, as it should. Each guard fails its test when undone: introductions off, a known page, the time left, 40 ms for a likely-suspended tab, the second wake, `late` for a kicked tab, the barrier cap, the call-off when left in front, and the kick for a slow permission check.
- **WebKit host:** `wake-wait` and `wake-cap` now expect a kick before the real show. The page in front must be hidden once (the real switch), never by the kick, and the tab's visibility must change twice for the kick before its real show. New: `wake-awake` (a tab that answers in time isn't kicked before 60 ms). The host can't suspend a process, so there the page is busy instead, and the kick can only cost time. WebKit held each kick 254–261 ms for the busy page, and the barrier after it ran out (291 ms) where HEAD's drew in 7 ms. `run.sh all` (76 scenarios; load average 14 at the start, 4.6 at the end): everything passed except `press` (it fails on HEAD and every slice before it, as recorded) and a host crash (Trace/BPT trap) in `behindlate`. Run alone, `behindlate` passed 1 of 2 on this build; HEAD's build crashed in it 3 of 3. In that run, `wake-wait` was kicked at 63 ms (`kickMs` 258), drawn (barrier 40 ms) and shown about 1 s after the switch started; `wake-cap` was kicked and shown at the cap. Earlier runs, at load averages 4–29, alternating with HEAD's build (git archive, `MARGIN_HOST_DIST`): HEAD's build failed `wake-wait` 3 of 4 and `wake-cap` 4 of 4, with shows 1.3–3.9 s late and the host's background stalling for 3 s. This build failed them too, the same way, and in one run a 130 ms barrier ran to 354 ms with the timers late. Across 15 runs of the three scenarios, the page in front was hidden once (the real switch) and never by a kick in 14. The 15th never showed B at all (load 12, background stalled). No host or server processes were left afterwards.
- **Review, then fixed** (each with a test that fails on the build before it, `tests/wake.test.js` unless said):
  - **A second click on the row during the wait kicked the tab again.** The new switch took on the wake renewed after the first kick, already past due, so it kicked at once: in a scratch test, a re-click at 250 ms gave two kicks, three holds and four preparations, and the show no sooner. A tab kicked isn't kicked again for 1.8 s now (`KICK_AGAIN_MS`, the hold sent after a kick), and the second switch takes on its wake: one kick, one wake.
  - **The 150 ms barrier was for every tab resumed, on evidence that didn't cover it.** The 23 switches that ran the barrier out at 400 ms say nothing about a page that draws between 150 and 400 ms. A tab resumed behind (a hover or a press woke it, never in the window since) keeps 400 ms, as on HEAD; only a kicked tab gets 150 ms, as its page drew in the window for the kick.
  - **A blink during a kick wasn't seen for a switch that doesn't cover itself.** ⌃⌘ and an arrow to a space kick at 40 ms, but the page in front hears of the switch only with its cover at 150 ms, and `shared/switching.js` said a blink only with a switch under way. It now says any (`tests/switching.test.js`); `pageBlinked` already took only one inside a flip's span in that window, and now only from the page in front of the flip (the span keeps its id). The host's `unseen` and `unseennav` caught the first version of this: the tab introduced, hidden 15 ms before its introduction showed it, said a blink and turned introductions off.
  - **The cover waited for the kick.** The wait checked for the cover only after the kick returned: with WebKit holding the kick 250 ms, a space's switch was covered at 296 ms. It has a timer of its own now: 150 ms.
  - **A switch given up during its kick still woke and prepared the tab.** Superseded while Safari answered the kick, it sent a 1.8 s hold, a wake and a preparation to the tab the user had left. Now nothing, except the hold if another switch to that tab is under way (a second click on its row).
  - **The kick threw away the wake under way.** After the kick a second wake went out, queued behind the first in the same process: the wait ended only on its answer. Now the wake stands (its answer comes first anyway) and only its hold goes again: 7 or 8 scripts per kicked switch, was 9.
  - **Another switch waited for a kick before its wake.** A click on another tab while Safari answered a kick waited for that answer (up to 400 ms) before looking up its tab and waking it. Now only a restore, an introduction or a kick of its own waits for it (one flip at a time in a window): its wake goes out at once, and a running tab is shown at once (was 133 ms after the ask in a scratch test with the kick answered in 150 ms). A kick due while another is under way now waits for it, where it was dropped.
  - **A needless kick cost a barrier.** A tab hidden 8 s or more that answers 40–60 ms after its wake went out was running after all, but was drawn first as if resumed. Answered within 60 ms of its scripts going out (`RESUMED_MS`), it's now shown as awake, with no barrier (`kicked.outcome: 'awake'`): Safari answered the kick only once the page had drawn in the window for it.
  - **A switch forced at its deadline left the kick out of the switch log.** It carries `kicked` now, `under way` if it was forced during the kick.
- **Review, not changed:**
  - **Keeping the first preparation after a kick** when it hadn't answered by then (suggested). Safari can send the scripts it held for the resume between the kick's two updates (both are waiting on WebKit's hold), so a preparation sent before the kick can take the page between its visible and hidden changes, and the hide closes the sidebar it carries (`layout.leave`), the case `prepareForShow`'s own turn exists for. One sent after Safari answered both updates comes after both. The cost is one preparation more, in a process running by then.
  - **The kicked page's instant visible can start a storage read** (`shown()`'s refresh), and the preparation after it then reads storage too (a median 70 ms in Tirth's Safari, within the 250 ms the preparation has after the wake's answer). Introductions pay the same. If WebKit applies both changes before the page runs its queued `visibilitychange` tasks (likely for a suspended page, its two changes queued together), both handlers see it hidden and nothing is read. Not proven either way; TESTING.md asks to look at the preparation's `timings.known` and `readMs` on kicked switches.
  - **40 ms for a tab hidden 8 s or more.** A running process answers in a median 17–40 ms, but nothing measures how many answer between 40 and 60 ms. The 2026-11-02 round kicked at 20 ms and saw awake tabs kicked. A needless kick now costs less (no second wake, no barrier within 60 ms), and `kicked.outcome: 'awake'` counts them.
  - Found on the way, not changed: a kick tells keep-ready the tab was shown (`hooks.introduced`), which restarts the tab's hidden-for clock. A tab kicked by a switch given up counts as hidden since the kick, so its next switch kicks at 60 ms, not 40.
- **Verified after the review's fixes:** unit tests 506, all passing (`npm test` exit 0). New in `tests/wake.test.js`: the barrier test rewritten (kicked 150 ms, resumed behind and introduced 400 ms), a second click during the wait, the cover at 150 ms during a kick held 250 ms, a switch given up during its kick, the wake standing after a kick, another tab's wake during a kick, a kick that waits for another switch's, a tab never shown introduced after another switch's kick, a kicked tab running after all, and a forced show during a kick; the blink test now has the introduced tab saying it blinked; `tests/switching.test.js` has a blink with no switch under way. 10 of them fail on the build before the fixes; the two that guard "one flip at a time" pass there, as they should. Undoing each fix fails its test (the 1.8 s, the wait for another kick, the wait before an introduction, the hold for a switch given up, the running check, the barrier split, the wake going out at once, the kick in a forced entry, the cover's timer). `run.sh all` (76 scenarios, load average 3.3–4.3): everything passed except `press` (fails on HEAD and every slice before) and host crashes (Trace/BPT trap) in `behind` and `behindfocus`. `wake-wait` was kicked at 63 ms (`kickMs` 257, WebKit holding the busy page), answered 184 ms after the kick, drawn in 39 ms and shown at 550 ms (about 1 s on the build before the fixes, its second wake queued behind the first). The first full run, on a build whose `pageBlinked` didn't yet check who blinked, failed `unseen` and `unseennav` (above); both pass alone on the final build. `behindfocus` alone: 1 of 2 on this build, 1 of 2 on HEAD's (crashes). `behind` alone, alternating with HEAD's build at load averages 5–16: 5 of 11 on this build, 6 of 8 on HEAD's; every failure on either is the switch's show coming after the check's 4 s (the tab's introduction 5.6–6.3 s into the run, 2–3 s after its load, on both builds alike, the host's background stalling), with nothing of the kick in that path. No host or server processes were left afterwards.

## Startup: switches start, restores wait for the load, one introduction, fewer probes (2026-11-03)

- **Asked:** fix the startup bugs in Tirth's switch log for 11:43–12:17 (build 2760b61) and the round 7 report. After the relaunch at 11:42:23 (Margin's background started at 11:43:01, load average 35–50, every tab unloaded):
  - five clicks never started a switch: `activateTab` entries with `error: 'expired'`, `stuckAt: 'before'` (11:43:46 tab 456, then 457, 456, 454 and 465 a second apart from 11:44:26.8);
  - two tabs restored behind the page were shown before they had loaded (P1117, 19 ms of Safari's empty view; P1472, 1.0 s);
  - six tabs whose first show had no introduction were flipped on their next switch (0.1–0.5 s each);
  - the open-tab repair sent about 450 failing scripts in the first five minutes, in bursts at 11:43:01, 11:43:55, 11:44:26 and 11:44:34.
- **Found** (the code, the report's Safari log, a fake Safari):
  - **"Expired, before"** means the request reached `focusTab` with under 1 s (`REFUSE_MS`) of its page's 7 s left. Everything before `focusTab` was capped except Safari's delivery of the message and the lookup of a tab by its address (`resolveTab`'s `tabs.query({})`, no limit). The switch log didn't say which of them took the time. The repair is the likely cause, inferred, not measured: each of its passes sent every tab's questions at once (two `permissions.contains` and two `executeScript` per unloaded tab), so about 240 Safari calls sat ahead of the switch's own. Safari answers extension calls on its main thread, which was slow at that load. The bursts of failed scripts at 11:44:26 line up with the first of the four expiries. In a fake Safari that answers calls one at a time (15 ms each), a click as the background starts, with 40 unloaded tabs, was shown after 1982 ms on HEAD's code, against 3 s for its page.
  - Each switch also asked Safari for its tab twice before starting (`withTab`, then `activateTab` again), and a row whose ids weren't this copy's always asked Safari for every tab.
  - **Shown before it had loaded:** `loadStarted` took a script that hadn't answered by 600 ms as a load under way (a process still starting looks the same as a busy page). `untilLoaded` then took Safari's `complete` as loaded on its first look, but Safari says `complete` for any tab it unloaded. So the tab was introduced and shown at about 0.6–1 s, before its load had started or committed.
  - **The extra introduction:** the "never shown" mark (`neverShown`) and the "unseen" mark a page leaves as it starts in front (`pageStarted`, kept in storage.session) were dropped only when the background saw the tab's activation. A tab Safari showed before the background had started (the 11:42:43 switch came before it existed), or whose hello arrived after the user had already left it and come back, kept its mark. Its next switch flipped it although WebKit had marked it already.
  - **The probes:** the repair asked each unloaded tab twice per pass (the cut-off probe, then the current-copy probe) over four passes. It didn't remember that a tab had no page. It also made every such tab a cut-off suspect, so a switch to it asked once more.
- **Changed** (`background/tabs.js`, `background.js`, `background/handlers.js`, `content/core.js`, `background/diagnostics.js`):
  - **The repair gives way to switches.** It asks two tabs at a time, the tab in front of each window first. Before each tab, it waits while a switch is asked for and not answered yet (`switchAsked`, counted by background.js from each timed request's arrival) or under way in `focusTab`, for at most 8 s.
  - **A tab found with no page isn't asked again** (`noPageFound`: a script there failed, or WebKit answered it with an error) until it's shown or a tab event says its status or address changed. That holds for the repair's later passes, for a later repair, for `ensureCurrent`'s second question, and for the cut-off suspects (a tab with no page loads with this copy's scripts). The repair skips the second question for it. The passes still run while such tabs are left, so the tab in front, loading at the first pass, is asked again once it has loaded. `inPage` asks for site access once per site (`mayAccessCached`), not per question.
  - **A switch starts without asking Safari what Margin knows.** The tab background.js found goes with the request (`resolved`), and `activateTab` doesn't look it up again. A row drawn from ids that aren't this copy's is found by its address in Margin's own tabs once they're matched (`isSynced`), and only then in Safari's list, by `RESOLVE_LEAVES_MS` (2 s) before the deadline at the latest.
  - **A tab restored behind counts as loaded only once it has been seen loading, or its own document has answered.** `loadStarted` now says how it found the load: `loading`, `ran` (a script ran in its document) or `waiting` (no answer by 600 ms). After `waiting`, `untilLoaded` doesn't take `complete` as loaded until it sees `loading` or the document answers. The wait for that document ends at the usual limit (2.5 s), not the 4 s one for a load seen under way: the tab may not be loading at all (Safari putting its load off), or be a page too busy to answer.
  - **A page that has come into view from hidden says so** (`content/core.js` `cameIntoView`; the wake's `pageThere` answers `'seen'`). For a tab never in front, that means its tab came into the window with a process, so Margin drops its "never shown" mark (`sawInWindow`). The mark a page left as it started in front goes on `'seen'` only if its tab was heard left and back after that page's hello (below, after review). A page that loaded in front and hasn't been left and brought back since still answers `'unseen'`, and is still introduced once on its first return, as before.
  - **Switch log (DEVELOPMENT DIAGNOSTICS):** `before` on each `activateTab` switch: `left` (ms of the page's wait left as the request reached the background), `waited` (from then to `focusTab`), and of those `decided`, `sync` and `resolve`. `restoreStart` on a restore: `loading`, `ran` or `waiting`. A `repair` entry per pass: `pass`, `tabs`, `asked`, `noPage`, `gaveWayMs`, `ms`.
- **Not done:**
  - Proof of what held the five expired clicks. If it happens again, `before` says whether the time went before the request arrived (`left` far under 7000), in the first sync, or in the lookup.
  - The first sync still holds a switch up to 4.5 s (`SYNC_LEAVES_MS`). A switch could go ahead from the row's address before it. Stale ids in the state before the sync would then reach the wake, the flips' check of the tab in front and keep-ready, so this was left as it is.
  - Switches that Safari loaded in front instead of behind (five early ones in the report): the reason restoreTab declined still isn't logged.
- **Risks:**
  - The repair is slower now. Two at a time, and paused while you switch, a pass over many loaded but busy tabs takes longer (each busy tab up to 2 s). A page WebKit hadn't given Margin's scripts after a relaunch gets them later. The tab in front of each window goes first.
  - A tab whose start isn't seen (`waiting`) and that never loads behind (Safari putting it off while its scripts hang) now waits about 2.5 s behind the covered page before it's shown, where it was about 0.6 s. It then loads in front, as before.
  - The test "a page too busy to answer a script once back" now expects the show at the usual limit (2.5 s) instead of under 2.5 s. A busy page whose load wasn't seen looks the same to Margin as a tab not loading yet. The evidence (P1117, P1472) is for the second.
  - `'seen'` takes a page's own visibility as proof that WebKit marked its tab, for a tab never in front. A page that saw itself visible only for an introduction's or kick's instant counts as seen too, which is what those flips are for.
  - A script that fails on a page for another reason (not a missing page) marks it as having none until its next status change or show, so the repair skips it meanwhile.
- **Verified:**
  - Unit tests: 513, all passing (`npm test` exit 0). New: `tests/startup.test.js`. It drives background.js's own listeners against a fake Safari that answers extension calls one at a time, 15 ms each, with 40 unloaded tabs. It covers five things. A click as the background starts is shown within 1.2 s, and at most 4 of the repair's scripts go before its show; HEAD took 1982 ms. Each unloaded tab is asked once over all passes, and site access about once per site; HEAD asked 8 times per tab. A tab is asked again after a status change or a show. A switch to a no-page tab sends no cut-off probe. A row with another copy's ids is found without `tabs.query({})`, with one `tabs.get` before the switch, and its entry has `before`. In `tests/wake.test.js`, two new tests: a restore whose process starts 1.1 s after the selection is introduced only once it has loaded (HEAD: at 1008 ms, before its load), with `restoreStart: 'waiting'`; and a tab opened behind, or marked unseen by an earlier page, whose page says `'seen'` isn't introduced (the second half was wrong: after review). `pageThere`'s test now includes `'seen'`. All of these fail on HEAD's code (run from `git archive HEAD`).
  - Two existing tests changed. "A page too busy to answer a script once back" now expects the show at the usual 2.5 s limit, under 4 s (it was under 2.5 s). The install repair test compares the repaired tabs in any order, because the tab in front now goes first.
  - **WebKit host:** new `restartrepair` scenario (a relaunch with four unloaded tabs, then a switch to one). On this build, pass 0 asked all 5 tabs and found 5 with no page (the tab in front was still loading). Pass 1 asked 1 (that tab, once loaded), and pass 2 asked none. The switch was restored behind (`restoreStart: 'loading'`) with `before.left` 6999. It passes. HEAD's build fails it: it has no `repair` entries and no `before`. The first version of this change ended the passes once every tab left had no page, so the tab in front, loading at the first pass, was never asked again. The scenario caught that, and the passes now go on while such tabs are left. `run.sh all` (77 scenarios, load average 4–8): everything passed except `press` (fails on HEAD and every slice before), host crashes (Trace/BPT trap) in `behindbox` and `behindfocus` (as before), and `catchup-loaded` and `space-press`. Those two passed alone 2 of 2 on this build and 2 of 2 on HEAD's, alternating. On the final build (with `ensureCurrent`'s skip), `restartrepair`, `relaunch`, `unseen`, `unseennav`, `unloaded`, `switching` and `reloaded` pass. No host or server processes were left afterwards.
- **After review** (one reviewer, four findings; each reproduced in a unit test that fails on the build before these fixes):
  - Held, and the biggest: a page also goes hidden and comes back while it stays in front, when its window is covered and uncovered, minimized, or on another Space. WebKit marks a tab only as its view enters a window (`m_viewWasEverInWindow |= isNowInWindow`, which needs the in-window bit to change), so `'seen'` there says nothing about the mark. Dropping the unseen mark on it brought back the dark first return for the tab in front at a relaunch. Tirth's Safari log has exactly this: the tab restored in front at 11:42:43 (pageProxyID 177) went from activity state 30 to 58 and back at 11:42:48–56 (visible bit off and on, still in the window), was first left at 11:43:52, and its first return at 11:45:52 showed 420 ms of Safari's empty view. Over the session, 20 such 31→59 and 4 such 30→58 changes against 124 real leaves (31→32). Now `'seen'` drops only the "never shown" mark (a page that started hidden in a tab never in front can come into view only as the tab enters the window). The mark a page left as it started in front (`pageStarted`) goes on `'seen'` only when the background heard its tab left and back after that page's hello: an activation of another tab in its window, then its own, both after the hello came (`heardActivation`, counted in background.js as each activation and hello arrives, before any wait). That is the case the extra flips came from: a hello handled after the tab's return, its mark outlasting the `tabShown` that came first. Not the return alone: a background starting may be given the activations from before it ahead of a hello, or after it.
  - Held: with Safari slow to answer `tabs.get` (350 ms), `loadStarted`'s script went out past its 600 ms with no time left, and lost to its own timer. Every tab Safari didn't load behind was then `'waiting'`, held to the usual 2.5 s, shown with no document anyway, and not counted as a miss, so restores never went off. The script now gets at least twice as long as the `tabs.get` before it took, and 100 ms. In that case it's `'not loaded'` again, counted, and restores go off after two.
  - Held: a switch counted as under way until its steps were over, not until it answered. One stuck on a Safari call that never answers held the repair 8 s before every tab. Now a switch counts until focusTab answers its page (and background.js's request until its answer, including the guard's), and never for more than 8 s.
  - Held: a row naming a tab just closed could be found by its address among Margin's own tabs as that same tab, before the state had its removal, and its switch failed with "No tab with id". The tab Safari just said is gone is left out of that lookup, so the page is told the tab is no longer open, as before.
  - Verified after the fixes: unit tests 518, all passing (`npm test` exit 0). New: three in `tests/wake.test.js` (an uncovered window doesn't drop the mark; a hello handled after the tab was left and back does, with `'seen'`, and one whose bringing up came after it or whose page hasn't come into view doesn't; the slow `tabs.get` restore), two in `tests/startup.test.js` (a hung switch and the repair; a closed tab's row). Each fails on the build before the fixes (run from a copy of it). The test of a tab never shown whose page says `'seen'` stays, and passes.
  - WebKit host after the fixes (load average 4–5): `run.sh all`, 77 scenarios, failed only `press` (fails on HEAD's build alone too, as on every slice before) and a host crash (Trace/BPT trap) in `behindfocus`. Alone, `behindfocus` passed on this build and crashed on HEAD's. `restartrepair`, `relaunch`, `unseen`, `unseennav`, `unloaded`, `switching` and `reloaded` pass on the final build. The host's web views ignore window occlusion, so the uncovered-window case is covered by the unit test and Safari's log only. No host or server processes were left.

## After a reboot: a switch starts from what Margin knows, a slow Safari gets a quick show, and the page waits past its deadline (2026-11-03)

- **Asked:** Tirth, on build e4538f9 (installed 15:52). After a Mac restart (~15:50, load average 74 at 15:56) he reloaded the first tab and went to another. It "kept loading for a few seconds", then said "Margin didn't answer, so the tab didn't switch. Try again." (`content/core.js`). Other tabs came up after 5–6 s of loading, and the screen went black before the content.
- **Found** (his switch log; every switch was a first visit to a tab unloaded since the relaunch):
  - **The lookup before the switch.** `before.resolve` was 2283–3306 ms on five switches. `resolveTab` waited for Safari's `tabs.get` for up to the deadline less 2 s (about 5 s), even when Margin's own synced state had the tab. So each switch started with about 3 s of its 7 s gone. `focusTabNow` then asked Safari again at its first step (`stuckAt: 'tab'` on one, forced at 5098).
  - **Restores ran into the deadline.** The restore behind the covered page, the introduction, the preparation and the barrier each wait on Safari calls that took seconds. The tabs were then shown as they were (`forced`) at a stuck step (`restored`, `prepare`, `wake`, `introduce`) 3.2–5.7 s after the ask, often before they had drawn: the dark screen.
  - **One answer came after the page had stopped waiting.** Tab 189 was stuck at `introduce`, forced, `unconfirmed`, with `shownAfter` 7292 ms after a `before.waited` of 483. Its forced show was due 4.8 s after the ask. So the background's timers were about 2.5 s late, or Safari's `tabs.update` was that slow. The tab did come up, about a second after its page had said it didn't switch. The space switch at 15:55:57 says the same: a 400 ms barrier ran to 910 ms.
- **Changed:**
  - **A switch starts from Margin's record** (`background/tabs.js` `resolveTab`, `lookupTab`). For a tab that Margin's synced state has, a timed switch waits for Safari's `tabs.get` for `RESOLVE_KNOWN_MS` (150 ms) at most, then goes on from the record. The question to Safari isn't asked twice: `focusTabNow`'s first step takes on the same call (`lookupTab`, one at a time per tab) and waits `TAB_LOOKUP_MS` (500 ms) more at most. If Safari hasn't answered by then, the tab is shown as it is, at once (`plain: 'tab'`), as Safari itself would show it. A tab that turns out to be gone fails its show (below). A lookup still unanswered after `LOOKUP_REUSE_MS` (3 s, a tab's hung process) isn't shared any more: the next switch to the tab asks again. A switch with no page waiting for it (a key, a command: no deadline) waits for Safari's answer, as before. The look at the tab after a restore is bounded too (150 ms when cut short or slow, else 500 ms).
  - **Before the first sync** (`background.js` `untilSynced`, `store.js` `openTabsAsked`): a switch that names its tab by address too (`hint`, a row drawn from the last session's tabs) waits for the sync `SYNC_HINTED_MS` (1 s) at most, not until the deadline less 2.5 s. It then finds its tab among Safari's open tabs by the sync's own `tabs.query`, still under way, rather than a second one queued behind it. The ownership read (`decided`, 17 ms in the log) isn't changed.
  - **A space's switch** (`navigation.js` `showSpace`) takes the tab in front from Margin's record past `FRONT_KNOWN_MS` (150 ms) of Safari's `tabs.query`, as the lookup does.
  - **Margin notices a slow Safari** (new `background/slow.js`). A sign is a Safari call that takes 500 ms or more (timed while still unanswered: the switch's lookup, the restore's and introduction's `tabs.get`, `tabs.query` and flips, the show itself, the first sync), a script that fails with no page after 500 ms (they fail within milliseconds normally), or one of the background's own timers firing 500 ms late. Each call's sign is about a tab (`key`) or about Safari as a whole. Safari is slow while signs from two sources came within 20 s: two tabs, or a tab and the Mac. A tab's signs count once (its hung process), the Mac's once per stall (`ONE_STALL_MS`, 1 s: a stall makes every pending timer late at once). A sign of 30 s or more is the Mac asleep, not slow. A switch is also slow from the moment one of its own calls is (`slowFoundAt`), counted from its request's arrival, so its own 2–3 s lookup counts.
  - **A switch that finds Safari slow plans to show its tab soon after the ask.** Only timed switches. The plan counts from the ask, or for a switch found slow on its way from 0.7 s before then (`SLOW_SINCE_MS`), so a step under way isn't cut off the moment slowness is found. Its steps plan to `SLOW_SHOW_MS` (1.5 s) instead of 1.8 s before the deadline. A restore behind the page ends by `SLOW_RESTORE_MS` (1.1 s; the wait's `until` and `hardUntil` are getters). A restore that would start with under 600 ms left doesn't start, and the tab is shown loading. The introduction is skipped when there's no room for it, before its renewed wake and arrival cover are asked for, and always after a restore cut short (its flip didn't answer in time). The preparation and the barrier get only what's left, except for a tab resumed or kicked while the page before stayed on screen: it's still drawn first, within `WAIT_DRAWN_MAX_MS` and before the forced show. `focusTab` shows the tab as it is by `SLOW_FORCE_MS` (2 s) from the plan's start, as well as by 1.5 s before the deadline. A tab whose wake just found no page isn't woken again before its show.
  - **A restore can't overrun on a Safari call** (`withinWait`). The window's focus, the cover and the restore itself (flip, load start, load wait) are held to the wait's end plus 200 ms. Past that the switch goes on (`restored: 'cut'`): the wait is marked `cut` (its loops stand down: `stopsWait`), its flip is ended with its late activations left out (`endIntroduction` `linger`), and a flip whose query hadn't answered yet doesn't go out at all (`forwardAndBack` checks it's still the window's). A committed page is shown as far as it got. Safari draws it as it goes.
  - **A forced show** with Safari slow is answered once it's out (`FORCED_ANSWER_MS`, 250 ms for Safari's answer at most, never past the page's end less 0.8 s). With Safari quick, or for a tab only Margin's record says is there (`plain: 'tab'`), Safari's answer is waited for until 0.8 s before the deadline, as before; for the latter its lookup saying it's gone counts as the show failing, and the page says it isn't open.
  - **The page waits 3 s past its deadline** for a switch (`shared/switching.js` `SWITCH_GRACE_MS`, `waitsUntil` from `content/core.js` and from Margin's new-tab page, `themes/arcadia/newtab.js`; `newtab/newtab.js` waits until then; `MAX_MS` 8 → 10.5 s). Not for a new tab or a search: those open only before the deadline (`tooLate`). The background still plans to the deadline and answers by it. The grace is for timers that fire late and a Safari slow to carry out the show. `background.js`'s reply guard goes to `waitsUntil` less 0.5 s. `focusTab` refuses only past `waitsUntil` less 1 s. A switch asked after its deadline less 1 s but within the grace is shown at once (`plain: 'late'`), not refused. This goes through `activateTab`, `openPin` and `setSpace`. A page from an older build sends no `waitsUntil` and is treated as before.
  - **Switch log (DEVELOPMENT DIAGNOSTICS):** `plain` ('tab' or 'late'), `slow` on a switch that found Safari slow (`{ what, ms, ago, own, sources }`: the last sign, whether it was the switch's own call, and how many sources counted), and `restored: 'cut'`.
- **Reviewed** (two reviews of the first version, each finding reproduced in a scratch test first):
  - Slow mode came from one sign, for every tab, for 20 s (a hung tab's lookup, one 600 ms stall): the next unloaded tab at normal speed was shown at 1.1 s before its paint at 1.3 s, not introduced. Now two sources, or the switch's own calls.
  - A slow `tabs.update` (the restore's and introduction's flips) wasn't a sign: with `tabs.get` at 300 ms and updates at 3 s, the tab came up at 5.5 s after a second flip. The flips and the show are timed now; the introduction is skipped after a cut; shown at about 1.6 s.
  - A closed tab still in Margin's record, Safari slow to say so and to fail its show, was answered `{ shown }` after 250 ms. Its show's answer is now waited for as before.
  - A space's switch waited 2.5 s on `tabs.query` for the tab in front; a hinted click before the first sync waited for the sync and its write (4.5 s in the test).
  - A switch found slow past 2 s into a normal restore was forced at once, at the step it was at. A resumed tab got no barrier in slow mode. Untimed switches were forced and shown plain. A forced show at normal speed was nearly always `unconfirmed`. A stuck lookup was shared for good. A timer firing a millisecond early by the wall clock left no sign at all (a unit-test flake found that).
  - Margin's own new-tab page sent no `waitsUntil`; `newTab` and `search` waited 3 s longer for nothing.
- **Risks:**
  - Two slow sources within 20 s still slow every switch: unloaded tabs are shown loading, with Safari's empty view until they commit, and tabs never shown aren't introduced, so a dark first frame is possible. Signs taken while Safari was in the background (App Nap) still count, except sleep-length ones. A switch's own single slow call (a heavy tab's `tabs.get` as it loads) shortens its restore from 0.7 s before then.
  - A plain show skips the wake, the preparation and the kick. The sidebar of the tab shown may draw its old highlight for a moment, and a suspended tab shows Safari's empty view until it draws.
  - A cover can now stay up to 10.5 s for a switch whose background has really gone (it was 7 s), before "Margin didn't answer".
  - Not done: the host can't make Safari's `tabs.get` slow (WebKit answers it from the tab objects), so the slow lookup is covered by the unit tests only. The pages' `waitsUntil` (core.js, the new-tab page) has no unit test: no test loads those pages.
- **Verified:**
  - Unit tests: 539, all passing (`npm test` exit 0). `tests/slow-safari.test.js` drives background.js's own listener against a fake Safari whose `tabs.get`, scripts and updates take seconds, and whose background timers can fire late (setTimeout patched for Margin's code only). From the first version: a known tab whose `tabs.get` takes 2.5 s (shown within 900 ms, `plain: 'tab'`); a known tab Safari says late is gone (refused); an unloaded tab whose no-page scripts fail after 600 ms (shown within 1.6 s); a restore whose Safari calls take 3 s from its flip on (shown within 2.1 s, `cut`, no introduction); timers 1.5 s late with a hung show (answered as shown before the deadline); a request with 0.8 s left and a 3 s grace (shown at once; refused without a grace); Safari quick (restored, introduced, not slow).
  - New with the review fixes, each failing on the first version (run from a copy of it): one tab's slow lookup, then an unloaded tab at normal speed (restored `painted`, introduced, shown after its paint; was `loading` at 1.1 s); one 600 ms stall during a restore (the same); signs from two tabs or a tab and a stall, the same tab twice, a stall's timers, sleep-length signs (`isSlow`); flips taking 3 s with lookups at 300 ms (shown under 2.1 s, two updates of the tab, no introduction; was 5.5 s); a closed tab, Safari slow to say so and to fail its show (refused; was `{ shown }`); a space's switch with `tabs.query` at 2.5 s (shown under 600 ms; was 2.5 s); slow found 2.3 s into a normal restore (shown once painted, not forced; was cut at 2.8 s); a resumed tab with Safari slow (drawn first; was no barrier); an untimed switch with a 700 ms lookup (not plain; was); a lookup never answered (asked again 3 s later; was plain again); a call's timer a few ms early (still a sign); a forced show with Safari quick, the switch stuck on storage (confirmed, its 400 ms answer waited for; was `unconfirmed`). `tests/slow-start.test.js`: a hinted click before the first sync, Safari listing the tabs after 1.2 s and the sync's write taking 2.5 s more: shown at about 1.1 s (4.5 s on HEAD and on the first version). Both files passed three runs in a row.
  - WebKit host after the fixes, `run.sh all` (77 scenarios, load average 4–9, 25 for a minute): failed `stalled` (a 1.5 s stall of the host's background made its 3 s deadline, given with no grace, run out), `unseen`, `slowunloaded` (the host itself slow: its `tabs.get` took 2.6 s), `press`, and host crashes (Trace/BPT trap) in `behind` and `behindbox`. Alone: `stalled` passes, answered after 1877 ms (2010 on the first version, 2.6–2.75 s on HEAD); `unseen`, `behind`, `behindbox` pass; `slowunloaded` passes twice at load 5, as HEAD's build does, and failed once on each build at load 25; `press` fails on HEAD's build too. No host or server processes were left.

## After a relaunch, the tabs likely clicked next are loaded quietly, with a note in the sidebar (2026-11-03)

- **Asked:** Tirth. After quitting and reopening Safari, the first click on each tab is slow, because Safari brings every tab back unloaded. They asked for Margin to get the likely tabs ready on its own once Safari has settled, with no screen and no waiting, and a small note in the sidebar that can be ignored. Snapshots of pages while they load come later.
- **Changed:**
  - **The warm-up** (new `background/warm-up.js`, Safari only: `platform.warmUp`). Chrome restores and loads its tabs itself and has no flip to do this with. Once per Safari session, after a relaunch, Margin loads up to `settings.warmUpTabs` tabs (default 6, 0 = off, at most 12) behind the page, one at a time. It picks them in this order (`pickTabs`): the tab used last before the one in front, the tab each other space would show (its icon, a swipe), tabs open on a pin of their space, then the rest of the tabs used last. It only picks tabs that Safari unloaded and nobody has shown since: found with no page by the open-tab repair, never left this session by keep-ready's record, and not in front. A tab that has a page is never touched, so a suspended tab is never kicked.
  - **When.** The first sync says the Safari session is new. That happens after a relaunch, and also for a new copy of Margin (an update, Margin turned on again). So it's taken as a relaunch only if `runtime.onInstalled` didn't say `update`, and if at least 60% of the background tabs the repair could ask had no page (and at least one). After an update the pages are there. It starts no sooner than 8 s after the background started, and after the repair's passes are over. It gives up for good 5 minutes after it starts. Only the window in front when it plans: its picks come from that window's tabs alone.
  - **Gates, before each tab.** No switch asked for or under way. Introductions and restores still on for the session (`pageBlinked`, Safari leaving a tab in front, two of the user's restores Safari didn't load all turn it off). No sign of a slow Safari (`slow.js` `lastSignAt`) in the last 8 s, and the warm-up's own timers not more than 250 ms late. The tab warmed before it finished loading (20 s at most from its flip); if it plays sound by itself, the run ends. The window last focused is the one being warmed, not minimized (a window in Safari's full screen is warmed as any other: fixed below). The tab in front a web page that isn't playing sound. Then the page in front is asked (`warm-up-idle`, new `content/idle.js`). It must be visible and have focus, which it doesn't with the address bar, the find bar or another app focused. Focus must not be inside a frame (an editor, a card field: their keys never reach Margin, which runs in the top frame only). It must not be in full screen, playing media, have Margin's palette open, have the sidebar out over the page or in use, or have a switch under way. No key for 5 s (15 s with focus in a text field), and no pointer, wheel, scroll or input for 2 s. Last, a slow sign from any of the gate's own Safari calls (they are the look at Safari) means no flip. A wait for the same reason again (slow, another window, away, hidden, in a frame) is twice as long each time, up to 30 s. The page's listeners go on only when it's asked, and come off 20 s after the last ask or at the end. The first ask can't know what came before, so the first tab goes about 5 s after the first ask.
  - **How a tab is loaded.** Through the restore a switch uses (`tabs.js` `warmTab` → `restoreTab`): the same flip of the tab to the front and the page in front straight back, the same load-start check, limits (2.5 s, 4 s with no document) and safety nets. With nobody waiting for it (`Wait.quiet`), nothing is covered: no cover on the page in front, and no arrival cover or renewed hold once its document answers. A tab Safari didn't load behind doesn't count toward turning the user's restores off; two end the warm-up instead (`Safari not loading tabs behind`). It's left never shown, as a restore leaves it, and also marked unseen in storage.session (`unseenTabs`), so its first click introduces it and shows it (`introduced: true`, no restore) even from a background Safari started again since. If Safari leaves it in front, the page before is brought back at once, the warm-up stops, and introductions go off for the session as for any flip that fails.
  - **Giving way.** Any switch (asked for, under way, or the user's own in Safari's tab bar) ends the wait at its next 40 ms look, and the tab goes on loading by itself. A click on the very tab being loaded takes it on as it loads, with no second flip. Input on the page in front (its one-shot word, `warmUpInput`) ends the wait too, and the next tab waits for idle again. The flip itself doesn't go out if input or a switch came after the idle answer: it's checked once more after the flip's own `tabs.query`, just before the two updates (`forwardAndBack` `stop`). At most one flip's two updates (about 110 ms in Safari), or one lookup and one script of the load wait, can be in Safari's queue ahead of a switch. Nothing of the warm-up's own (its note, its look at the page in front) is sent while a switch is under way: it waits until it's over.
  - **Kept in storage.session** (`warmUp`: state, the tabs picked, what happened to each). A background that stops and starts again in the same session goes on with what's left, if it's still running and in time. It first asks each tab's page whether it has one, because the new background has no repair's word. A run whose time is up by then is ended, and the pages given the note are told. A run kept by the copy before an update stops. The note's × is kept at once, even by a background just started for it that hasn't read the run yet. Anything else does nothing. A background that stops before the plan is kept doesn't plan again.
  - **The note** (`themes/arcadia/sidebar.js` `createWarmUpNote`, `view.setWarmUp`). "Getting your tabs ready" with "3 of 6" and a thin bar, over the bottom bar of the sidebar, in the sidebar's text color (`--fg`: the gray `--muted` was 2.8:1 on the sidebar), its words wrapping in a narrow sidebar. It ends with "Tabs ready" for 2 s, then fades. If it's stopped it fades at once, and with reduced motion it doesn't fade. Its × stops the warm-up (`warmUpStop`), and is in the tab order as the sidebar's other buttons are. Only the label is a polite live region, filled just after it's shown so WebKit announces it, and a screen reader says it as it comes and as it ends; the count is a progress bar labelled "Getting tabs ready". It's changed in place, never by drawing the sidebar again. It goes only to pages in the window being warmed: by message as each tab starts and ends, by its activation when a tab comes to the front, and with the hello answer. Each page given it is kept (`told`) and gets the last word. A page drops it 15 s after its last news (`forMs`), so a run waiting on a busy user shows no stuck note, and a last word for a page that wasn't showing it shows nothing. A hidden page drops it, and a hidden sidebar hides it.
  - **Setting:** Settings → "After Safari restarts, quietly load this many of the tabs you're likely to open next, one at a time, while you're not typing or clicking (0 = off)" (Safari only). Turning it to 0 during a run stops it before the next tab.
  - **Switch log (DEVELOPMENT DIAGNOSTICS):** `kind: 'warmUp'` entries. `step: 'plan'` has the tabs picked, why each was picked, and the evidence that it was a relaunch (`noPage`, `withPage`, `update`). `step: 'not relaunch'` has the same evidence. `step: 'tab'` has `tabId`, `why`, `waitedMs`, `outcome` (the restore's outcome, `not tried`, or `skipped: …`), `ms`, `restoreStart` and `committed`. `step: 'end'` has `why`, `done`, `total`, `ms`, and `waits` (what it waited for, and how often).
- **Review fixes (same day).** Two reviews of the first version; each finding was reproduced in a scratch test first.
  - Fixed: a warmed tab clicked from a background Safari had started again since wasn't introduced (the never-shown mark was in memory only; now also `unseenTabs`). The warm-up's own late timers were written into `slow.js`, so a user away for a while came back to switches planned for a slow Safari (now kept to its own `quietUntil`). A pick closed before its turn, or a plan made of another window's tabs, kept the run and its "1 of 2" note up for 5 minutes, asking Safari every 1.5 s (now skipped as gone; picks from the window in front only; repeated waits back off). The ×, sent to a background just started, was lost and the run went on. The note's last word could miss pages (a background that took the run over, a run out of time, a page in another window). Safari slow during the gate's own calls still got a flip (12.7 s, 26 s, 39 s at 700 ms a call; now none). Two warm-up restores Safari didn't load turned restores off for the user's own clicks. Typing in a frame looked idle. The note's messages went out during a switch (with 150 ms calls a click took 1.42 s against 1.07 s; now 1.25 s with no pointer move before it, 1.07 s with one). The note stayed up while the run waited on the user. A heavy page past 8 s let a second load start. Contrast, the bar's label, the × out of the tab order and the live region.
  - Kept: Safari leaving a warmed tab in front still turns introductions off for the session. That's evidence the flip fails in this Safari, which the user's own switches use too, so it isn't the warm-up's to keep to itself.
  - Fixed after Tirth's first try (2026-11-03): nothing was warmed and no note showed. He ran `npm run safari`, then quit and opened Safari. Safari applied the new build as it launched, so `runtime.onInstalled` said `update`, and the warm-up took any update as no relaunch, although all 33 background tabs had no page (`step: 'not relaunch'`, `noPage: 33`, `withPage: 0`, `update: true`). Now an update counts as a relaunch when every background tab has no page (3 at least, `UPDATE_RELAUNCH_MIN`). An update with Safari running finds its tabs loaded and still warms nothing. Only a run taken over from the copy before stops at an update. A run planned by the copy that the update started goes on.
  - Fixed after his third try (2026-11-03): nothing was warmed again, and the run's end said why: `fullscreen` 5 times, then `no page in front` 11 times (he had opened a new tab), until it ran out of time. Tirth uses Safari in full screen, and the warm-up waited for any window in Safari's full screen, as for a minimized one. That was a mistake: his own clicks restore behind the page in that same window with the same flip, and Tirth saw no flash there. Now only a minimized window waits; a page's own element in full screen (a video) still does (`content/idle.js`). His first try after the update fix is consistent with working as designed: his pointer was over the sidebar 11 s and 22 s after the launch, and he clicked at 29 s.
  - Sooner (2026-11-03): with that fixed Tirth saw the note, but 13–20 s after Safari opened, long after the tab in front had drawn. Three waits added up: a fixed 8 s from the background's start, 8 s with no slow sign (a launch nearly always leaves one), and the page's first idle answer, which watched for 5 s because it couldn't know what came before. Now: the run starts 3 s in (and still after the open-tab repair, about 3–4 s in his log). The quiet is 3 s, or 8 s while slow.js says Safari is slow (two sources) or the warm-up's own timers run late. The page in front of each window is told to watch for input as the run starts waiting (`prime`, sent to the tab Margin's record has in front: no Safari call, so none is timed into slow.js at a launch's busiest). Its first answer then knows what came, and with no key since that watch and focus not in a text field, a 2 s pause of the pointer is enough. A watch an ask began keeps the 5 s pause. It also waits while the page in front, the one Safari loads itself, is still loading (8 s at most from the run's start), so a warmed tab doesn't compete with it. With the user idle, the first tab goes about 3–5 s after the launch. Two reviewers found the prime's own timed Safari call (it could make Safari count as slow for the first clicks), the short pause applying without a prime, the missing wait for the page in front, and the warm-up's own late timers holding it back only 3 s; all fixed, with tests.
  - Not done: a warmed tab playing sound isn't watched after the next tab's look (WebKit holds media in a page that isn't in a window, which a warmed tab's never is with a process; unverified in Safari, so TESTING.md asks).
- **Why one at a time:** each load is a new web content process, its network and its first layout. Two at once on a Mac that just rebooted is what made Safari slow before. One at a time, each after the one before has loaded, with a gap and the slow check in between, keeps the warm-up from making Safari slow itself. Six tabs take about 20–30 s once the user is idle.
- **Risks:**
  - The flip still moves Safari's selection for an instant. A click on Safari's own tab bar, or a key the page doesn't see, in the few milliseconds between the last check and the two updates could be undone (the user clicks again). The pointer moving out of the page to the tab bar, or a key the page sees, stops the flip.
  - Six more loaded tabs use memory (about 10–16 MB each over plain Safari, plus the sites' own). A tab warmed and left for a while is suspended by Safari, so its first click waits for its wake as any background tab does (the page you're on stays on screen meanwhile), not for a restore.
  - An unsigned build whose storage Safari wipes at every launch might also say `update` at every launch. Then the warm-up never runs; the log would say `not relaunch` with `update: true`.
  - Only the window in front as it plans is warmed. With another window in front it waits (backing off to 30 s between looks) until that window is focused again, within the 5 minutes.
  - Not done: Margin's own start page in front never answers the idle question, so nothing is warmed while it's in front. No WebKit host scenario: the host's background stops about 5 s after it starts and its window never has focus, so the gates can't pass there. The flip itself is the restore's, already covered by the host's `unloaded*` scenarios.
- **Verified:**
  - After the review fixes: unit tests 566, all passing (`npm test` exit 0). `tests/warm-up.test.js` has 22 (9 new) and `tests/idle.test.js` 5 (1 new); all 10 new ones fail on the first version (run from a copy of its build), and so does the click-during-a-load test's new check that nothing of the warm-up's is sent during the switch. New: a warmed tab clicked from a background started again (introduced, two updates); the warm-up's own timers 700 ms late (no slow sign); a pick closed (skipped `gone`, run done, note done); picks from one window (`pickTabs`); the × sent to a background just started (kept stopped, nothing flipped, no note); a run taken over that ends at once or is out of time (each page told gets the last word); `windows.get` slow (no flip until it's quick); tabs Safari doesn't load (the run ends, restores stay on, a click is still restored); a warmed tab playing sound (run ends); focus in a frame or a text field (`idle.test.js`). The full file passed three runs in a row.
  - First version: unit tests 556, all passing (`npm test` exit 0). New `tests/warm-up.test.js` (13) runs background.js's own listeners against a fake Safari whose calls take turns on its main thread. It checks: the order of `pickTabs`; a relaunch run (three tabs flipped once each, in order, one at a time, each `painted`, the page in front never covered and straight back, no arrival cover or wake in them, the note 0→3 then done, kept `done`, the first click introduces a warmed tab); a background starting again (nothing once over or with no record, goes on with what's left, and a tab that has a page by then is asked and left alone); an update (`not relaunch`); the setting at 0; waiting while Safari is slow, typing, not focused, minimized, in full screen or playing; a switch during a load (shown no slower than without the warm-up, the wait ended, nothing more flipped until it's over); a click on the tab being loaded (no second flip before its paint); input during the wait (`hurried`) and just before the flip (not sent); the note's ×; a tab shown or gone since it was picked (skipped); and a guard that a click on an unloaded tab is still restored behind the covered page and introduced. New `tests/idle.test.js` (4) covers `content/idle.js`. With the `stop` check and `Wait.quiet` taken out of tabs.js, two of the new tests fail.
  - WebKit host after the review fixes, one at a time: `unloaded`, `relaunch`, `unseen`, `switching` and `restartrepair` pass; `unloadedtwo` and `deferred` failed while the load average was 21 (the runs back to back) and passed alone at 7–8. `unloadedspace` fails at load 8 on the first version's build too (2 of 2) and on this one 1 of 2: the host stalling under load, not the change.
  - First version, WebKit host after `npm run build`, one at a time (load average about 5): `unloaded`, `unloadedspace`, `relaunch`, `switching`, `restartrepair`, `deferred` and `unseen` pass. `unloadedtwo` failed once (E shown without being loaded behind) and passed the next four runs alone.

## No dark frame on Margin's own switches: a tab is introduced unless Margin knows WebKit has seen it, and a show waits for the tab's page to draw (2026-11-03)

- **Asked:** Tirth. Safari's log, matched frame by frame to screen recordings that day, showed when a switch goes dark. WebKit keeps the page before on screen as a tab is shown (it holds Safari's window up to 250 ms for the tab's first paint) only if both of these hold:
  - The tab's web view has come into a window while its page's process ran (`m_viewWasEverInWindow`).
  - Its content is attached: not suspended, and with no earlier hide still waiting for a paint.

  22 of 22 switches meeting both had no dark frame. 34 of 34 others were dark for 5–1046 ms. 5 of Margin's 16 switches that session were dark (25–63 ms), all to tabs without the mark that Margin showed without an introduction. Tirth asked for both fixes, done properly.
- **Why those five weren't introduced.** The switch log of that session wasn't exported, so which path each took isn't certain. Each path is reproduced by a test in `tests/in-window.test.js` that fails on f841fa2.
  - A tab Safari restored as it was selected, then left before its page's script started. The page started hidden, so it never said it was unseen, and Margin hadn't seen the tab open, so it took it as seen. pg3820 fits: left 0.6 s after Safari restored it.
  - The page said hello before the activation that restored the tab was handled. That activation's `tabShown` then wiped the session mark, and after that only the page's own word protected the tab. That word is gone once the page comes into view while still in front (its window covered and uncovered) or goes to another page.
  - A held key passing through tabs (`navigation.js`): every tab but the last is shown with a plain `tabs.update`, with no introduction and no paint first. pg263 and pg453 fit: warmed tabs, first returns 0.88 s apart, running, no flip.
  - Safari found slow: the plan for a slow Safari left less than the 500 ms an introduction needs, even with seconds of the page's wait left.
- **Changed (T1, WebKit's mark, `background/tabs.js`).**
  - The default is reversed. A tab counts as having the mark only on proof:
    - Margin's own flip of it (an introduction or a kick) with its page answering;
    - Margin's own show of it with its page answering its wake just before (a held key's show in passing too);
    - its page's word that it came into view from hidden, after its tab was known to be out of the window during that page's life. That means the page started out of it (loaded or opened behind), or its tab was heard left after the page's hello and back after that.
  - Every other tab is introduced before Margin first shows it: tabs Safari restored as they were selected, tabs restored or warmed behind, the tab in front at a launch, tabs opened in front, and any tab a new copy or background knows nothing of.
  - The mark is kept through a link in the tab. In WebKit's source it belongs to the web view (WebPageProxy) and is never cleared, through navigations, process swaps and crashes. The task had asked for it to be lost on a process change; the source says otherwise. Losing it on every navigation would introduce most tabs again on their next return.
  - The mark is lost whenever Safari may have made the tab a new web view:
    - a page made from the tab's saved history (`back_forward`), or a tab's first page, from a copy that started with its document;
    - a page saying it started visible that way and hasn't come into view since;
    - a script finding no page;
    - the tab restored behind, opened, or discarded.
  - A Back in front loses it too, which costs one extra flip.
  - Kept in storage.session under `inWindowTabs`, which replaces `unseenTabs`. Each record names the copy that wrote it. A page's hello handled late doesn't undo a proof that came after it.
  - The page's hello now carries its own id (`doc`), whether its copy started with it (`fresh`), and how it was made (`made`). A new quiet word, `inView`, is sent each time the page comes into view from hidden (Safari only).
  - A held key passes over a tab with no proof, or one whose wake didn't find it running. The tab it stops on is introduced.
  - An introduction plans to the page's deadline, not to the slow plan. A restore cut short still gets none.
- **Changed (T2, the layers back before the show).**
  - Margin notes each flip of a tab (introduction, kick, restore). Its show waits for a paint of its page that started after the flip. A kick whose wake answered at once used to be shown right after it, while the kick's own hide was still pending.
  - A paint barrier that runs out after a flip of the tab's own (its hide still pending) is followed by one more under the cover, up to 600 ms more, within the page's deadline. (The review below narrowed this: first it followed every barrier that ran out with new ones.)
  - A click whose tab's wake hasn't answered by 1.6 s goes on waiting until its deadline less 2.2 s (4.8 s of the page's 7 s), the page before on screen. A key or command keeps 1.6 s.
  - The time a switch spends waiting for its tab's own page (its wake's answer once its scripts are out, its preparation, its paint) isn't counted against a slow Safari's plan. Its other steps still are. (The review below narrowed this too.) Two exceptions count all of it, as before:
    - the background's own timers are running late, because a plan to the deadline couldn't be kept;
    - this switch's own Safari calls were slow, because its steps would only wait on Safari.
- **Switch log (DEVELOPMENT DIAGNOSTICS):**
  - `introduced` is now true or false whenever Margin's record of the tab could be read.
  - `markReason`: `proven:<how>`, `unproven`, `never shown` or `loaded behind`, with `:skipped:<why>` when a tab needed an introduction and didn't get one.
  - `layersBack`: `barrier`, `timeout` or `skipped:<why>` (`running`, `capped`, `forced`, `plain-tab`, `plain-late`, `passing`, `no page`…).
- **Not done:**
  - Safari's own tab bar and keys can't be prepared, so a sleeping or unmarked tab can still blink there.
  - A switch Safari is too slow to look up (`plain: 'tab'`), one asked with no time left (`plain: 'late'`), and one forced at its deadline are still shown as they are.
  - A key's or command's wake keeps the 1.6 s cap.
  - The warm-up's flip, should Safari report its two activations the other way round, would still be taken as a real switch. That order hasn't been seen in Safari's log; it's noted in cause.md.
- **Costs and risks:**
  - The first Margin return to each tab without proof gets one flip, about 0.3–0.4 s more, under the cover. That happens after every relaunch, every Margin update (new tab ids), for the tab in front at a launch, and after a Back in front.
  - A click to a tab that stays suspended and can't be kicked can wait up to about 4.8 s under the cover, where it used to be shown at 1.6 s and blink.
  - A page too busy to draw adds up to 0.6 s.
  - Each tab shown sends one quiet message until its tab is proven with that page in it, and each activation writes `inWindowTabs`.
  - A restored page made other than from its history (not `back_forward`) after Margin had proven the tab would keep a mark it no longer has. That isn't seen in Safari's log, where restores go through `goToBackForwardItem`.
- **Verified:**
  - Unit tests: 585, all passing (`npm test` exit 0). New `tests/in-window.test.js` (16). 14 of its tests were run on f841fa2 and all failed there (`noblink/before-fix.log`); the other two were added afterwards. The four reproductions fail there for the reasons above: `[2]`, `[3]`, the passed-through tab shown plain, and `[30]` with Safari slow.
  - Changed on purpose in the older tests:
    - the kick barrier test (retries after the first barrier runs out);
    - a kick answered at once is now drawn first;
    - the warm-up test's session mark is now `inWindowTabs`.
  - The older test files assume a tab they never proved has the mark (`assumeMarkedForTests`), as every tab did before.
  - WebKit host:
    - `navigated` is rewritten: the tab in front at install is introduced once, then a link keeps its proof.
    - `wake-cap`: a click now waits past 1.6 s, is answered at 2.2 s, drawn, then shown.
    - `catchup-slow` proves its tab first.
    - The host's `check`/`checkPage` now wait for a tab the extension is still opening. `introduce` crashed the host (a forced unwrap of `new1`) once Margin answered its request sooner than the tab was made.
    - Full run: 77 scenarios, 5 failed at load 6–8. Alone, `slowunloaded` and `navigated` pass. `press` fails on f841fa2's build too. `wake-cap` and `catchup-slow` were updated as above and pass.
    - A second full run after those updates (load 4–6): 76 of 77 pass. Only `press` fails, as it does on f841fa2. No host crash.
- **Review (2026-11-03).** Two reviewers read the change. Every finding was reproduced against a fake Safari before deciding.
  - **Fixed: a slow Safari's plan was off for almost every click.** The wait for the tab's wake set a flag for the whole switch, so a switch stuck on Margin's own steps (a storage read while a background starts, a permission check) was shown at 5.5 s, not about 2 s. Now only the time actually spent waiting for the tab's own page is left out of the slow plan: the wake once its scripts are out, the introduction's page steps, the preparation, the paint. Margin's own steps and Safari's calls count against it. The switch log's forced entries say how much was spent on the page (`pageWaitedMs`).
  - **Fixed: suspended tabs without proof weren't kicked.** A kick needed the tab to have been seen left this session, so a warmed tab, one Safari restored as it was selected, or one never shown waited for its background resume (seconds), and a key showed it as it was at 1.6 s, dark. WebKit's source says a suspended process still counts as running for the mark (`hasRunningProcess` is false only once the process has ended). So such a tab is now kicked, if its page said hello this session and no wake found it without a page since. The kick is its introduction: it isn't flipped again, and its page draws before the show. The switch log says `markReason: 'unproven:kicked'` or `'never shown:kicked'`.
  - **Fixed: no room to introduce at the end of a click's wait.** For a tab without proof, the wait now ends early enough for its introduction too (deadline less 2.5 s). A tab whose wake still hasn't answered by then is introduced, which resumes it, then drawn and shown. Before, it was shown as it was.
  - **Fixed: an old introduction kept a new web view from being introduced.** A tab introduced by a switch that was then given up stayed marked as introduced. If Safari later unloaded and restored it, it was shown with no introduction. Losing the mark now forgets that introduction.
  - **Fixed: a clock set back could keep a stale proof.** Order values are clock readings stored in storage.session. When a background starts and finds stored ones ahead of its own clock, it moves them all back by the same amount, so a new page's hello still comes after them.
  - **Fixed: a reload dropped a proof.** A reload in a tab with one history entry counted as a new web view. Only `back_forward`, or a tab's first `navigate`, counts now (`shared/platform.js madeAfresh`).
  - **Fixed: barrier retries with no reason.** A barrier that runs out is followed by one more only while a flip of the tab's own still has its hide pending (introduction, kick, restore), as one longer barrier rather than new ones every 400 ms. A tab only resumed behind is shown when its barrier runs out, as at f841fa2: 23 of 68 such switches on 2026-11-03 ran to that limit and none was dark.
  - **Fixed in part: background wake-ups from `inView`.** A page stops sending it once the background answers that its tab is proven with that page in it. The storage writes per activation stay: they're already merged into one per event, and delaying them could lose proofs when Safari stops the background.
  - **Not changed: the ⌘T hand-off.** It shows the previous tab with no wake and no introduction, as before this change. Introducing it there would flip a page whose palette just opened, and hiding it for that instant could close the palette. That can't be checked without Safari. The switch log now says `markReason: 'unproven:skipped:no wake'` for it.
  - **Not changed, to check in Safari: flips of tabs that already have the mark.** After a Margin update every tab is unproven, so each one's first return is flipped, including tabs WebKit has seen. For those, WebKit may hold the flip's first update up to 250 ms. Every flip in the 2026-11-03 log was of a tab without the mark, so whether such a hold can flicker isn't known. TESTING.md has the check.
  - Verified: unit tests 596, all passing (`npm test` exit 0, twice). 11 new tests are in `tests/in-window.test.js`, and the barrier test in `tests/wake.test.js` was changed. Run against the change as it was before the review, 10 of the new tests and the barrier test fail. The 11th is a guard (a slow Safari still waits for a page that is resuming) and passes on both. The slow-restore test now expects `introduced: false` with `markReason` ending in `:skipped:cut`, where it expected `null`.
  - WebKit host after the review, one at a time: `switching`, `introduce`, `unseen`, `unloaded`, `wake-wait`, `wake-cap`, `wake-awake`, `navigated`, `unseennav`, `unloadedtwo`, `restartrepair`, `relaunch`, `slowunloaded` and `catchup-slow` pass. Full run (load average 4–6): 76 of 77 scenarios pass, `press` included. `space-click` failed once (C's sidebar not out yet when the host clicked it) and passed 3 of 3 alone.


## After the proof runs: a show only into a free page, the flip's own word, the proof kept with the page, the keys taken first, no reload in front (2026-11-04)

- **What the runs showed** (e2 and e3 on 9f1611f, Safari's log and the screen recordings side by side):
  - The fix of 2026-11-03 held: 10 of the 11 switches that could be judged had no dark frame, and no introduction's flip reached the screen.
  - One WebKit empty view was left: Instagram, 2.05 s. Its tab had WebKit's mark, its layers were attached and its process ran. Its main thread was stalled (a page mostly swapped out). Margin showed it with one of its own scripts still unanswered, WebKit's 250 ms hold ran out, and the screen stayed dark until the page drew.
  - That introduction wasn't needed: the flip was held, so WebKit had seen the tab. Margin had no proof because 9f1611f had just been installed, and its records are kept per copy.
  - The about 500 ms of dark on the Gmail switch was the tab before, reloaded in front by the cut-off repair, as was MSU's re-layout.
  - Lost presses were a separate cause: on Safari, ⌃⌘↓ reaches Margin only as a manifest command, and WebKit gives the page the key first. Outlook and Instagram call `preventDefault` on it.
- **S1: the show waits for one more look at the page.** Just before the show, after all of the switch's other scripts, one more script goes to the tab (`pingInPage`). The page runs Margin's scripts in order, so its answer means it got through all of them and is free now. Margin's holds are left out of it: a running hold doesn't block the page.
  - Another look only if that one took over 100 ms, or the page handled a change of its visibility less than 150 ms before (Instagram's second stall started 30 ms after it handled being hidden). One at a time, never on a timer.
  - Three looks at most: a page that answers each one slowly is busy but running, and is shown after the third (`gate.outcome: 'answered'`). A page free costs one round trip.
  - Once the first look is 100 ms out, Safari is asked something about no page (`tabs.get`). If that isn't answered within 250 ms either, Safari is slow, not the page: the gate ends there without waiting for the page (`slow safari`), and the show goes out as it would with no gate. slow.js counts that call by its own measure.
  - Changed after review: the first version pinged the page in front instead, and judged only once the target's own look had answered. A busy page in front (Gmail at 128–169% CPU in the proof run) then passed for a slow Safari, and a slow Safari still held a click until the target answered. The first version also had no limit on the number of looks.
  - A later key press gives the show up (`hurried`): the tab stays behind, the page before stays in front, and the next press goes on from that tab. Before, a press only cut the barrier short and the show still went out.
  - Changed after review: the next press now goes on from that tab however long the gate waited. navigation.js chained presses only within 1.5 s of the earlier press, and the gate holds a key's show for up to 4 s, so a press 1.5–3 s in started again from the tab in front and came back to the same busy tab.
