The pointer over the page is handed to the page (at most 30 times a second) for peek, which can't hear it through a window that ignores the mouse.
- **Keys:** ⌃⌘↓/↑ (next and previous tab), ⌃⌘→/← (spaces) and ⌘T (the palette, in the front window's overlay) as Carbon hot keys, held only while a linked browser is the active app and its front window has a shown overlay, let go the moment it isn't. The arrows go as `req navigate { action }` for that window. A key another app holds is skipped and noted. Fixed combinations for now; whether they should follow Settings' shortcuts (Safari's defaults there are ⌥⌘↓/↑ for tabs) is open, and so is taking ⌘T from Safari while the app draws Margin.
- **Typing:** a field of Margin's getting the focus makes the overlay a key panel (non-activating, so the browser stays in front); leaving it, or a press on the page or another app, hands the keyboard back (`yieldActivation` to the browser, then its `activate`), and the palette closes.
- **The link going down** closes that copy's overlays (no "Reconnecting" state yet); a gap asks for the state again every 2 s until it comes.
- **Not reused from `feat/native-panel`:** SkyLight, AXSafari, Eligibility, SpacePlacer, the lease. No private macOS calls and no Accessibility in the shell (the 2026-11-05 SkyLight exception for instant Space switches isn't built).
- **Tests:** `npm run test:app` (`tools/app-tests/run.sh`, about 4 s once built, about 16 s with both Swift builds from nothing): 156 unit checks of the shell's logic (messages, frames, chunks, the mirror, matching and stacking, pass-through, layouts into `setLayout`, keys and their gate against a stand-in for Carbon plus one real register of an unused key, coordinates), and 51 end-to-end checks: the real link, shell and overlays in an accessory app that never activates, against `fake-extension.mjs` (Node, several copies at once), with stand-in browser windows at (−20000, −20000): linking and refusals, the theme in pieces, nothing done until the browser is in front, matching, the overlay drawn and shown over its window, Arcadia's `setLayout` (260/8/8/8 at 1280×800), its picture (the sidebar drawn, the slot transparent), a real press on a row becoming `activateTab` for the window, deltas drawn, a gap and its resync, a resize followed, re-stacking, a corrupt theme refused and Neo-brutalism drawn, the keys held and let go with the front app, ⌘T's palette taking and giving back the keyboard, a second profile, a copy not in charge, a newer link taking over, a silent copy closed, `bye`, nothing over 1 MB. Linked to overlay shown: about 430 ms. `npm test` 929 (9 new: `tests/ui-host-app.test.js`).
- **Still to check on screen, in real Safari** (not done: nothing is shown on Tirth's screen in tests): the overlay over full-screen Safari at `.normal` level with this collection behavior; pass-through over a real page (clicks, scrolling, selection, drags); typing in the palette with Safari staying in front, undimmed; the keys only while Safari is in front; moving between two full-screen Safari windows; and the extension's end of all of it, which is step 3's.

## Margin 2, Phase 2 step 1: Margin's tabs, and the data Margin.app will own (2026-11-05)

Margin's sidebar is now the real list of tabs (`PLAN.md` §6, "Margin's tabs"), all on the extension's side, and the lasting data set the app will keep is written down and built in JS (`DATA.md`). The link and the app's store are step 3. What `PLAN.md` doesn't already say:

- **Where a Margin tab lives.** An open one is its browser tab's record in `tabs`, which now carries the Margin tab's own id (`mid`: `t-` and ten letters or digits) and its place (`order`). One that isn't open is in `keptTabs`, keyed by its own id, with its address, title, icon, space, place, last use, when and why it stopped being open (`window`, `sleep`, `not restored`, `gone`). Not one map of every Margin tab: the rest of the extension (switching, keep-ready, cut-off, tab updates) keys everything by the browser's tab id, and a second list would have to repeat every open tab's fields and be kept in step on every event. The data set's `tabs` collection is the two together. `keptTabs` is stored inside `openTabs`, in the same write as the open tabs, so a tab that closes or opens is never in both lists or in neither.
- **Places in the list.** The sidebar used to sort a space's tabs by their index in the window's tab strip. A tab that isn't open has no index, and shows in every window, so each tab now has a number of its own (`order`; the list newest first, highest at the top). An open tab's place follows its strip: a new tab gets a place between its neighbours there, or after everything (the newest) at the end; after a move or a sync, the tabs that no longer follow their window's order get new places between their neighbours and the longest run that does keeps theirs. Places closer than 1e-6 are renumbered. A tab that isn't open keeps its place, and dragged in the list it takes one next to the row it's dropped by. An open tab dropped next to one that isn't open doesn't move (its place follows the browser's strip; refused, as a drop that can't happen is).
- **What a close does** (`state/model.js closedTab`, from `tabs.onRemoved` and its `isWindowClosing`):
  - closed on its own (Margin's ×, ⌘W, Safari's tab bar): to the archive, as a web page; anything else is just gone. New: until now only Margin's × archived; ⌘W and Safari's tab bar forgot the tab.
  - lost with its window: kept, not open, in its space, with the window it was lost with (`from`: a key, when, how many web pages it had).
  - closed by Margin itself (`background/closing.js`): Margin says what for before it closes. `keep` (put to sleep) keeps it; `done` (the ×, Clear and auto-archive archive first; a ⌘T tab handed off; Escape on a fresh new tab) does nothing more.
  - A tab pinned in its space isn't kept: its pin is the lasting record and opens it again.
  - A sync that finds open tabs gone that no close was heard for (a close queued behind the sync) keeps them with the id they had (`wasTab`); a close heard after that archives the kept tab, as a close on its own. A tab Safari lists again at the same address is the same Margin tab. After a renumbering those ids are dropped (they mean other tabs then).
- **Opening one** (a click, the palette, a pin): `tabs.create` in the window where it was clicked (the page's, or the window the app names: `activateTab` with a Margin tab's id and `windowId`), before the first open tab placed after it in that window, read from Safari's own tab list (Margin's indexes lag after tabs before it close), then linked (`linkKept`): the new browser tab takes the Margin tab's id, place and space, and counts as used now and as placed on purpose (never taken for a tab coming back). A pin whose tab isn't open opens as the space's kept tab at its address, if there is one.
- **Lost tabs are kept tabs now.** An earlier build's `lostTabs` become kept tabs as they're read, and `lostTabs` is written as null (records without Margin ids get theirs in the same write, so they keep them). A window coming back (History › Reopen…, Safari's restore, a tab whose address came late) still takes back tabs lost with a window within a day (`reclaimTabs`, the same matching and its two-pages rule); after that they stay, not open, until the archive takes them. Kept tabs are at most 500, the least recently used beyond that archived.
- **After a relaunch:** restored tabs are matched by address and order (`matchTabs`, as before) and keep their Margin tabs; the records nothing matched become kept tabs (`not restored`). The restored tabs nothing matched also try the kept tabs lost with a window (the same rule as a window coming back), so if Safari reports its windows closing as it quits, the first sync after the relaunch still gives them back. The kept tabs' times move forward by the time Safari was closed, as the open tabs' do. Whether Safari sends any tab events at a quit isn't known (see below).
- **Auto-archive** applies to kept tabs by the same rule (`staleKept`): unused for `archiveAfterHours`, not pinned.
- **Sleep in Safari is closing** (`platform.sleepTabs: 'close'`): a pass does every check it did (the front tab, sound, loading, a draft in any frame, the never-sleep list, a page asked last), then closes the tab as Margin's `keep`. Its page's process is gone (WebKit host). The sleep page stays in the code for tabs an earlier build left on it: they're still moved to a new copy's page and woken when shown, and "Wake all" wakes only those now. Chrome and Edge keep `tabs.discard`.
- **What pages draw.** The sidebar's rows for a space are its open tabs in this window and its kept tabs, by place. A kept tab's row has its own id (a string), `open: false`, `notOpen: true`; Arcadia fades its icon and title, Neo-brutalism its title and icon box (`m-class:not-open`). Its menu is a tab's. The palette finds it. ⌘⌥↓/↑ skip it (each would open as it was passed). A space's `tabCount` includes its kept tabs. A space with only kept tabs, switched to, opens its start page as an empty space does, rather than opening one of them by itself.
- **The data set** (`DATA.md`, `shared/data-set.js`, `shared/data-merge.js`): collections of items with ids, `updatedAt` and tombstones: spaces, pins, folders, Margin's tabs (web pages only), the archive, settings (one item per setting), theme settings (one per theme), site icons (one per origin), user themes. Not the browser's ids, which window shows which space, page zoom or the sidebar's scroll.
  - **The merge:** the later `updatedAt` wins; on the same time a deletion wins over a live item, else the change wins over what was there; changes apply in order. Eight JSON vectors in `tests/fixtures/data-merge/` for the Swift store to run.
  - **The queue:** the extension's storage stays its cache. Beside it, `dataMeta:<collection>` keeps each item's time and fingerprint and the tombstones. The time is taken as the state's keys are written; the meta goes in a `set()` of its own 1.5 s later. In the same `set()` as the state, the WebKit host's `switching` failed 2 of 4 runs (a carried sidebar not open on the switched-to page's first frame) and passed 6 of 6 once the meta was written apart; the starting build passed 4 of 4. A meta write lost with the background is made good at the collection's next write, with that later time. Pages ignore these keys. The first time, everything starts at 0, so a cache Safari wiped (started from defaults) never overwrites the app's data; every change after has its time. The queue is every item and tombstone after the last change the app took, one entry per item. Tombstones are kept 60 days, at most 2,000 per collection.
  - **The converter** (`dataSetFromStorage`) turns any build's storage into a data set for the first link; `stateFromDataSet` refills the cache from one, keeping the live state.
  - User themes are in the converter but not queued: nothing writes them yet (Phase 6).
- **Tests:** unit 966, from 920 (new: `tests/margin-tabs.test.js` 23, `tests/kept-tabs.test.js` 7 through background.js's own listeners, `tests/data-set.test.js` 16 with the eight vectors; updated for kept tabs: model, owner, spaces-switch, view model, copies, sleep). `npm run test:theme` 58. E2E not run.
- **WebKit host:** 89 scenarios (84 and five new: `keep-window`, `archive-own`, `reopen-kept`, `relaunch-match`, `sleep-close`, all passing). `sleep-show` (now: put to sleep, closed and kept, its row opens it again), `sleep-kept`, `sleep-frames` (the tab put to sleep is checked as kept) and `sleep-copy` (a tab an earlier build left on the sleep page) were rewritten for closing and pass. The host has two new steps: `close` (Safari closing a tab, with `windowClosing`) and `restart`'s `drop` (a tab Safari doesn't restore). Against the starting build's run (61 of 84): 67 of 89 pass. Failing, all on the starting build's list: `arrival`, `behind`, `behindbox`, `behindfocus`, `behindkeys`, `behindlate`, `loadcover`, `restartrepair`, `shellcover`, `shelllate`, `sleep-switch`, `slowunloaded`, `unloaded`, `unloadedheavy`, `unloadedlate`, `unloadedspace`, `unloadedtwo`, `unseen`, `unseennav`, `wake-reveal`, `wake-space`, plus `space-press` once (its "back in front" check, in the full run only; 5 of 5 alone on this build and on the starting one, as `press` and `space-click` were flaky before). `press` and `space-click` passed. `sleep-switch` still tests a switch waking a sleep page behind a cover (gone since Phase 1 step 4); Phase 3 deletes it.

**Open, for Tirth's Safari:**
- What Safari sends when it quits: nothing (expected), `isWindowClosing` for each tab (handled: the relaunch matches them back), or closes one by one without it (they'd go to the archive; the relaunch would then not find them). TESTING.md asks.
- A window closed by closing its last tab with ⌘W: if Safari reports that tab as the window closing, it's kept rather than archived.
- A window new to Margin within a day of a window closing takes back that window's kept tabs when it opens two of its pages (one if it had one), as a window coming back after a relaunch did before. That now covers windows closed by the user too: a new window with the same two pages within a day shows them as the same Margin tabs, in their space.

## Margin 2, Phase 2 step 3b: Margin.app's data store (2026-11-05)

On `feat/margin-app` (from 1bf7a38). The app's end of the lasting data: its files, the merge in Swift, and the link's data messages. The extension's end (sending `data.hello`, taking the app's answers) is step 3a's, built alongside against `DATA.md`. Nothing in `extension/` changed. What `DATA.md` ("The app's files", "Messages") and `PLAN.md` §6 don't already say:

- **Where the code is.** `app/shell/DataSet.swift` (items, changes, the merge, tombstone pruning), `DataStore.swift` (the files), `DataSync.swift` (the data messages, the read API). `Shell` owns one `DataSync` (`shell.data`) and passes it each link's `data.*` messages; `LinkMessage` and `AppMessage` carry them as `.data(kind, body)`. The Xcode script picks the new files up (`app/shell/*.swift`); Tirth's `xcode/` wasn't touched and needs `npm run safari` (or `make-xcode.sh`) again.
- **Files: one per collection**, not one per data set: a new site icon rewrites `siteIcons.json` only, and a space's rename doesn't rewrite the icons. A `set.json` per profile, written last, says the set exists and keeps the `acked` the app has confirmed. Each file is written to a temporary file, flushed, its old version kept as `.bak` by a hard link, then renamed over (the file is never missing). Writes run on a queue of their own, in order; the set in memory changes at once, and `data.ack` goes only once everything is on disk.
- **A crash mid-write** can leave collections a mix of old and new files, each whole. That's safe because nothing was acked: the copy resends the same queue and the merge gives the same result again. Checked with a store that stops as if it died after a given number of files (`DataStore.crashAfterWrites`), then a new store (a relaunch) over the same folder.
- **The app's files older than the copy's `acked`** (a restore from an older backup, or a write a power cut lost after its ack): the app asks for the whole set (`data.need`), merges it into its own and sends the result back. Not in the contract before; added to `DATA.md`.
- **Names on disk.** Profile ids may be `..` or differ only in case (the link allows `A-Z a-z 0-9 _ . : -`), so a folder name writes everything but `a–z 0–9 _ -` as `%XX` (`P1` is `%501`). Margin's collections keep their names; any other is written out after `x-`. The real names are inside the files.
- **Tombstones** are pruned as a set is saved: older than 60 days, or beyond the newest 2,000 in a collection, as the extension does. The merge itself never prunes (the vectors' times are from 2024).
- **Parts** are split by collection and item at about 900 KB, as `DATA.md` had it, with a `transfer` id added so parts of two snapshots never mix. An item too big for one message (only a user theme package can be) is left out and named in `omitted`. Every collection is named in part 0, an empty one too, because `stateFromDataSet` leaves a collection the set doesn't have as it was.
- **Answers the contract didn't have:** a first link's converted set isn't sent back (only `data.ack`); a change from the copy that loses to the app's newer item gets that item back in `data.changes`; changes that come before the profile has a set are held until the snapshot. `upTo` is the newest time among the copy's changes stored, never lower than its `acked` (not the newest time in the whole set: the app's own items aren't the copy's to confirm).
- **For step 4:** `shell.data` reads any profile's set, linked or not (`profiles`, `set(of:)`, `values`, `marginTabs`, `onChange`), and takes the app's own changes (`change`). `shell.notOpenTabs(profile)` gives a profile's Margin tabs that aren't open: all of them when it isn't linked, else those no open tab of its copy carries.
- **Revisions, so a relink doesn't resend everything** (asked for after review: the whole set on every `data.hello` was a few MB at each link with many site icons, and Safari's background restarts often). Each stored set has a `rev` (a number, a dash, random hex), new at every save that changes it. The app's snapshot and, once the link is in step, its acks carry it; the copy names the last one it took in `data.hello`. If that's the set's `rev` from before the queue's merge, the copy has every item the app had, so only the queue's losing changes go back (`data.changes`) and the ack gives the new `rev`. Anything else (an app-side change the copy hasn't had, a backup, another Mac's set, no `rev`) gets the snapshot. Compared with the `rev` before the merge, not after: the merge itself makes a new one. The ack carries the `rev` of its own save, not a later one still being written, so a copy never claims an app change it hasn't been sent. The app's own `data.changes` carry no `rev` (the copy's next hello then gets a snapshot: a resend, never a miss). `set.json` gets the new `rev` before the collections are written, so a crash between them can't leave new app items under a `rev` the copy holds. `acked` alone couldn't do this: it says what the app has of the copy's, not what the copy has of the app's.
- **Not done:** user themes too big for a message stay the extension's until Phase 6.
- **Tests:** `npm run test:app` about 6 s once built, about 21 s with both Swift builds from nothing. Unit 262 checks (106 new, `tools/app-tests/data-tests.swift`): the eight merge vectors read from `tests/fixtures/data-merge/`, each also through the files and back; malformed changes; tombstone limits; file names; saving, reading back from another store, the backup kept and used for a damaged file, temporary files removed, a crash mid-write and during a first write, a later format's data left alone, profiles listed; the data messages against a stand-in copy (first link with a snapshot in parts and a held queue, changes both ways, one that loses answered, another profile's message refused, another epoch's left, a relink with a queue, files restored from an older backup, a wiped cache, parts within budget, 2.4 MB of icons none over 1 MB, revisions: kept by a save that changes nothing, new otherwise, a hello with the set's `rev` getting no snapshot with or without a queue, an unknown `rev`, an app-side change, a link not yet in step acked without `rev`; a write that fails sending no ack). End to end 91 (40 new, `e2e-data.swift`, with `fake-extension.mjs` using the extension's own converter and refill): first link (1.2 MB of icons in 3 parts), relink with nothing changed (no snapshot, the same `rev`), relink after an app-side change (the snapshot), relink with a queue and an unknown `rev` (the set back in parts, refilled by `stateFromDataSet`), relink in step with a queue (no snapshot, the losing change answered, a new `rev`), a second profile kept apart even when it names the first, a wiped cache refilled, the app's own change reaching only its profile's copy, and a crash mid-write followed by a relaunch that loses nothing. All in temporary folders. `npm test` 975, unchanged.

## Margin 2, Phase 2 step 3a: the extension's end of the link (2026-11-05)

From `feat/margin-app` 1bf7a38, beside step 3b (the app's data store). The extension links to Margin.app and, while it's linked, draws nothing in pages. `app/PROTOCOL.md` has every message; what it doesn't say:

- **Safari's background is a page** (`background.scripts`, non-persistent; `platform/safari/manifest.json`, `scripts/build.js`). Chrome keeps its service worker (the link is off there: `platform.nativeApp`, and no socket is ever made in a worker). Nothing in the background needed a worker. In the WebKit host the page background answered 22 s after install (`bg-page`), where the worker always stopped after about 5 s.
- **The link client** (`background/link.js`, built from `feat/native-panel`'s `panel-link.js`; `background/app-link.js` wires it to the store). One lasting `connectNative` port, a `ping` on it every 20 s, from the copy's start (it also hears `appUp`); `wantLink` by `sendNativeMessage`; the `link` with its nonce; the socket, `welcome`'s key checked; `hello`, `snapshot`, the theme in 512 KB pieces with their SHA-256, `keys`, `data.hello`; ticks answered, 12 s of silence a drop; requests run through the same `route()` as pages' messages (`background.js`), with `{ app: true, tab: { windowId } }` as the sender, only the types the UI makes (`APP_REQUESTS`); deltas (icons as a diff), the window list looked at every second and on window and tab events, sent on a change. Reconnects with the kept link (0.25–4 s), then asks again (5 s doubling to 60 s), and on every page's `hello`. The guard from 2026-10-30 stays: a socket only from a document, and a socket whose constructor never returned turns the link off, now only until another version of Margin (the panel's needed clearing by hand). (Later: every build is 0.1.0, so that was forever; since "Margin 2: Safari reliability" it's for one Safari session, then 24 h.)
- **The profile id** is `p-` and 20 random letters or digits, made once per profile's storage and kept with `acked` and `rev` in `dataMeta:link` (pages ignore `dataMeta:` keys). Safari wiping the storage makes a new one: the app then sees a new profile. No stable profile id is visible to an extension in Safari (open question below).
- **Data** (`DATA.md` as step 3b changed it): `data.hello` with the queue since `acked` and the `rev` it has; `data.need` answered with the converter's set, whole; the app's `data.snapshot` taken once all its parts are in (`transfer`, `part`, `parts`), this copy's changes since its hello merged on top, `omitted` items kept as this copy has them, part 0's `rev` kept once it's applied; `data.changes` both ways (this copy's as the store writes them: `store.js onDataChanges`); `data.ack`'s `upTo` and `rev` kept. Data messages are taken in order, and only of this link's epoch and profile. A refill (`store.js adoptDataSet`) gives the meta the set's times for every item that agrees with the set (keys in any order), so nothing the app sent is queued back; an item that differs (a live title the app hasn't heard) keeps its own time and is sent. `rev` is dropped when storage started from defaults.
- **What pages know** is storage.local `appLink` (`shared/app-link.js`): `linked`, `reconnecting` or `off`, and each window's layout. Storage because a page must know before it draws anything: it's in the page's first read. `hello` answers with the background's own word (it wins), and the window's page in front gets each new layout at once (`appLayout`). The background writes it only as the copy in charge; a background starting finds the last one and keeps it as `reconnecting` while it tries its kept link.
- **The box from the layout** (`boxFromLayout`): the window's insets less where the viewport sits in the window, from the page's own outer and inner sizes: extra width on the left (Safari's sidebar), extra height on top (its toolbar). In the page's zoomed pixels, to half a pixel. A page that doesn't know its window yet takes the layout every window shares. No layout for the window yet: the box stays the registered first-paint one. **First paint** is unchanged (Phase 3's): the registered `at-<left>` file from the settings. With Arcadia it's the app's own layout (260/8/8/8 with Keep open at the default width, the frame alone without), so a new page is right from its first frame in that case; another theme, width or Safari's toolbar shown means a move when the content script runs (about as late as today's in-page host's first box).
- **Pages while the app draws** (`content/core.js`): the shadow host goes into the document only once the page knows it draws Margin (after its first storage read, a few ms), and comes out when the app takes over; the runtime is torn down (`unmountUi`) and drawn again when the link is off. Kept: the box, gestures, pinch, the draft check, navigation keys. Notices from pages ("Margin isn't responding") don't show while the app draws.
- **Keys (Tirth, 2026-11-05: the app owns them while linked).** The copy sends `keys`, each command's combinations from Settings, with Safari's manifest command keys too (pages take both: ⌥⌘↓ and ⌃⌘↓), and the sidebar's (`toggle-sidebar`, new in the app: `Shortcuts.swift`, `Shell.swift`). The app holds the front window's copy's keys (`AppShortcuts.rebind`), more key names than before (punctuation, F1–F12). The extension's `new-tab`, `open-palette` and `toggle-sidebar` commands and a page's ⌘T do nothing visible while the app draws and `push` `keyHeard` for the app's log. Navigation commands still navigate. Unlinked, everything as before. Not seen in the WebKit host: the host has no menu or hot keys, so whether Safari's own ⌘T or the extension's command ever fires past the app's hot key is for real Safari.
- **Settings and the new-tab page while linked:** drawn by the app's overlay, in the page slot. Margin's new-tab page draws nothing (and reloads itself when the link comes or goes); the overlay draws the theme's launcher, with its Settings, while that page is the window's tab in front (`ui/host-app.js` `launcher`, `inNewTab`, `tabId` follow it; `#settings` opens Settings). An address chosen there opens in that tab (new handler `openHere`). Safari's own New Tab making a tab: closed again as before, and the palette opens in the overlay (`push palette`). Reason: they're Margin UI, and the overlay can draw over the slot (its hit boxes take the pointer there); drawing a second theme inside the page under the overlay would mean two sidebars.
- **A link dropping (Tirth's default):** the app keeps the overlays where they are for 6 s, dimmed, "Reconnecting to Safari…" (`Shell.reconnectGrace`, `overlay.html`); the copy coming back takes them back as they were (theme and keys kept, not hidden while they come again); else they close. A replaced link, a goodbye or quitting closes them at once. The copy's pages go `reconnecting` then `off` on the same 6 s, at once on a goodbye or when the handler says the app isn't running.
- **Started on demand (Tirth, 2026-11-05).** The extension handler starts Margin.app when a `wantLink` finds it not running (`NSWorkspace.openApplication`, `activates` false, not added to recent items, at most once per 10 s per handler process) and answers `launching`; the copy asks every 0.5 s for 10 s, and at once on `appUp`. Tested off screen with a sandboxed stand-in for the handler (ad-hoc signed, App Sandbox on) opening a stand-in app nested as Margin.app nests its extension: it's allowed; two opens 50 ms apart gave the same process (no double launch); the app was never active or frontmost and showed no window; 165 ms from the call to the app's `didFinishLaunching`. But **a sandboxed caller's `arguments` never reach the app**, so `--background` can't say "no set-up window": the handler leaves a note in its own container (`Library/Caches/margin-launch`), which the app (not sandboxed) reads and removes at launch (`AppDelegate.startedForLink`, within 15 s). Tested the same way. Opening with a URL would need Margin.app to claim a scheme (sandboxed callers get `kLSAppDoesNotClaimTypeErr`). Not done: quitting by itself 10 minutes after the last copy goes (the app shell's, left for step 3's merge); `LSUIElement`, so no Dock icon shows for a moment as it starts (the generated Info.plist: `scripts/make-xcode.sh`).
- **Protocol changes to the app** (Swift, small): `keys` (`LinkMessage.swift`), the sidebar's command, rebinding (`Shortcuts.swift`), the reconnect grace (`Shell.swift`, which also keeps a relinked copy's overlays until its state comes), the overlay page's `palette`, `switch-done`/`switch-failed`, `keyHeard` and the reconnecting note (`app/overlay/`). The handler answers `ping` quietly.
- **Tests:** unit 989 (from 975: `tests/link.test.js` 11, against `tests/fake-app.js`, a WebSocket server done by hand that speaks the app's end; `kept-tabs.test.js` 3 for the store's refill, merge and told changes). `npm run test:theme` 58. `npm run test:app` 170 unit (from 156) and 59 end-to-end (from 51): the copy's keys held, the sidebar's key, a drop dimmed and closed after the grace, a drop mended within it keeping the same overlay. E2E (Playwright) not run.
- **WebKit host, end to end:** `app-link` runs the real extension against the app's real link code (`tools/webkit-host/link-server/`, app/shell's ExtensionLink, LinkSocket, LinkMessage, Chunks, built with the app's flags), through native messaging the host now implements: linked about 0.65 s after install (wantLink to `hello` 30 ms, the snapshot 5 ms later), the theme's pieces add up, the page's box follows the app's `setLayout`, a `req` runs, then a goodbye, a relink and a kill all behave as above. `app-linked` and `bg-page` too. Full run: 70 of 92 pass (the baseline after step 1: 67 of 89). The three new ones pass; `space-press` passed this time; the 21 failing on the baseline still fail (all from before Margin 2's switching: Phase 3 deletes them); `prepared` failed once (its check that a switch prepared from the page's state found the last write under way), then passed 6 of 7 runs on this build and 4 of 4 on the starting one: likely the copy's one extra write at its start (`dataMeta:link`) landing beside the state's; watch it.

**Open, for Tirth:**
- Safari wiping Margin's storage gives the profile a new id, so the app keeps the old profile's data set as another profile's; it isn't lost, but nothing joins the two. A way to tell it's the same profile (the app could match by the set's content, or ask) is a choice.
- For real Safari (TESTING.md has the list): the app started from the extension handler inside Safari (the sandboxed appex, not a stand-in), with no window and no focus change; ⌘T going to the app's hot key and never to Safari's New Tab or the extension's command; pages fitting the slot with Safari's toolbar shown and its sidebar open (the viewport offsets are inferred); the background page staying alive for hours with the port's pings; the new-tab page's launcher and Settings drawn by the overlay.

## Margin 2, Phase 2 step 3, after the merge (2026-11-05)

- **Safari's own profile id.** The extension handler now sends, in every reply, the id Safari gives the profile its copy runs in (`SFExtensionProfileKey`, `s-<uuid>`, `s-default` for the default profile). The link uses it instead of the id the copy made for itself, so when Safari wipes the extension's storage the copy finds its profile's data in the app again rather than starting a new profile there. A copy that had its own id before takes Safari's with nothing acked and no `rev`, so the app asks for or sends the whole set. Nothing has shipped with the old ids.
- **No Dock icon at launch.** `make-xcode.sh` gives the app `LSUIElement`, so Margin.app started by the extension handler never flashes a Dock icon. Opened by the person, `AppDelegate` gives it its Dock icon and brings the set-up window forward.
- **Quitting when idle.** With no copy linked and no set-up window for 10 minutes, Margin.app quits by itself; a browser starting again starts it again (`AppDelegate.checkIdle`).
- **Keys held down repeat (Tirth, 2026-11-05).** A hot key fires once per press, so a held ⌥⌘↓ moved one tab and stopped. The app now repeats the tab and space keys while held, at the Mac's own key repeat delay and rate (`NSEvent.keyRepeatDelay`, `keyRepeatInterval`), until the hot key's release; it also stops if the combination's modifiers are no longer down (a missed release) or Margin's keys are let go (another app in front). The palette and sidebar keys don't repeat. (Kept in "Margin 2: Safari reliability", 2026-11-06: repeats go through the copy one switch at a time, the newest waiting, and a held space key never starts a switch to another profile's space.)
- **Peek from handed-in positions.** The app hands the pointer's position to the overlay's page wherever it is over the overlay, not only over the page slot, and peek's `pagePointer` applies its pointermove rules there too (the edge brings the sidebar out, over the sidebar keeps it, over the page hides it). So a hidden sidebar comes out at the edge whether or not the overlay's web view hears the pointer itself.
- **One log for the link (2026-11-05).** The extension's link events (asking for the link, the app starting, no answer, a link that didn't open, linked, dropped, goodbye, a socket given up on) go, besides the background's console, through the lasting native port to the extension handler, which writes them to the system log next to Margin.app's own (subsystem `io.tirth.margin`; the app's are category `shell`, the extension's `extension`, each line with its profile). The same line isn't sent again within a minute. `npm run log:app` (`scripts/margin-log.sh [minutes] [--stream]`) shows both; nothing needs Safari's Web Inspector to see why a link failed.


## Margin 2, Phase 2 step 4: spaces across profiles (2026-11-05)

On `feat/margin-app` (from 8b7fb26). Tirth's decisions of 2026-11-05: each space belongs to one profile, a profile has any number of spaces, a new space is made in the profile you're in, switching within a profile is today's switch, and switching to another profile's space creates that profile's full-screen window and then closes the old one (one browser window at a time, macOS's slide accepted, no private calls). What `PLAN.md` §15, `DATA.md` "Spaces across profiles" and `app/PROTOCOL.md` don't already say:

- **The data.** A space's profile is the profile whose set has it: nothing was added to the sets, and the copies keep working alone (the in-page host). The order across profiles and the profiles' names live in one browser-level file in the app (`data/<browser>/browser.json`, `BrowserProfiles.swift`), not in any one set: it spans them, and no copy needs it (the app sends each overlay `across`). The order is read against the sets each time by the slots rule, so a profile reordered or given a space while the app wasn't there still lands sensibly, and a move that changes a profile's own order is written back to it. Considered and not done: an `order` collection in each set (every set would need every other profile's ids), and the app's order only (a copy alone would show another order).
- **Names.** "Default" for Safari's default profile, "Profile N" for the others by when Margin first met them, a color each; the person names a profile once (the space editor's part, `editor.isProfile`). Safari doesn't give names without permissions (2026-11-04 research).
- **The view model and the themes.** `spaces[]` is every profile's spaces in the one order; this window's own come from its own state (always current), the others' from `across`, as chips whose ids are `x:` and a hash (two profiles' space ids can be the same; an action naming one is routed to the app, never the copy). New chip fields `profile`, `foreign`, `profileMark`, `profileStart`, `switching`, `label`; top-level `profiles`, `profile`, `multiProfile`. Arcadia: a badge with the profile's initial on another profile's chips and a thin divider between profiles; Neo-brutalism: a tag and a dashed edge, and a gap. Nothing depends on hover (the overlay's web view may not hear the pointer move, 8b7fb26). One profile: nothing changes.
- **Actions** (new): `space.move` ({space, dir}: the app's order with several profiles, the copy's own order with one), `space.newIn` (profile), `profile.edit`, `profile.forget` (menus only, with "Really delete?"). Menus: another profile's space offers Switch, Move, Edit Profile, New Space in it, Forget (only when it isn't open); "+" offers New Space in each other profile; the palette lists other profiles' spaces. Renaming another profile's space goes through its copy if it's linked, else straight into its set; deleting one needs its copy (its tabs and pins move to its first space: the model's work), so the app says so if it isn't open. A profile's last space can't be deleted. A profile not seen for 30 days is marked in its menu, never removed by Margin.
- **The switch** (`ProfileSwitch.swift`, driven by the app, which knows every link): create (`openSpaceWindow`, 8 s), wait until the new window is full screen and the browser's first window on screen (6 s; a normal window asked once for full screen after 0.4 s), close the old (`closeWindow`), then the new one must still be in front 0.5 s later (else focused again, twice, 1.2 s apart). "In front" is the window server's public list (`CGWindowListCopyWindowInfo`), not the active Space (that needs SkyLight). Failure cases, each in a unit test with a fake world and clock: refused, timed out (and its late window closed), never in front (new window closed, old one focused), the new or the old window closed by the person, a switch asked mid-switch (queued; the last asked wins; the same profile becomes a `setSpace` in the new window), Safari quitting (ended, nothing asked), the target's copy not running (the person asked to open one of its windows; 90 s), back to the start while waiting, the desktop after the close. Keys: ⌃⌘→/← go along the one order: within a profile the copy's own `navigate` (held keys as before) or `setSpace` when the order skips, across the switch.
- **The copy's side** (`handlers.js`): `openSpaceWindow` makes the window (`state: 'fullscreen'`, again without it if Safari refuses), its first tab linked to the space's last used kept tab, the others kept with it from the window the switch closed opening behind it 1.2 s later, 150 ms apart (`model.js spaceWindowTabs`); `closeWindow` is a plain `windows.remove` (tabs kept: step 1's rule for a window closing); `focusWindow`, `orderSpaces`.
- **Safari: does a profile's copy keep running with no window? (item 3)** Not something the WebKit host can show (its controller is ours, not Safari's policy). Read off screen in Safari 27.2's code instead (the system's shared cache, `Safari.framework`, its selectors and who calls them; nothing run, nothing in Safari touched): Safari keeps an extensions controller per profile (`_profileServerIDToWebExtensionsControllers`); at launch `-[AppController awakeFromNib]` loads only the default profile's extensions (`_loadExtensionsInProfile:` with `defaultProfile`) and only creates the named profiles' controllers; a named profile's extensions load in `loadExtensionsInProfileIfNecessary:`, called when a browser window of it opens (`-[BrowserDocument _commonBrowserDocumentInit…]`) or its Settings show; the controllers are deleted only in `tabGroupManager:didRemoveProfileWithIdentifier:` (the profile deleted). Nothing unloads them when the last window closes. Windows an extension creates go through `createHiddenBrowserDocumentForWebExtensionsWithBrowsingMode:profileIdentifier:…`: the extension's own profile. **So:** the default profile's copy is always running; a named profile's copy runs from its first window of the session until Safari quits, window or not (its background page kept alive by the port pings, Phase 0); before its first window, nothing of Margin's runs in it and nothing Margin may call can open one. TESTING.md has the two checks that confirm this in Safari, and what each outcome means.
- **Opening a window in a profile whose copy isn't running.** No public API (no profile parameter in `SFSafariApplication openWindowWithURL:` or AppleScript; the extension can only make windows in its own profile). The routes that exist all need the person: File › New Window › New "Work" Window, the Dock menu's "New Work Window", a macOS Focus with Safari's profile filter (Safari opens that profile's window when the Focus turns on: `showProfileForFocusModeWithProfileIdentifier:`), or Settings › Websites › "Open Links With Profile" for a site. Built: the app asks the person, waits, and finishes the switch when that profile's window appears. Not built (Tirth's choice if wanted): a Focus per profile switched by a Shortcut Margin runs (`/usr/bin/shortcuts run`), which would open the window without the person, at the cost of setting up a Focus and a Shortcut per profile.
- **Tests.** Unit (`npm test`) 1001 (11 new, `tests/profiles-across.test.js`: `across` checked, chip ids, the order with live spaces, the view model with one and several profiles, the profile editor, actions and their mapping, menus, the palette, the app host's routing, the copy's `spaceWindowTabs` and `orderSpaces`; two tests updated for the new actions). `npm run test:theme` 60. `npm run test:app`: 333 unit checks (57 new, `profiles-tests.swift`: the slots rule, names, the file read back, moves, wrapping, forgetting, `across`, and the switch's state machine in every case above), 119 end to end (20 new, `e2e-profiles.swift`: two profiles' copies played by `fake-extension.mjs`, the overlay drawing both profiles' spaces with marks and the divider, a press on the other profile's chip driving the whole switch with stand-in windows, the old window closed only after the new one is in front, one window left with its overlay, ⌃⌘← within a profile as `navigate`, ⌃⌘→ across back, a rename and a move reaching the overlay and `browser.json`). E2E (Playwright) not run.
- **WebKit host:** 71 of 92 pass, the same scenarios as the baseline after step 3 (no new failure, none fixed). It can't show anything of this step: its one window and controller are ours, not Safari's profiles.

**Open, for Tirth:**
- The two Safari checks in TESTING.md that confirm a named profile's copy keeps running with no window, and doesn't run before its first window of a Safari session. If the second holds (expected), the first switch to a named profile after each Safari launch needs the person to open one of its windows. Worth a Focus-per-profile Shortcut to do that for them? (Setup per profile; not built.)
- Defaults chosen here, to confirm or change: "Default" / "Profile N" names; another profile's spaces marked, this one's not; a divider between profiles; Move Left/Right in the space menu only with several profiles; a profile forgotten only by hand, never after a time; 90 s waiting for the person to open a profile's window.
- Chrome and Edge (Phase 5): their copies need the `background` permission to stay linked with no window (PLAN.md §15); not added (the link is Safari's only for now).
- Dropping a tab on another profile's space does nothing (a tab can't move between profiles); the drop isn't refused visibly yet.

## Margin 2, Phase 2 step 6: which window is which (2026-11-05)

Tirth's log (14:32:20, two profiles, one full-screen window each, same bounds): the two windows' matches swapped for about 650 ms during a Space switch, so each drew the other profile's sidebar. The matcher picked by cost each read, with focus (each copy's `focused` flag) as the strongest tie-breaker; the copies' flags lag the window server's front window and disagree with each other while a Space changes, so focus forced a swap. It also matched same-bounds windows with nothing to go by ("one each, the same every time"), a guess. Replaced by lasting bindings (`app/shell/WindowMatcher.swift` `WindowBinder`, one per browser):

- **A binding holds** while both windows exist and their copy is linked (or dropped a moment ago, or linked again without its state yet). It goes when the extension's window closes (that window server window is then never bound again, so a new window can't take a closing one), the window server's window goes (Safari quitting), the copy goes, or a new copy of Margin starts in that profile (another `instanceId`: after a Margin update its window ids may be new). It never changes on a focus flag.
- **It moves only on strong evidence:** two separate focus changes (below) both saying the window is another one (one alone is logged and kept), or its bounds matching one other free window alone, both still, for 2 s while its own window's don't (a wrong binding mended; a full-screen transition or Mission Control moving windows doesn't do it).
- **Learned only between windows not bound yet, from evidence:**
  - *made by Margin*: the app's own `openSpaceWindow` (the profile switch, step 4) answered with the window's id, and the one new window the window server lists from about when it was asked, once their bounds agree (the overlay's page is loaded while the window goes full screen, so it shows as soon as the window's in front);
  - *created together*: a copy's `windows.onCreated` time (`createdAt`) and a window first listed then, each alone within 1 s;
  - *focus moved*: a copy's `windows.onFocusChanged` time (`focusedAt`) and the browser's front on-screen window changing within 0.5 s of it (beyond the reads either side), with no other focus change or front window change within 1 s, and not contradicted by that copy calling another of its windows focused in the same report; weighed 2 s after (a busy background's report may come late);
  - *the only free window with its bounds*, on both sides (so also by elimination once the others are bound); another profile's window with the same bounds and no linked copy keeps it unbound;
  - *settled focus*: the only window the copies call focused, the newest focus heard, the same front window for 5 s and the flags for 1 s, and as many of the browser's windows with those bounds as the copies report (for a relaunch with windows already open, and focus flags that change with no event heard).
- **Can't tell yet:** no binding, so no overlay is shown over that window; the log says so once per reason ("3 windows have its bounds (…): waiting for a focus change or a creation").
- **The log tells the story:** each binding made, moved or dropped is one line with its rule and inputs (times to the millisecond, window numbers, bounds), e.g. `safari:B#1169 is window 12881: focus moved to it at 14:32:20.512 and window 12881 came to the front by 14:32:20.540, each alone then`. Reads that change nothing log nothing.
- **The extension** reports `focusedAt` and `createdAt` per window (`background/link.js`, from `background.js`'s `windows.onFocusChanged` and `onCreated`; `WINDOW_ID_NONE` ignored), only when heard, not kept past the background (a restarted copy is a new copy anyway).
- **Timing it costs:** a window with bounds of its own is bound at the first read, as before. Same-bounds windows: the one a switch made, as soon as its bounds agree; one that came to the front, about 2 s after the switch; after a relaunch with several same-bounds full-screen windows, about 6 s for the front one if only it is called focused (else at the next Space switch), then the rest by elimination as each shows.
- **Not used:** window titles (Screen Recording), Accessibility, private calls. Nothing the extension can see says which Space a window is on.
- **Tests:** `npm run test:app`: 384 unit checks (with `tools/app-tests/window-tests.swift`'s, replacing the old matcher's: every case above and in TESTING.md, the logged swap replayed with the flags lagging 650 ms over six switches, the profile switch both ways with the old window lingering or not, and 300 seeded runs of 12 Space switches among three same-bounds windows of two profiles, from bound or from nothing, the copies' flags as each profile's last focused or only the key window, their reports lagging up to 1.2 s, the browser leaving the front: never bound wrong at any read, all bound once each window was shown cleanly; 5000 runs and lags up to 2.5 s also clean; at 4 s one run in 2000 paired a focus with the wrong front change, mended by the next two), 133 end-to-end (`e2e-windows.swift`, 14 new: two profiles, three same-bounds full-screen windows and a normal one, Spaces played by on-screen flags, the copies' reports lagging; an overlay checked every 20 ms never over another window). `npm test` 1001.
- **To check in Safari:** TESTING.md "Margin 2 Phase 2 step 6". The open question is what Safari's `focused` flags and `onFocusChanged` do across profiles: if a profile's copy hears no focus event when its window comes forward from another profile's, same-bounds windows are learned from settled focus (5 s) and elimination instead, and the log shows which. (Closed 2026-11-06, "Margin 2: Safari reliability" › "Telling same-bounds full-screen windows of different profiles apart": settled focus never fired across profiles in Tirth's runs, and the flags are no longer used across copies. Pages saying they're shown take their place.)

## Margin 2, Phase 2 step 5: only Margin.app draws Margin (2026-11-05)

From `feat/margin-app` 7b47bf0, rebased onto 671294e (steps 4 and 6). Tirth (2026-11-05): the in-page UI goes now rather than in Phase 7, so what's tested in Safari is exactly the app-drawn UI and any gaps show up now. Pages never draw any of Margin's UI, linked to Margin.app or not.

- **What pages run** (`content/core.js`, from 1,684 lines to 793): the box, the trackpad's gestures and Margin's pinch, the draft check, the navigation keys (`shared/shortcuts.js`, as before), ⌘T, the page's icon and zoom, keep-ready's holds and the warm-up's idle check (Phase 3's), the copies' checks, and the background's switch handshakes. No UI runtime, theme loader or in-page host, no shadow host, no `<margin-app>`: in no state is there an element of Margin's in a page. The content bundle went from 273,690 to 55,035 bytes (at 671294e), the new-tab page's from 206,426 to 8,896; the extension's sources lost about 2,200 lines and gained about 500; neither has `ui/runtime.js`, `ui/host.js`, `ui/theme-loader.js` or `ui/sanitize.js` in it (`tests/build.test.js` checks). `ui/host.js` stays for the browser tests (`tests/theme/`, `tests/ui-host.test.js`).
- **The box.** Linked (and reconnecting), the app's layout for the page's window, as in step 3a (`content/layout.js`, now only that and the first-paint placeholder's hand-over). Not linked: none, the page as it is (`margin:box` off, the placeholder switched off on the page). The first-paint registrations follow the link too (`background/registered.js`): the placeholder CSS and the page's script are registered only while the stored link state draws (`appDraws`: linked or reconnecting), dropped when it goes off, registered again when it comes back (`background.js` reruns them when `appLink` turns, not on every layout). A background starting keeps the last state as reconnecting while it tries its kept link (step 3a), so a session that ended linked keeps its first-paint box until the link is found gone. A page that puts in a new `<html>` gets the placeholder switched off on that one too (a mutation observer: before, the in-page runtime's redraw did it, and `read-shape` failed without it).
- **WebKit keeps dropped registrations in web views made after them** (seen in the WebKit host, `link-drop`, and before this step for the frame's setting): `scripting.unregisterContentScripts` succeeds and `getRegisteredContentScripts` is empty, but a tab opened later, its reloads, and other sites' tabs still get the placeholder CSS and the page's script, until the host restarts; tabs open before the registration are clean. Margin's core takes both off once it runs (`data-margin-docked="off"`, the box off), so such a page is plain, but may paint a box or frame first. Whether Safari does the same is for Tirth's check (TESTING.md); if it does, a first paint with nothing registered needs another way (the page's script reading the link state itself, say), which is Phase 3's new first-paint path anyway.
- **The new-tab page** (`newtab/`): linked, as before (draws nothing; the app's overlay draws the launcher and Settings in its slot). Not linked: "Margin.app isn't running", one line on why, a "Start Margin.app" button and an address field (focused; an address opens in that tab, other words are searched with Settings' engine through the background's `search`). The button asks the link now (`startApp` → `link.js askNow`: wantLink from the start of the waits, so the extension handler starts the app if it isn't running); the page reloads when the link comes, and the app draws it from then on. Chrome has no link (Phase 5): no button there, and the line says the app works with Safari for now. Phase 7 makes this the install prompt.
- **⌘T and the palette's key not linked:** no palette to open, so Margin's new-tab page, with its notice and field: Safari's `new-tab` and `open-palette` commands, and ⌘T heard in a page (`newTabKey`), taken once per window per press (both can come for one press); on that page already, its field takes the focus. The sidebar's key does nothing (⌥S then reaches the page, as without Margin). A new tab Safari made (its New Tab) stays: with no app there's no palette to hand it to (`handlers.js handOff`). Linked, as in step 3a.
- **What pages drew, and where it is now.** Drawn by the app's overlay while linked (the same runtime and theme): the sidebar, the palette, menus, the drag copy, renaming, the launcher and Settings (on the new-tab page's slot), the peek at the edge. Notices and prompts: the app's runtime has its `notice` and `prompt` parts, but the notices pages put up came from the page's own code, and none of those has an app path yet (the gaps below). Gone with nothing in their place, by decision: the switching, arrival, loading and startup covers (Safari's own blink is accepted), the carried-open sidebar (the app's sidebar is the window's), the scroll handed from page to page (the app has one list per window).
- **Gaps (in pages only before, no app path yet; struck: closed in step 7, below):** ~~(1) "Margin isn't responding on this page… Reload" (a page whose copy of Margin is gone; the reload itself still happens for pages behind: `background/cut-off.js`)~~; ~~(2) a switch that didn't happen: "That tab is no longer open.", "Margin didn't answer, so the tab didn't switch.", "Margin couldn't show that tab." (the app's overlay drops the highlight on `switch-failed`, with no word)~~; ~~(3) "Margin was updated while Safari was open. Quit and reopen Safari…" (a copy that stood back for a newer one)~~; ~~(4) "Margin was updated. Reload this page to fit it beside the sidebar again." (pages an old fitted build laid out)~~ (deleted: it can't happen now); ~~(5) the ⌘T hint: "If ⌘T opened this page: choose Margin in Safari Settings → General…" (`background/newtab-hint.js` asks a page, which says no now; the set-up window checks the same setting)~~; ~~(6) the page-load pill as a page is left (`beforeunload`) and for a site's own same-document navigations (the app's sidebar shows a tab loading from its status only)~~ (it showed nothing at all: the stored tabs have no status); (7) Chrome: nothing at all until Phase 5 (no link there, so no sidebar, palette or Settings; its "works in full screen" prompt is gone too); (8) the warm-up note and the startup cover (Phase 3 deletes their machinery). Settings needs the app: not linked, the new-tab page's button starts it, so nothing leaves Tirth stuck in Safari.
- **Kept for Phase 3, answering as linked pages did:** `prepareForShow` reads the state for the tab and window it's shown as, then answers `{ ready: false, reason: 'nothing drawn in pages' }` (what every page answered while the app drew: 'theme not ready'); `coverArrival` answers false; a `switching` message is answered not covered; `inView`, the visibility times and the copies' checks as before. The page-side drawing of the covers is gone with the UI; the background's side of the switching stays for Phase 3. Pages now take state changes from the change events once they have the state (linked pages before read storage again on every change, as the in-page runtime's first render never came).
- **Deleted:** `content/palette.js`, `content/loader.js` (the page-load pill), `content/ready.js` (the startup cover) and its test, `shared/no-answer.js` and its tests; the shadow-host helpers in `shared/dom.js` (`createHost`, `mountHost`, page zoom and pinch counter-scaling of hosts, `asBrowserUi`, `el`, `adoptStyles`, `siteModalOpen`); the handlers `themePackage` and `newTabKeyUsed`; the page's `show-palette`, `hide-palette`, `newtab-hint`, `command` (toggle, peek, carry-open, open-palette) and `warm-up` note handling; the in-page sidebar's key (⌥S in pages), reveal edge and pointer pass-through. The stored theme package (`uiTheme`) stays: its change event sends it to the app.
- **Tests.** Unit: 994 on the merged tree (from 990 at 7b47bf0, before steps 4 and 6 came in: the in-page ⌘T key, the readiness cover and the no-answer notice's tests went; new: `tests/no-ui-in-pages.test.js` 3, the registrations following the link, ⌘T and the palette's key opening the new-tab page once a press, a new tab kept with no app; `link.test.js` 1 for `askNow`; the layout's tests rewritten for the app's layout). `npm run test:theme` 60. `npm run test:app` 384 unit and 133 end-to-end. E2E (Playwright) not run: every file in `tests/e2e/` tests the in-page UI and needs rewriting (archive, enhancements, favicons, loader, navigation, newtab-palette, newtab, palette, platform, resize, scroll, search, sidebar, smoke, spaces; and `helpers.js`).
- **WebKit host.** The box scenarios run linked to Margin.app's real link code (`link-server/`), whose layout a scenario now sets: `appStart` takes the page slot's insets, `appLayout` sends new ones (the overlay measuring again), and `appOnDemand` has the host start the link server at the next wantLink and answer `launching`, as the extension handler does. New: `no-ui`, `link-drop`, `newtab-off`, `newtab-start`. Full run on the merged tree: 61 of 61 pass (the baseline: 71 of 92, the 21 failing all Phase 3's). Converted to run linked (`appStart` with the layout they need, `appLayout` where Keep open or the width changed): `box`, `hidden-box`, `toggle`, `csp`, `lock`, `body-size`, `late-body`, `late-reads`, `read-limits`, `read-shape`, `in-view`, `answers`, `keys`, `history`, `top-layer`, `linked-sheet`, `zoom` (the box at the zoomed width; Margin's own scaling went with the hosts), `pinch-box`, `update-old-layout`, `screenshot`, `reloaded`; kept with "Margin's core runs here" in place of "the sidebar is drawn", and rows' checks turned into the state's or the request a row sends: `switching`, `stalled`, `update`, `tabids`, `owner`, `relaunch`, `bfcache`, `pinch`, `titles`, `addresses`, `twins`, `deferred`, `behindleft`, `wake-wait` (its preparation now 'nothing drawn in pages'), the sleep and Margin's-tabs scenarios, `app-linked`, `app-link`, `reloaded-newtab`. Retired: what tested only the in-page UI (`newtab`, `spaces`, `closed-frame`, `press`, `dragtab`, `recorder`, `space-press`, `space-click`, `wake-reveal`, `wake-space`; `clean-tags` replaced by `no-ui`), the 19 of Phase 3's switching scenarios that failed before (`unseen`, `unseennav`, `restartrepair`, `unloaded`, `unloadedheavy`, `unloadedtwo`, `unloadedspace`, `unloadedlate`, `loadcover`, `slowunloaded`, `behind`, `behindlate`, `behindbox`, `behindfocus`, `behindkeys`, `shelllate`, `shellcover`, `arrival`, `sleep-switch`), and what a page drew or did for a prepared show (`prepared`, `catchup`, `catchup-slow`, `catchup-late`, `catchup-loaded`: the catch-up in a preparation was already unreachable while linked). Phase 3's that still pass are kept until Phase 3 deletes their code (`introduce`, `heavyflip`, `shellbehind`, `navigated`, `wake-cap`, `wake-awake`, `keep-ready-cap`, `keep-ready-away`). `commandkeys` failed once in the first full run (⌃⌘↓ from a text field) and passed on its own and in the merged run.

## Margin 2, Phase 2 step 7: what pages said, said by Margin.app (2026-11-05)

From `feat/margin-app` 61add4d; commits 2c66744 (the first read) and 54ff68c. Step 5 left a list of what pages said or drew in their own UI with no app path yet ("Gaps", above, struck where closed). Each has one now: the copy decides, the window's overlay draws it through the theme's parts, and an action goes back to the copy as a `req`. `app/PROTOCOL.md` "Notices, loading and gestures" has the messages; `extension/themes/README.md` the view model's.

- **One way for notices.** The copy pushes `notice { kind, tabId? }` for a window (`background/notices.js`): a kind, never words. The overlay has Margin's words for each kind (`extension/shared/notices.js`, the only place they're written) and draws them in the theme's guarded notice part (`ui/app-feedback.js createAppNotices`, through the runtime's `notice`); an unknown kind draws nothing, so a link can't make Margin say anything new. One notice at a time, the newest in place of the one before; one about a tab's page goes once that tab has been in front and isn't, or after its time. A button comes back through the runtime's `notice.run` and the host's `perform('notice')` as `req noticeAction { notice, action, tabId }` (`handlers.js`, in `APP_REQUESTS`); the copy does what it means and never says that notice again for that page. Said once per page (again 10 minutes after one that went by itself). Decided while the link is down, a notice waits up to a minute and is said once linked, if its tab is still in front with the same page; the app keeps a window's last notice for a minute and its last loading set, and gives them to an overlay made later with its hello (`app/shell/Shell.swift HeldPushes`): a push reaches only overlays already there, and the copy that finds a page cut off is usually one just started, before its window's overlay is.
- **(1) "Margin isn't responding on this page".** The page can't say it any more (its script is the copy that's gone), but the copy now running finds such a page already (`background/cut-off.js`, `tabs.js repairCutOff`, when a tab comes to the front or a switch goes to it): a page in front, or about to be, whose world is still bound to a gone copy gets the notice, with Reload and Not now. Reload is `tabs.js reloadCutOff`: a GET of its address, as Margin loads such a page behind. A page that isn't in use was already loaded again by Margin once it's behind; one in use (a draft, media) only by the person, now from the notice.
- **(2) A switch that didn't happen.** The copy's `switch-failed` push carries its `reason` now (`tabs.js tellPage`), and a switch the overlay asked for itself remembers its request's error (`host-app.js takeFailure`, called from the runtime's `onSwitchFailed`). Either becomes "That tab is no longer open.", "Margin didn't answer, so the tab didn't switch. Try again." or "Margin couldn't show that tab. Try again." for 4 s (`shared/notices.js switchFailure`, the words pages used). None with no reason (as pages had it), for the link going down (the overlay says it's reconnecting), or for a copy that stood back.
- **(3) "Margin was updated while Safari was open".** The copy in charge sees a page in front still bound to an older copy Safari keeps running (`cutOffInPage` answers `otherCopy`): that page's Margin stood back, and loading it again binds it to the same copy, so the notice, with OK, says only quitting Safari finishes it. Not checked in the WebKit host: it can't run two copies with the link.
- **(4) "Reload this page to fit it beside the sidebar again": deleted, it can't happen.** Only builds before 9ad5c68 (2026-11-01: the fitted layout and the frameless test build) mark a page `data-margin-shell` or `data-margin-frameless`; no build since does, none of those shipped, a Safari relaunch reloads every page, and a page still laid out that way is left alone (`page-link.js`, the `update-old-layout` scenario) and loaded again by `content/takeover.js` once it's hidden and nothing in it would be lost. The box follows only the app's layout now, so there's no new way for a page to be laid out wrong that a reload would mend.
- **(5) Safari's New Tab setting.** `newtab-hint.js` asks the page only whether it was opened from nothing (`content/core.js` answers `{ fromNothing }`, drawing nothing), and the copy says `new-tab-setting` in its window, with OK and Don't show again (which turns `settings.newTabHint` off). The words follow the set-up window's check: "In Safari Settings › General, set "New tabs open with" to Margin." With the app, ⌘T is the app's hot key, so the hint is about Safari's own New Tab (the tab bar's +, File › New Tab); the setting's label says "a new tab" for "⌘T". The app's Settings now show the settings for the build's features (sleeping tabs, the trackpad gestures, this note): its host had none, so they were missing there since step 3a (`host-app.js` platform `features`, Safari's profile stamped into the overlay).
- **(6) Loading.** The app's sidebar showed no tab loading at all: the stored tabs have no status (only pages' own pill did, before step 5). Now `background/page-loads.js` keeps which tabs are loading, from three signs: the tab's status (`tabs.onUpdated` loading, until complete), the page being left (`content/loads.js`: its `beforeunload`, said at once, taken back if it stays: a "Leave page?" prompt, a download, input there), and a site's own same-page navigation through the Navigation API taking over 150 ms; each at most 10–15 s. Not stored (every write reaches every page): pushed to the window's overlay as `loading { tabIds }` 16 ms after it changes, and for every window when the link comes. The overlay's host marks those tabs `loading`; the view model already had `loading` on rows and on the tab in front. Both themes draw it without hover, with the sidebar hidden too: Arcadia its pill in the frame's top strip (it had it) and a pulse on a loading row's icon; Neo-brutalism the reload button's spin (in the sidebar, as before), a striped bar marching along the gutter above the page, and a blink on a loading row's icon box.
- **(7) The swipe and pull-to-refresh badges.** The gestures still run in pages (`content/trackpad.js`, unchanged); their feedback goes to the overlay: the page tells each change of state at once and the progress in steps of 0.05 at most every 30 ms (`gestureTeller`, nothing while idle), through the copy (`gesture`, a page request) as `push gesture`. The view model has `gesture` (`pull` or `swipe`, `back`/`forward`, `armed`, `done`, `progress`); both themes draw an icon badge over the page (a round dark one in Arcadia, a bordered square in Neo-brutalism) that comes in with the fingers and takes the space's color (yellow) once letting go would do it, `pointer-events: none` (so never a hit box: the page keeps the pointer). The last word shows 450 ms; one with no word for 1 s goes. It lags the fingers by a trip page → background → link → overlay: not measured on a trackpad (the WebKit host plays synthetic wheel events), for Tirth's check.
- **(8) Pages' first read.** It was `storage.local.get(null)`, so it took the stored theme package (`uiTheme`) that only the link uses. Now it reads the page's keys, the site icons and the link state (`shared/stored.js firstReadKeys`; the old names of the open tabs only when `openTabs` isn't there). `uiTheme` stays in storage for the link. Measured in the WebKit host (`first-read`, median of 7, in a page's Margin world): with Arcadia's package stored (59,959 bytes), all of storage 5 ms and 61,614 bytes against the first read now 0 ms (under the timer's 1 ms) and 1,564 bytes; with a 3 MB package stored, 32 ms and 3,001,764 bytes against 1 ms and 1,564 bytes. (The host's tabs have few site icons; in Safari the icons are most of what's left.) A key a page never saw isn't found by its first read any more; pages use none outside the list.
- **Choices made conservatively (questions for Tirth below):** "Not now" on the not-responding notice never asks again for that page; the switch failures are notices that go by themselves with no button; the New Tab note's OK counts as seen (once a Safari run, as before).
- **Tests.** Unit: 1010 (from 994 at 61add4d: `tests/app-feedback.test.js` 14, the copy deciding each notice against the real background, its buttons' `noticeAction`, loading from each sign, the overlay's notices and gestures, the app host's loading and failures, the gesture's view model, the page's tellers; the homepage note's tests in `wake.test.js` rewritten for the overlay, one new; page reads' rewritten for the first read, one new). `npm run test:theme` 76 (from 60; `tests/theme/feedback.test.js` 16: loading, gestures and notices in both themes and both engines, with the sidebar hidden). `npm run test:app` 389 unit (5 for `HeldPushes`) and 141 end-to-end (8 new: a pushed notice drawn in Arcadia's part with its words, its Reload pressed becoming `noticeAction` for the tab and window, an unknown kind drawing nothing, a switch failure's words, loading on a row and the pill, the pull badge, armed and gone).
- **WebKit host:** 66 of 66 (the 61 and five new: `notice-cutoff`, `notice-newtab`, `loading`, `gesture-pull`, `first-read`), through the app's real end of the link: the host now keeps what the link server heard (`appMark`, `appHeard`) and can send a request from the app's UI (`appReq`; the link server takes it on stdin), and `open` can make a tab selected before the extension hears of it (`selectedFirst`, as Safari's New Tab). No overlays there: the overlay drawing is `npm run test:app`'s and the theme tests'.

**Open, for Tirth:**
- Should "Not now" on "Margin isn't responding" ask again later (say, the next time that page comes to the front)? Now it never does for that page; Margin loads it again itself once it's behind and nothing would be lost.
- Should a switch that didn't happen offer Try again (re-sending the switch), or stay a plain 4 s notice as it was in pages?
- "Margin was updated while Safari was open" could offer "Quit Safari" (the app could ask Safari to quit). It only says so now.
- For real Safari (TESTING.md "Margin 2 Phase 2 step 7"): the notices drawn over full-screen Safari and their buttons; how the loading indicator follows real loads (whether Safari's status comes early enough that the page's own word adds anything); single-page apps' navigations; how far the gesture badges lag a real trackpad.

## Margin 2, Phase 2 step 9: Safari's own profile names (2026-11-05)

On `feat/margin-app` (from eb79cea). Tirth's decision of 2026-11-05 ("option A"): Margin shows each Safari profile's exact name, kept in step with Safari, read with Full Disk Access (his Margin.app has it). Without the permission Margin keeps the names typed in Edit Profile and offers the permission in its set-up window.

- **Where Safari keeps the names** (the probe in Margin.app, two rounds, read only, then removed): `~/Library/Containers/com.apple.Safari/Data/Library/Safari/SafariTabs.db`, table `bookmarks`, rows `type = 1, subtype = 2, parent = 0`: `title` the name, `external_uuid` (`DefaultProfile` for the default one), `extra_attributes` a property list with `SymbolImageName`. Tab groups are `subtype = 0` folders; `subtype` 3 and 4 are devices.
- **The link from a copy to its profile** (the hard part: the id Safari gives the extension, `SFExtensionProfileKey`, matched none of the probe's UUIDs). Read off screen in Safari 27.2's code (the system's shared cache, extracted to the scratchpad, disassembled; nothing run, nothing of Tirth's read): the only code in `Safari.framework` that loads `SFExtensionProfileKey` is `-[SafariAppExtensionsController _connectToExtension:shareExtensionContext:reason:completionHandler:]`, which puts `[extensionData privacyPreservingProfileIdentifier]` there. That value is set in `SafariSharedUI`'s `-[WBSExtensionsController _updateExtensionStateWithPrivacyPreservingProfileIdentifierIfNeeded:]`: for a named profile, the extension's state dictionary's `ProfileIdentifier` string if there is one, else a new random `NSUUID`, written back into the state. The state is saved by `-[WBSWebExtensionsController _urlForExtensionSettings]` at `safari_profileDirectoryURLWithID:<the controller's profileServerID>` + `WebExtensions/Extensions.plist`, keyed by `"%@ (%@)"` (bundle id, team id or `UNSIGNED`). A profile's server id is its row's `server_id` (`WebBookmarks` uses `server_id = 'DefaultProfile'` for the default one). So: copy `s-<uuid>` ⇐ `ProfileIdentifier` in Margin's entry (`io.tirth.margin.Extension (…)`) of `Profiles/<server_id>/WebExtensions/Extensions.plist` ⇐ the row with that `server_id`. This also explains the probe's folders: the two with `History.db` and `WebExtensions/` are named by the profiles' `server_id`s, the two with only `TopSites.plist` by their `external_uuid`s. The id is random per extension and profile by design ("privacy preserving"), so there's no other way from the id to the profile; the default profile's copy (`s-default`, no key sent) is always `DefaultProfile`.
- **How sure:** the chain is read from Safari's code, not yet seen on Tirth's Mac (the probe didn't log `server_id` or open the plists). The reader logs, at each change, every profile with its `server_id`, symbol and the copy it linked (`safari profiles: …`), so Tirth's first run confirms it: the profile shown with `s-8d454f81-…` must be the one that copy runs in. If a profile's entry can't be read, that profile just keeps Margin's typed name (nothing wrong is shown).
- **The reader** (`app/shell/SafariProfiles.swift`, replacing `SafariProfileProbe.swift`): a read attempt first (`open()` on the database: `EPERM`/`EACCES` is no permission; no private calls). SQLite opened read only (`mode=ro`), with `immutable=1` when there's no `-wal` beside it so nothing is ever made in Safari's folder (macOS's SQLite keeps an empty `-wal` after Safari closes, so this is rare); 200 ms busy timeout; opened, read, closed each time. Only the profile rows' four columns and Margin's own plist entry are read; never a tab, an address or another extension's settings. Busy: the last names stand, again in 2 s. No access, no Safari data, or a table that isn't Safari 27.2's: the typed names, again in 10 s (so access given while the app runs is picked up).
- **Watching:** FSEvents reports a file's change only once the writer closes it, and Safari keeps its database and `-wal` open, so those two are watched with kqueue (`DispatchSource` vnode sources: each write; deleted or replaced, watched again after the next read), and Safari's folder with FSEvents for each profile's `Extensions.plist` (written whole) and profiles' folders. A read at most once a second while they change; the outcome goes on (and is logged) only when it changed.
- **Names in Margin** (`BrowserProfiles.swift`): Safari's name wins over a typed one; it's never written into the typed `name`, so without the permission the typed names come back. `browser.json` keeps each profile's `browserId` (Safari's `server_id`) and `browserName`, so a profile whose plist entry can't be read keeps Safari's name by its id, and a profile Safari no longer has (gone from the database after a good read) keeps the name Safari last gave it as a typed name (unless one was typed), with its spaces and data as for any profile not seen. Edit Profile on a Safari-named profile shows the name in a disabled field with "The name is Safari's: rename the profile in Safari › Settings › Profiles and Margin follows. The emoji and color are Margin's." (`editor.nameLocked`, `labels.note`); the app ignores the name it sends.
- **The symbol:** the app draws the SF Symbol (`NSImage(systemSymbolName:)`, white, 12 pt at 2x) into a PNG `data:` URL once per symbol, sent as the profile's `symbol` in `across`; pages take only a PNG data URL there. Arcadia's profile mark draws it white on the profile's color in place of the initial, Neo-brutalism's tag black. Both of Tirth's named profiles use the same symbol (`person.lanyardcard.fill`), so the color is what tells them apart there, as in Safari.
- **The set-up window:** a "Show Safari's profile names" step (Recommended, not required): what it's for, that Margin reads only names and symbols and never changes Safari's data, a button opening `x-apple.systempreferences:com.apple.preference.security?Privacy_AllFiles`, and whether Margin has it (checked every 2 s with the same read attempt).
- **`npm run safari`** makes the Xcode project again when `app/shell/` has a Swift file more or less (it used to only when `app/safari/` changed), since the project lists each shell file.
- **Chrome and Edge** (Phase 5, not built): `Local State` `profile.info_cache` (PLAN.md Phase 5).
- **Tests.** Unit (`npm test`) 1011 (1 new: `across` checks `nameFrom` and `symbol`, the view model's `fromBrowser`, `symbol`, `nameLocked`). App (`npm run test:app`) 443 unit checks (54 new, `tools/app-tests/safari-profiles-tests.swift`, against a stand-in for Safari's folder made in a temporary folder with Safari 27.2's schema, never Safari's own: the profiles, the default profile, the link through Extensions.plist, another extension's and an unsigned build's entries, a folder name that isn't an id, no write-ahead log (nothing made), a busy database, no permission, no Safari, a table that isn't Safari's, the watcher picking up a rename, Margin turned on in another profile, the permission withdrawn and given back; the names: Safari's over typed, Edit Profile, browser.json, by Safari's id, a rename, no permission, deleted in Safari; the symbol's picture) and 141 end-to-end, no compiler warnings with the Xcode target's settings.

**Open, for Tirth:**
- The TESTING.md checks ("Margin 2 Phase 2 step 9"), first the log line that shows each profile's copy: it confirms the link read from Safari's code.
- Whether macOS applies Full Disk Access given while Margin.app runs without a relaunch (the reader retries every 10 s; the set-up window says to quit and reopen if not).
- The symbol on the profile mark replaces the initial; with two profiles on the same symbol, the initial might be more useful. Keep the symbol, the initial, or both?

## Margin 2, Phase 2 step 8: what Tirth saw in full-screen Safari, fixed (2026-11-05)

From `feat/margin-app` eb79cea. Tirth's report (full-screen Safari, Margin.app linked, one space and 3–4 tabs per profile): the highlight flickers on a switch (new tab, back, new again), the palette sometimes looks frozen as it opens, the overlay sometimes shows on the desktop, and generally slow. Then three more from him while this was under way: the pointer at the left edge also opens Safari's own sidebar behind Margin's, Safari's full-screen toolbar comes down half covered by Margin's sidebar, and the docked Web Inspector is covered by the overlay. Nothing was shown on Tirth's screen: what's measured is from the unit tests, the app's end-to-end run (the real overlay page, off screen) and the WebKit host.
