- **The highlight flicker: measured, two causes.** (1) The overlay dropped the switch's own highlight before the stored state had the new tab in front: on the copy's `switch-done` push at once (a key's switch), or 300 ms after a click's answer (shared/switching.js GRACE_MS), and the state comes later, after Safari's onActivated, an ownership check and a storage write, then the link's delta. Measured against the real background with a fake Safari whose writes take 40 ms: a key's switch said done at +55 ms and the state with the tab in front went out at about +113 ms, so for about 60 ms the overlay drew the tab left; with slower writes a click's grace ran out first too. (2) The old switching itself: a tab not proven shown before was introduced (tabs.update to it and straight back) before the real update: three updates and three activations for one switch, and two to three writes of the open tabs. **Fix:** the overlay keeps a switch's highlight until the state has that tab in front (`createSwitching` `landed`, `switching.settle()` on each state change; ended at once if another tab came to the front or on a failure, and 2 s after the answer at the latest; only for hosts that stay on screen: `host-app.js staysOnScreen`), and the copy's own switches take the same path (`runtime.switchTo` with `backgroundSwitches`). And the plain switch (below). Tests: the highlight changes once, `[1, 3]`, for a key's switch and for a click with 400 ms writes, through the real background (`tests/switch-plain.test.js`), and the cases in `tests/switching.test.js`.
- **The plain switch, brought forward from Phase 3.** `focusTab` is `tabs.update(active)`, the window focused only if it isn't, a tab suspected of a gone copy asked first (150 ms at most), and an update Safari doesn't answer in 2 s taken as made (a hung tab process: Safari carries it out). Navigation keeps its chaining and its `switching`/`switch-done`/`switch-failed` word to the page and the overlay; no wakes ahead, no passing checks. A new tab from the palette still loads behind the page first, now only until Safari says it's loaded or 1.5 s (no paint watching). Deleted, with their tests and WebKit scenarios: in `background/tabs.js` the wakes and holds, preparation, covers and paint barriers, the ping gate, `focusTabNow`, WebKit's mark and introductions, kicks, restores behind, the warm tab and the `showing` note (4,065 lines to 831); `keep-ready.js`, `warm-up.js`, `slow.js`; `content/hold.js`, `idle.js`, `shared/page-ready.js`, `away.js`; the pages' preparation, first-show and warm-up code in `content/core.js`; the handlers `prewake`, `away`, `back`, `inView`, `warmUpInput`, `warmUpStop`, `pageBlinked` and the carried-open sidebar; the switch log's switch and warm-up entries; the settings `keepLeftTabReadyMinutes` and `warmUpTabs` (saved values left alone) and the platform flags `wakeTabs`, `keepTabsReady`, `introduceTabs`, `restoreTabs`, `warmUp`, `hiddenPaints`, `asksBackground()`, `madeAfresh()`. Extension: 5,971 lines out, 379 in; tests: 8,647 out, 603 in. Sleeping tabs, the draft check, cut-off repair (a page found cut off in front is loaded again once it's left: `tabs.js tabLeft`), copies and the open-tab repair stay. Still Phase 3's: the sleep page, `diagnostics.js` (repairs, sleep, created tabs), the state's write numbers (`prepareSidebar`), the first-paint path.
- **Each switch's timeline in Margin.app's log** (`background/switch-log.js`, through `link.js note`): one line per switch once it's over, e.g. `switch to tab 2 (activateTab, window 1): asked +0, tabs.update +0, updated +1, onActivated +4, stored +66, delta out +82 ms`, with `N activations heard` if something else switched too, `failed: <why>`, or `no onActivated within 3000 ms` and the like. Ids only.
- **The palette: no slow work found off screen; one likely cause fixed.** In the app's end-to-end run (the real overlay page, Arcadia, the fixture's tabs): the app's key to the overlay holding the keyboard 22 ms; opening the palette 6–12 ms of work in the page (style and layout included); typing, each key's re-ranking and drawing 0.7–1.8 ms on average, 8 ms at most; the walk of what's over the page slot (`hitRects`, a suspect) 1–3 ms each, about 15 a run; the slot's measure about 2 ms. So not the walk, not the page's work. What was wrong: `takeKeyboard` made the panel key but never made its web view the first responder, and a panel made key with no first responder keeps the keys itself, so a palette opened by the app's key with no click in the overlay before showed its field focused while typing went nowhere: frozen-looking, and only sometimes (a click in the overlay earlier had made the web view first responder). Now the web view is made first responder (the end-to-end run checks it). Lasting diagnostics for Tirth's log: each opening (`palette opened (the app's key): drawn X ms after the word, Y ms of work, its field focused`; the drawing time needs frames, which an off-screen overlay doesn't get), the app's `the palette's key: sent to …` and `… took the keyboard Z ms after the palette's key`, and `slow: <what> took N ms (<cause>)` for any of the page's work over 50 ms (the app's word handled, a measure), a key or press not drawn within 50 ms, and WebKit's long tasks where it reports them, each kind at most every 10 s. `marginApp.stats()` has the counts.
- **The overlay on the desktop.** The overlay joins every Space (`.canJoinAllSpaces`), so it stayed drawn after a Space change until the next read of the window server (bursts at 30 a second, but the slide plays first), and a profile switch's new window, reported full screen while macOS was still taking it from the desktop into its Space, got its overlay on the desktop. Now the Space change reads the window server at once, in the same turn, and puts away every overlay whose window isn't on screen (`Shell.spaceDidChange`); an overlay is shown only over a bound window that's on screen and fills its display's height (`ScreenGeometry.fillsDisplay`: inside one display, at its bottom, its top within the camera housing or 60 pt, so one side of a Split View too); every show and put-away is logged with why. What's left: during the slide itself the overlay is still drawn (macOS says the Space changed once it's over). A better collection behavior exists in principle, `[.managed, .fullScreenAuxiliary, .ignoresCycle]`, joining only the Space the overlay is shown in (the full-screen window's), so it would slide away with Safari; whether it shows over another app's full-screen window can only be checked on screen, so it's an experiment, off by default: `defaults write io.tirth.margin MarginOverlayOwnSpace -bool YES` (TESTING.md). No private calls.
- **The left edge (Safari's own sidebar).** The overlay is one window whose `ignoresMouseEvents` follows the pointer after the fact, so moves arriving at the edge from over the page reached Safari, which reveals its sidebar from the edge in full screen. Now each shown overlay has an edge guard: a 4 pt borderless, non-activating panel at its window's left edge, the window's height, filled at alpha 0.01 (the window server passes the pointer through fully transparent pixels), the same level and collection behavior, ordered just above the overlay, never ignoring the mouse (`PassThrough.swift EdgeGuard`, `Overlay.placeEdge`). Docked too (a quick move from the page reaches the edge in one event); not while the overlay takes the whole window, nor when the overlay starts beside an inspector docked on the left; it goes and comes with its overlay. Clicks there are swallowed; positions there still reach peek (the guard is inside the overlay's frame). The top edge isn't guarded (Safari's toolbar must still come down). No documented Safari setting turns the edge reveal off (Apple's forums say there's none; an unsupported plist edit some posts mention is said to crash Safari).
- **Safari's full-screen toolbar.** Tirth's recorder (the window server's list, read only) showed it's a window of Safari's own: same process, layer 0, the window's left edge and width, 52 pt tall there, parked at the window's top less its height and sliding down over the window's top, listed above the window and below Margin's overlay. The overlay now goes between them (`WindowMatcher.fullScreenToolbar`, `order(.below, relativeTo: toolbar)`), and `stackedAbove` takes that as in place, so the toolbar slides over Margin as over the page. Tested against the recorded lists.
- **The docked Web Inspector.** The page's area is then less than the window. The page in front tells the copy where its area sits (`viewport`, in window points: its size from the viewport, its corner from any pointer event, else assumed: the width missing on the left, the height missing at the bottom in full screen), the copy puts it in that window's `windows` entry (only the tab in front's: an inspector is a tab's), and the app puts the overlay over that part only (`Shell.overlayFrame`, at least 200 pt each way), so the theme is laid out beside the inspector, nothing is drawn over it and the pointer reaches it; `setLayout`'s insets stay from the window's edges (`PageLayout.inWindow`), and the page's box takes the area's corner into account (`boxFromLayout` `x`, `y`). Resizing and closing follow (the page's resize). Full-screen windows only for now.
- **General cost.** The app handed the overlay's page every pointer move over it, at up to 30 a second, wherever the pointer was. Now the page says which it needs (`pointerWant`: none while the sidebar is docked, the reveal zone of its peek edges while it's hidden, all while it's out), and the app sends only those, only moved ones, the last of a run after the rate's wait, and the first one out of the zone once. A read of the window server and all that follows over 8 ms is logged (`slow: following the windows took …`; the first overlay's web view takes about 60 ms to make). Re-renders on deltas that change nothing visible were already skipped (the runtime draws only a new view model).
- **Tests.** Unit: 696 (from 1010: about 325 tests of deleted code gone with their files, `hold`, `idle`, `in-window`, `keep-ready`, `warm-up`, `slow-safari`, `page-ready`, `away`, `answer-in-time`, `slow-start`, most of `wake` (its homepage note and sleeping-address cases kept in `homepage-note.test.js`), parts of `spaces-switch`, `cut-off`, `copies`, `model`, `startup`, `open-behind-chrome`; new: `switch-plain.test.js` 7, `switching.test.js` 4, the inspector's box and windows entry in `link.test.js`). `npm run test:theme` 76. `npm run test:app`: 422 unit (the edge guard, pointer wants, display filling, the toolbar from the recorded lists, the inspector's frame and insets) and 160 end-to-end (the palette's timings and first responder, typing, the guard with its overlay and its frame, peek from the edge, a Space change putting the overlay away in the same turn, the inspector docked and closed).
- **WebKit host:** 57 scenarios (66, less the 10 retired with the code they tested: `navigated`, `introduce`, `heavyflip`, `shellbehind`, `deferred`, `wake-wait`, `wake-cap`, `wake-awake`, `keep-ready-cap`, `keep-ready-away`; plus `plain-switch`: a click and the app's key through the app's real link, the tab coming into view once with no flip, one activation, the timeline line, e.g. `switch to tab 65 (activateTab, window 63): asked +0, tabs.update +0, onActivated +3, updated +4, stored +6, delta out +24 ms`, and a key's switch pushed as switching then done). `switching` and `stalled` lost their switch-log checks. The host now passes the copy's log lines on as the app's (`appHeard` sees them).
- **A switch always answers within its page's deadline (after the step 8 merge, 2026-11-05).** The plain switch waited up to 2 s for Safari's answer to `tabs.update`, then for the window's focus, and the background answers "too late" 500 ms before a page's deadline: with a slow Safari (the WebKit host's `stalled`, failing 1 run in 4) the page heard "too late" though the switch was made. Now Safari's answer is waited for 1 s at most (it only confirms what Margin knows; a closed tab still fails at once), and both waits end 800 ms before the page's deadline; Safari carries the switch out either way. `stalled` passed 8 of 8 after, and a unit test covers a hung tab with a short deadline.
- **Margin's layout at once on every window switch (Tirth, 2026-11-05).** Overlays now join only their own window's Space by default (`.managed, .fullScreenAuxiliary`; the step 8 experiment) and stay there while that Space isn't showing, instead of being put away and shown again after each Space change: switching to a window, its overlay slides in with it, already drawn. The public counterpart of `feat/native-panel`'s SkyLight Space placement, with no private call. If macOS doesn't keep an overlay with its window (it isn't on screen with it 0.25 s after being placed, or it's on screen while its window isn't), every overlay goes back to joining every Space for that launch and the log says so; `defaults write io.tirth.margin MarginOverlayOwnSpace -bool NO` turns it off. The app e2e runs the windows scenario in both modes.
- **The docked inspector's area follows faster.** A page reports a resize on its next frame (and again once a drag settles, 120 ms later) instead of only after 120 ms, and the copy sends the changed area at once from the last window list it sent instead of asking Safari for the windows first; the app applies it on the same turn.

## Margin 2, Phase 3: the extension slimmed, the first paint at any layout (2026-11-05)

On `feat/margin-app` (from 5c2e5a7). `PLAN.md` §8's deletions, the new first-paint path (§14, Spike 4) and a cleanup sweep, with nothing that works now changed beyond what's listed here. Hand-written extension code: about 1,400 lines out and 700 in (the background 564/324, content 391/124, shared and state 347/181, the sleep page 66), plus the 36 generated placeholders (1,843 lines); unit and theme tests 860 out, 244 in; the e2e suite 953 out, 96 in; scripts 90/30.

- **The sleep page is gone** (`extension/sleep/`, its parts of `shared/sleep.js`, the copy move in `cut-off.js`, `wakeAsleep`, the Back guards, the pre-flight fetch and landing check, "Wake all"'s path in Safari). Safari's sleeping tabs are closed and kept since Phase 2 step 1. **A tab an earlier build left on a sleep page** (any copy's address, known by its path): Margin keeps knowing it by its page (`state/model.js seenTab`, through `shared/sleep.js oldSleepPage`), so the update's matching keeps its Margin tab; then, found by the open-tab repair as the new copy starts or as the tab comes to the front (`tabs.js leftOnSleepPage`), hidden it's closed as Margin's own close to keep (`closing.js 'keep', 'sleep'`: kept, not open, in its space, the same Margin tab, not archived), and in front it's loaded at its page's address. Chosen over loading every such tab at once: that's what sleep was for, and it's the model sleep has now. One whose sleep page names no web address is left. A tab reached later through Back into an old sleep page's entry is left as Safari shows it (handling it would make a Back trap); `oldSleepPage` and that handling can go a release or two after Phase 3. WebKit host: `sleep-leftover` (replaces `sleep-copy`).
- **"Wake all sleeping tabs" shows only where sleeping tabs are discarded** (Chrome, Edge): in Safari there's nothing open to wake. The features the app's Settings get now carry `sleepTabs` as its value ('close', 'discard').
- **A bug fixed on the way:** a Margin tab opened again from its record (one put to sleep, one lost with its window, a space's window) taught Margin a search engine from its old search page. The sleep page's wake had that rule (2026-11-04 review); closing and keeping lost it. Now such an address is noted for 30 s (`search-engine.js noteReopened`, from `openTab` with a kept tab, `openKeptBehind`, `openSpaceWindow`), replacing `sleep.js wokeToAddress`. Test: `own-search.test.js`.
- **`diagnostics.js` is gone** (its IndexedDB log, read only by the `switchLog` handler whose button went in Phase 1 step 4). What diagnosed Tirth's Safari is now a line in Margin.app's log through the link's `note()` (`switch-log.js appLog`): each sleep pass with tabs due (`sleep: 3 due, 1 put to sleep (tab 12 closed, kept, unused 262 min, its page judged by margin); skipped: playing 1, edited 1 (840 ms)`), each pass of the open-tab repair, and a start that found storage empty. Created-tab records and Chrome's wake records went with it. The switch timeline, the link's lines, overlay reasons and slow-work lines are unchanged.
- **`stateRev` and `platform.prepareSidebar`** (write numbers for pages that drew a prepared show) are gone from the store, the stored keys pages read, the link's snapshot keys and the content script. A stored `stateRev` from the build before is left in storage, unread.
- **Stubs and old ids, proved against an update from the build before (5c2e5a7).** Safari loads none of its stored registrations if a file one names is missing (2026-10-25), so: that build registers `margin-docked` (a `themes/arcadia/docked/w-<width>.css` or `closed.css`) and `margin-page` (`content/page/at-<left>.js`, `content/page.bundle.js`). Those 72 paths stay as empty files (`scripts/build.js LEGACY_FILES`; the new background drops `margin-docked` and updates `margin-page`). Every build since each older registration (the probe, the fitted layout, the frameless test build, the rail, the theme script, Arcadium's paths) unregistered it at each background start (`OLD_IDS`), so Safari's stored list no longer names them: their stubs, `OLD_IDS` and `dropOld` went, with `shared/probe.js` and `content/probe-registered.js`. Only an update straight from a build before 2026-11-05 would miss them (none shipped).
- **Upgrade paths no update from the build before needs, also gone:** `content/takeover.js` and the background's `reloadForUpdate` (an earlier build's sidebar in a page: no build since step 5 draws one), the host hiding in `cut-off.js`'s quiet probe (it now only takes the dead copy's box off) and its `proven` word (introductions went in step 8), the trackpad's and the pinch guard's "over Margin's host" checks, the guards for pages a pre-2026-11-01 layout laid out (`data-margin-shell`, `-frameless`; the `update-old-layout` scenario), and the alarm keep-ready used. Dead code of deleted machinery: the page world's catch-up for a prepared show (`box.js catchUp`, `sheets.js onCatchUp`, the page link's `catchUp`), the pages' blink watch (`switching.js onBlink`), the content script's placeholder hand-over (`layout.js syncPlaceholder`) and its observer of a new `<html>`, `scripts/build-styles.js` and `npm run styles`, unused imports and locals.
- **The first paint at any layout** (`background/registered.js`, `shared/page-box.js`, `content/page/main.js`, `themes/<id>/first-paint.css`, `scripts/build.js`). One registration, `margin-page`, while Margin.app draws: the page's script in its own world, and CSS: the layout most windows have (`chooseLayout`; a tie keeps the one registered; before any is measured, Arcadia's from the settings), spelled as 19 digit files of quarter pixels plus `at.css` (165 files built), and the active theme's first-paint file (`content/layout/theme-<id>.css`: `--margin-fp: <id>` and the box from the variables, from `boxRules`, with the theme's look around it). Nothing in it draws by itself: every rule is under `html[data-margin-at="<id>"]` (a unit test checks each theme's file), which the page's world sets only on a page starting fresh, when the variables name that theme and a layout with a box; it reads them at its start, as `<head>` and `<body>` arrive and at its first three rendering updates (WebKit applies registered CSS after that script starts), and takes the mark off at Margin's content script's first `margin:box` (its own sheet has the box from then on: the hand-over no longer needs a style read in the content script). A window whose layout isn't the registered one gets `html:root` values (or `--margin-fp: off` for no box) by `scripting.insertCSS` on `webNavigation.onCommitted` (`fixUp`; the new `webNavigation` permission). A layout change re-registers only once layouts have been the same for 2 s (`layoutsChanged`); meanwhile the fix-up has new pages right.
- **The registration is updated, never taken away, once made in a run of Safari** (storage.session `firstPaintOn`). In the WebKit host, on, then `off.css` alone (a first design: the newest sheet says off), then on again gave every new page `off.css`'s word: a registration taken away and made again doesn't win over one in between, where an update's files do (back to a layout two layouts before: right). So no box (the link off, no box at any edge, Chrome with no full-screen window) is the same registration at a layout of zeros, which the page's world reads as nothing to do (it doesn't even mark itself as run, so an injection can still come). A Safari started since has no leftovers, and a registration from before with nothing to draw is dropped then. This answers step 5's open question for the host: a dropped registration still applied to new pages can't paint a box. Whether Safari keeps applying dropped ones too, and across a relaunch, is still for Tirth's Safari.
- **Measured (WebKit host, `first-paint`, `first-paint-off`, `first-paint-theme`), with Margin's content script's word to the box held back 400 ms by the page (as Safari's content scripts come about that late):** 24 of 24 fresh pages in their box from their first frame and in every frame before that word (22 from the registration at 260/8/8/8, 300/12/16/20, 8/8/8/8, back to 260 after two others, and in Neo-brutalism's theme and back; 2 from their window's own sheet within the 2 s before the registration followed); 3 of 3 with no box in any frame (no box at any edge; Margin.app gone, the dropped registration kept by WebKit). First frames at 12–20 ms after navigation; about 23 frames each before Margin's word. With the word on time, the box the same and the mark gone.
- **Tests.** Unit 681 (from 698: the sleep page's, `stateRev`'s, the placeholders' and the takeover's tests went; new: the old sleep page's address, a sleep pass closing and keeping, Wake all only where discarded, the leftover repair in `cut-off.test.js`, the reopened search, digit files and the page's reading of them, the layout chosen and what's registered, each theme's first paint gated). `npm run test:theme` 76. `npm run test:app` 476 unit and 174 end-to-end (one check, "every binding made in the log with why", failed about 1 run in 3 before this work too: a window closed in both the browser and the window server is dropped for whichever is heard first; it now accepts either reason). e2e (Playwright, not run): 85 tests to 48, 37 of the in-page UI deleted, 45 of kept behaviour that drive or observe the in-page UI kept and skipped until they're rewritten against the app, 3 still as they were (`TESTING.md` says so).
- **WebKit host:** 59 scenarios, all passing in a full run (57, less `sleep-copy` and `update-old-layout`, plus `sleep-leftover`, `first-paint`, `first-paint-off`, `first-paint-theme`). `read-limits`' "an observer that writes back doesn't keep reads running" assumed the reads a poke starts are over in 400 ms; the first paint's hand-over changes no style read now, which shifts the page's doubling read schedule: it waits 1.6 s, the same outcome checked. `link-drop` checks the page's script stays out after the link goes, in place of logging that WebKit ran a dropped one. `sleep-kept` lost its keep-ready step.

**Superseded, kept for the record (nothing of history is deleted):** the anti-blackout sections, from "Switching without black: wait for the wake, wake early, a switch log; ⌘T and Safari's "New tabs open with"" (2026-10-26) through "After the proof runs: a show only into a free page, the flip's own word, the proof kept with the page, the keys taken first, no reload in front" (2026-11-04), whose code went in Phase 2 step 8 and here; "Themes: Margin's look as the Arcadia theme" (2026-10-25) and the theme-JS parts of "The Arcadium theme is Arcadia" (2026-10-31) and "Margin 2, Phase 1 step 4" (its placeholders and stubs went here); the sleep-page parts of "Sleeping tabs" (2026-11-04) and its review's and first check's (the page, its Back guards, its copy moves, its pre-flight); "Removing a registered script's files breaks all registered scripts" (2026-10-25) stands, applied above.

**Open, for Tirth's Safari** (`TESTING.md` "Margin 2 Phase 3"): the first paint against Safari's own timing (content scripts, `insertCSS`); whether Safari, like the host, keeps applying a dropped registration, and across a relaunch; that adding `webNavigation` prompts nothing; the update from the build before (stubs, the repair, any leftover sleep page).

## Margin 2: Safari reliability (2026-11-05)

On `fix/safari-reliability` (from `feat/margin-app` e3725be). Tirth's report from his own Safari: the sidebar disappeared after a while, an empty sidebar over one profile's page, "Margin isn't connected" in another, tab switches still flickering on fast shortcuts. Each fix below comes from Margin.app's own log of that run (21:51-21:56: three Safari profiles, one full-screen window each, all on one display) and the runs before it.

### Bindings through blips

- **What happened.** At 21:53:39.519 window 18342 (the third profile's, `#795`) was missing from one read of the window server, and the binder dropped its binding on the spot ("is no longer window 18342: window 18342 is gone from the window server"). The shell put its overlay away. 42 ms later 18342 was listed again, but the three windows have the same bounds (0,33 1512×949), so nothing could bind it again, and the overlay stayed away for the remaining 2.5 minutes. That's "saw Margin there with tabs, but it disappeared after some time". `#795` had been in full screen for 35 s by then, so this was not a full-screen transition. The same read had none of Safari's windows at all: every extension window logged "no free window of the browser has its bounds". Reads like that came back at 42.968, 44.271, 55.930, 21:56:20.641 and 21:56:21.658, mostly next to a Space change, each fixed a read later. In the earlier run, 15875 was dropped and listed again in 42 ms (19:33:18.223 → .265). It was re-bound only because it was the only window with its bounds. Of the 5 "gone from the window server" drops in the last 3 hours, those 2 were blips. The other 3 (19:30:51, 19:33:58, 19:37:19) were real goings: a link drop or a new number came right after each.
- **A bound window missing from a read keeps its binding** (`WindowMatcher.swift`, `WindowBinder.update`). A window server number is the window's for the whole login session, so the same number listed again is the same window. While a bound number is missing, it still counts as bound, so no other window can take it, and `absentNumbers` lists it. The first missing read is one line: `safari:…#795: window 18342 missing from one read (kept for 3 s)`. Listed again, the binding is live again with no line. The binding goes, with why, only when:
  - its extension window closes ("closed in the browser", the number retired, as before);
  - its copy changes or isn't linked (as before);
  - the browser isn't running (no pids);
  - it has been missing for `Timing.absent`, 3 s ("window N is gone from the window server (not listed for 3 s)"). A held (reconnecting) link's binding goes the same way.
- **A missing number keeps what the binder knows about it for the same 3 s**: its `seen` (when it was first listed), `retired` (a closed window's, never bound again) and `serverMoved`. Without this, a window back from a blip looked newly created. The "made by Margin" rule (the profile switch's openSpaceWindow) or the "created together" rule could then take it for a new window, and a closed window's number became free again. Tests show both happening before the fix.
- **A read that tells nothing.** Such a read has no list at all (`CGWindowListCopyWindowInfo` gave nil: `WindowSource.read` now returns nil for that, not `[]`), or it lost every browser-sized window the last good read had, or every bound one, or it lists none of the browser's windows while the copies report some. On such a read, while the browser runs and its copies still report windows, the binder learns and forgets nothing for up to 3 s. Bindings stay, and so do `seen`, `retired` and `serverMoved`. No front change is noted, and no binding rule runs. A new window number in the read makes it a real read, because the list is evidently live. The extension's own word still counts on such a read: a window it closed, or a copy gone or new. There is one log line per episode, with counts only: `safari: a read of the window server without the browser's windows (N entries, M the browser's, K at layer 0; window 18342 not listed): bindings kept, nothing learned from it`, or `the window server gave no list (…)` for nil. If a bound window is listed at another layer, the line gives its layer, alpha and whether it's on screen. The next Safari log will show which of these the blips are.
- **Elimination waits for a whole list.** "The only free window with its bounds" makes no binding on a read that lost a browser-sized window the last read had, unless a new number came in the same read (a window replaced). While a free window is missing, it still counts against its last bounds: one free window listed with those bounds isn't "the only one" while another that had them is missing. A window gone before a new one came isn't counted, so a close followed by a new number binds as fast as before. The moved rule and settled focus count missing free windows the same way.
- **Front changes through a blip.** The front window missing with nothing else in front isn't a change until it's back (then nothing happened) or gone. A change seen across reads that told nothing, or while the front window was missing, is kept "near" but untimed when the gap is over `Timing.blind`, 0.3 s. Other changes may have come and gone in it. This came out of the seeded runs. Seed 1049 bound a window wrong on a 0.95 s gap that hid a switch: the focus heard for the hidden switch was taken for the one seen after.
- **The shell keeps the overlay over a missing window** (`Shell.track`, `OverlayController.conceal`/`reveal`). Bound but missing from the read: the overlay is concealed. It stays ordered in its Space above its window, at alpha 0, the mouse passing through, and it keeps the keyboard if it had it. It isn't ordered out and gives nothing back. The next read that lists the window reveals it through the usual show (an overlay kept in its own Space whose window is off screen is revealed there too). `hide()` still ends a concealed overlay when the binding really goes. While the front window is missing, the keys stay Margin's: `frontOverlay` falls back to the concealed overlay over the last front window of the front browser, so a key pressed during a blip still reaches it.
- **Not changed:** an extension window that isn't bound still has nothing drawn over it. A window closed in Safari still drops at once on the copy's report. A real going (the link down, a new copy, a new number) re-binds at the first read that lists the new number, as the log's three real goings did.
- **Cost:** a window that really leaves the window server while its extension window lives on stays bound and concealed for up to 3 s. Before, it was dropped at once. Nothing is drawn over anything in that time.
- **Tests** (`tools/app-tests/window-tests.swift`, `npm run test:app`). These are new, and 29 of their checks fail against the old binder:
  - the 21:53:39 replay (one window missing for a read, then every window, then 20 s on);
  - every same-bounds window missing for 0.4 s with some reads giving no list (bindings kept, no front change, nothing new bound after);
  - the front window alone missing for 0.3 s;
  - missing past 3 s (dropped once, with why);
  - closed in the browser while missing (dropped at once, retired);
  - a retired number that blips;
  - a blip as a window is created, and during the profile switch's openSpaceWindow (no wrong binding);
  - the log's real goings (gone, link down, a new copy and number: bound at the first read that lists it), and a window replaced in one read;
  - a held link whose window is gone (dropped after 3 s).

  The seeded runs now go twice, the second time with blips: a window missing for 1-15 reads, and every window missing (a quarter of those reads with no list) for 0.2-1.1 s around each switch. In the blip runs, no binding may go for the window server before 3 s. The old binder breaks that in all 300 runs. The new one is never wrong and binds every window at the end: 3,000 runs at the default flag lag of 1.2 s, and 1,500 each at 0.3 s and 2 s. Those runs found two faults in the first version of this fix, both fixed before the commit. One is the untimed change above. The other was a run that started inside a blip: the copies report windows while the list has none of the browser's, and that read is now one that tells nothing, the first read included. `e2e-windows.swift` (both overlay modes) removes the front stand-in window for 40 ms, then every one, then gives no list. The overlay stays bound and over its window all along, concealed while its window is missing and seen again after, with no "put away", "shown" or "no longer" line.
- **Open:** whether Safari's windows really leave the list during a Space change or only change layer, and whether `CGWindowListCopyWindowInfo` returns nil then. The new log line says which. Why `#155` and `#153` were never bound in that run (same-bounds full-screen windows of several profiles, where focus-moved and settled focus don't fire) is a separate finding: this fix keeps a binding, it doesn't make one.

### An update never leaves a stale Margin.app

- **What happened.** This explains the link outages of the earlier runs, not the 21:51 run, which never lost its link (all three copies linked at 21:52:19-20 and stayed linked, with no SFErrorDomain lines). Twice, a Margin.app process answered every `wantLink` with a `link` that Safari refused: pid 73613 from 19:31:55 to 19:32:09, and pid 50812 from 19:55:00 to 19:57:40 (17 times, for s-default and for the newly opened s-4282ea5b). The error was SFErrorDomain 1, which is `SFErrorNoExtensionFound` in SFError.h. The copies logged "Margin.app is running but sent no link within 2000 ms", backed off to 60 s and went 'off', so new tabs said "Margin.app isn't running". Each streak ended only when a new pid started, and that pid linked on its first dispatch. That's "another profile says Margin isn't connected" in those runs.
- **Why it lasted.** `scripts/safari.sh` swapped the bundle (renamed aside, new one in, old one deleted) and never quit the running app. The header of `AppDelegate.swift` said it sent a SIGTERM and reopened with `--background`. That was never true on this branch: a9d34c4, which did it, is only on feat/native-panel. The extension handler counts any running `io.tirth.margin` as open, so it never started the new build. `SafariDispatch` only logged the failure, and `answer()` logged "link sent" whatever happened, even right after "not sent: Safari isn't running". The 19:55:21-24 Release build falls inside the 50812 streak, which fits an update as the trigger. The first two failures (19:55:00 and :07) came about 20 s before that build's log, and the build log store is pruned, so an earlier install can't be shown. A second trigger can't be ruled out either.
- **The script quits the old app after the swap** (`scripts/app-swap.sh`, sourced by `safari.sh`). Before the swap it notes the pids of `pgrep -x Margin` whose executable (`ps -o comm=`) is `$DEST/Contents/MacOS/Margin`. That is never the build folder's copy or Xcode's. It renames the bundles and registers the new one (lsregister, the pluginkit loop) as before. Only then does it send the noted pids a SIGTERM (the app's normal quit: the copies are told goodbye), wait up to 5 s, and kill any that are left. Only once they're gone does it delete `$DEST.old`. Quitting after the swap means anything that starts Margin.app meanwhile (the handler, for a copy's link) starts the new build. If one was running and Safari is running, and no new Margin pid from `$DEST` appeared within 2 s, it runs `open -g "$DEST" --args --background`, never `-j`: a hidden Margin.app shows no overlays. It prints `quit the running Margin.app (pid N)`. A first install still opens the set-up window.
- **One instance at a time** (`AppDelegate.endOthers`, `BundleWatch.othersToEnd`). At launch, before the shell starts, the app looks for other running apps with its bundle id whose bundle path (standardised) is its own, or its own plus `.old`, and that aren't itself. It sends each a SIGTERM, waits up to 2 s, then starts. The new instance ends the old one, never the other way round. It's a SIGTERM rather than `NSRunningApplication.terminate()`: that is a quit Apple Event, which might need the person's permission for Automation, and the app's SIGTERM handler is the same quit. The test hosts (tools/app-tests, webkit-host) don't run AppDelegate and have no such bundle, so they're unaffected.
- **The app notices it was replaced** (`app/shell/BundleWatch.swift`). At launch it notes its executable's `fileResourceIdentifier` and `contentModificationDate`. It compares them on the 30 s idle-check timer and right after any dispatch Safari didn't pass on. When they differ, it logs `Margin.app was replaced on disk: quitting so the new build starts (…)` and quits. The copies get `bye`, ask again 5 s later, and the handler starts the installed app (its startedForLink path, no set-up window). It does this whether or not links are up, because the old process would run old code against the new bundle's overlay files (`OverlayWebView.appRoot` is `Bundle.main.resourceURL`). It never quits on SFErrorDomain 1 alone: the extension's record can be gone for seconds after an install (safari.sh's pluginkit loop), and a new process wouldn't help then. Consecutive failures are counted for the log only. An executable that can't be read (mid-swap) is looked at again next time. This covers updates made any other way too (a copy by hand, an Xcode archive).
- **Dispatch results** (`ExtensionLink.swift`). `ExtensionDispatch.dispatch` takes `done: (String?) -> Void`: nil means delivered, anything else is why not (`Safari isn't running`, `not JSON`, or the error's domain and code, as `SFErrorDomain 1`). `SafariDispatch` calls it at once for its own refusals and after the await otherwise. The log now says `wantLink: link handed to Safari (port P, #n)` and then either `link #n reached the extension` or `link #n not passed on: SFErrorDomain 1` (with `(N in a row)` from the second), and the same for `appUp`. The old unconditional "link sent" and "link not passed on to Margin: …" lines are gone.
- **The copy's side** (`background/link.js`). After "Margin.app is running but sent no link", it asks again after `NO_LINK_ASK_MS` (5 s) at most `NO_LINK_ASKS` (3) times in a row, then goes back to the doubling backoff from there (10, 20, 40, 60 s), so it never holds 5 s forever. Before, it was 5, 10, 20, 40, 60. The count resets on a link, on any other kind of failure, and on the new-tab page's Start. While in that state, `appLink` carries `why: 'no-link'`. It is cleared on any state but 'off', and when the app isn't running or is being started. The new-tab page then says "Margin.app isn't answering", with "Margin.app is running but didn't link to Margin. Margin keeps asking; this page changes once it's linked." and no Start button (the app is running, so starting it does nothing). The text changes in place when `why` changes. This is visible: a new heading and text on the new-tab page in that one state.
- **Tests.**
  - `tests/app-swap.test.js` runs `scripts/app-swap.sh` against a temporary folder. The stand-in app is a copy of `/bin/sleep` (signed ad hoc, since an unsigned copy is killed at launch) named Margin and started from the folder's Margin.app. It checks that the stand-in is still running at registration with the new build already in place, is sent a SIGTERM after the swap and waited for, and that the old copy is deleted only after it has gone. One that ignores the SIGTERM is killed after the wait, and its copy is kept until then. A Margin started from elsewhere (a build folder) is left alone. One started during the swap (the handler) is the new build, kept running, and not opened a second time. With nothing running, nothing is quit or started. The steps that would reach the system are the test's own. It never looks at a process outside the folder.
  - `tools/app-tests/e2e.swift`: a `FakeDispatch` that refuses with "SFErrorDomain 1" makes the log say `not passed on`, never `reached` or `link sent`, and nothing reaches the copy. Three refusals with the same build on disk don't quit. Another build in place quits at the next refusal, exactly once, while a link is up. The 30 s check quits once for a file copied over in place (same id, new date), and not for one that can't be read for a moment.
  - `tools/app-tests/tests.swift`: which instances a new one ends, and the identity of a real executable through the rename-aside, rename-in swap.
  - `tests/link.test.js`: three asks at the short wait, then doubling, with `why: 'no-link'`. Linked at last: no `why` left. Not running: no `why`.
  - The WebKit host's `newtab-no-link` scenario: the app's end of the link (`link-server`, new `refuse` input, host step `appRefuse`) refuses every dispatch. The new-tab page says it isn't answering, with no Start button. Once the dispatches go through, the copy links at the next 5 s ask and the page is the app's.
- **Open:** whether the 19:55:00 failures had a trigger other than an install. The new `#n` lines and the `replaced on disk` line will say, next time it happens.

### Telling same-bounds full-screen windows of different profiles apart

- **What happened.** From 21:52:20 to 21:53:02 two profiles' full-screen windows (`#155`, `#153`) and two window server windows with the same bounds (18278, 18285) sat unbound for 42 s with Safari in front: no link drop, no Space change. The same happened in the 19:58 run (37 s), and at 21:53:44-55 with three copies and three windows (11 s). An unbound window gets no overlay, but its page keeps the app's box. That's "an empty sidebar, but one of my tabs' content was there". `real.txt` has no "settled focus" binding in any run. The bindings made were 4 by "the only free window" (one window each time) and 1 by "focus moved". Settled focus needed exactly one window called focused across every copy, plus that window's `focusedAt` being the newest any copy heard. Each Safari profile runs its own copy, and a copy's `windows.getAll()` `focused` is about its own windows only. The log can't say which gate failed (each copy flagging its own window, none flagged, or `latest`), because nothing logged the flags. Whichever it was, a copy's flag can't single out one profile's window from another's. And if Safari flagged a background profile's window, `fits` would pass for same-bounds windows and settled focus would bind it wrong.
- **Settled focus is within one copy now** (`WindowMatcher.swift`). It runs only when every window the copies report with the front window's bounds is one copy's. Its flag orders that copy's windows, never another profile's. A copy that heard `WINDOW_ID_NONE` after its flagged window's focus doesn't bind it either. When it can't bind, it says why, once per change of reason: `safari: settled focus can't bind window N: links held (…)`, `… the windows with its bounds are 3 copies' (…): a copy's focus flags only order its own windows`, `focused count N (…)`, `… is bound to another window`, `its copy heard none of its windows focused …`, `…'s page says it's hidden`, `sameServer X vs sameExt Y`, `… is not the newest focus its copy heard`. One profile's two full-screen windows still bind by it, as before.
- **The pages say which window is shown.** This is evidence each copy has about its own windows. `content/core.js` and the new-tab page send `{ type: 'visibility', visible, at }` (`document.visibilityState`, `Date.now()`) at each `visibilitychange`, on a `pageshow` from the back/forward cache, once as they start, and when the background asks (`reportVisibility`). This is Safari only (`platform.nativeApp`). The background (`background/link.js`) keeps the word per tab, and each window's `visible`/`visibleAt` comes from its tab in front only. It forgets a window's word when another tab is shown in it (`tabs.onActivated`; the new tab is asked again), when the tab's page commits a navigation (a word said before the commit), and when the tab is removed, replaced or moved to another window. As it starts, it asks every page in front again. That's the active tab of each window, not every tab, because only the active tab's word counts and asking a tab behind could wake its process. Missing is unknown, never hidden. `blurredAt` (the copy's last `WINDOW_ID_NONE`) goes on each window's entry. Documented in `app/PROTOCOL.md`.
- **The visible rule** (`WindowBinder.update`). It binds extension window X to window server window W when all of these hold for `visibleHold` (0.3 s):
  - the browser is in front and no link is held;
  - the reads are steady: at least two in a row over 0.3 s that told something and missed no window;
  - W is free and the only window on screen with its bounds;
  - every window with those bounds is a reported one (so a Private window, or a profile without Margin, blocks it);
  - X's page alone, among every window with those bounds, says it's shown, said as of now (below);
  - every other free window with those bounds says it's hidden.

  Bound windows elsewhere aren't asked. A page that said nothing blocks while its window is free. The log line looks like `…#153 is window 18285: visible: its page alone says it's shown (since 21:52:20.104), window 18285 the only one on screen with its bounds (3 window(s) with them, all reported)`.
- **"As of now".** A page's word may come late (WebKit's occlusion, a busy background). So a word counts only if it was said after the last *transition*: a new front window, from the read before it was seen, or a Space change as Margin.app heard it. It must also be `visibleLag` (1 s) after the transition before that, so a late word from an earlier switch isn't taken for this one. The Shell passes the last `activeSpaceDidChange` into `update` (`spaceChangedAt`, on the extension's clock). A Space change heard within 1 s after a new front window is the same transition: macOS says the Space changed once its slide is over (21:53:44.475 read, 44.479 notice). A new front window after a Space change is a transition of its own. A "hidden" said before the last transition counts again once 1 s has passed since it: a page that was hidden and still is needn't say so again. With three profiles, the third window is usually the one that didn't change. "Shown" always has to be said since the transition. With no transition since Margin.app started (a relaunch with Safari's windows open), any word counts, but not in Margin.app's first second, because a switch just before it may not have been said yet. If Safari never marks the pages of a full-screen window on another Space hidden, no window ever has the only page saying it's shown, and the rule never binds. So it ships turned on, and it can't guess.
- **When it can't** (once per window per transition, after the pages have had their time): `safari: window W (on screen, …) not bound from its page: <reason>`. The reasons are: the reads aren't steady yet, N windows with its bounds are on screen, N window(s) have its bounds and M are reported, no page with its bounds says it's shown since T, N pages say so, X says it's shown but it's bound to another window, or X said nothing of being hidden since T.
- **A binding the pages contradict goes.** A bound window on screen, the only one with its bounds there, whose page has said it's hidden (since the last transition) for `hiddenDrop` (2 s), while another profile's same-bounds window's page says it's shown (since then), is dropped: `…is no longer window N: its page has said it's hidden since … while window N is on screen, and …'s page says it's shown (since …): not its window`. If that other window is bound to one not on screen, it's dropped too. Neither check runs within 1 s of a Space change, on unsteady reads, or on pages that said nothing. Elimination and settled focus never pair a window with the one on screen while the pages say it isn't that one, so a dropped binding isn't made again a read later.
- **Focus moved and blips.** A front change to no window that lasted under 0.5 s between two with one (Safari leaving the front a moment, a window off screen for a read) isn't counted against a focus, nor is the change back to the window from before it. Everything else is as before, including the 0.68 s double switch being refused. Each focus weighed and not used says why: `…: focus heard at T not used: contradicted (…)`, `others (…)`, `near count N (front changes within 1 s: …)`, `at count N (…)`, or `doesn't fit window N (…)`.
- **The front window's changes are logged**, as numbers or `none`, at most one line a second: `safari: the front window: 18342 at 21:53:03.004, none at …`.
- **The extension's side in Margin.app's log** (`appLink.note`, ids and times only): `focus heard: window 795 at 21:53:02.735`, or `focus heard: none (WINDOW_ID_NONE) at …`, and on each windows report whose flags changed: `windows report at …: 795 focused (focus heard …) shown 21:53:03.010; blur heard …`.
- **The moved rule, hardened** (the latent wrong sidebar next to a Private window). The binder keeps each window server window's settled bounds: the last it held for `moved` (2 s) with no Space change meanwhile. A binding moves to another window only when all of these hold:
  - the extension's window itself reported new bounds since the binding was made;
  - its own window has settled at other bounds;
  - the other window has kept the bounds all along;
  - as many of the browser's windows have those bounds (bound ones included, a window counted by its settled bounds too) as the copies report.

  The first condition is mine, beyond the finding's fix. Without it, a bound window offset for 5 s (a held swipe) next to an unreported same-bounds window still moved, because its own window had settled elsewhere and the counts were 1 and 1. A window the window server alone moves is in a swipe, Mission Control or full screen's animation, never a reason to move a binding. The case it gives up is a wrong binding mended by the wrong window moving while the extension's doesn't. Two focus changes, or the pages' drop, still mend that.
- **Elimination, hardened.** A free window counts as having bounds B if it has them now or settled there and hasn't settled elsewhere since. So a window offset for a moment still counts as a rival, and the one picked must have B now. For full-screen bounds (any reported window with them in full screen), "the only free window" binds only when every window with B is a reported one, no link is held, and every copy that has reported this session still reports ("copies seen" come from the links that reported, not from Safari's profile list, whose mapping is wrong today). This applies even to one window and one extension window with those bounds, since a held copy's window may be the one listed while this one's is missing. That's a deviation from "a window whose bounds nobody else shares stays instant": it holds for windows that aren't full screen, and for full-screen ones once no link is held. Cost: while a profile's link is held (reconnecting, at most 6 s), a new full-screen window waits. If a profile's copy goes for good mid-session (Margin turned off there), full-screen elimination stays off until Margin.app restarts; the visible rule and focus still bind. (Changed in "Review fixes" below: for 5 s after its last report.)
- **Not changed:** an unbound window still has nothing drawn over it. Focus moved, created together and made by Margin are as before.
- **Tests** (`tools/app-tests/window-tests.swift`). The test world now has pages that say they're shown or hidden a set time after the window server shows or hides their window. Some say nothing, words can be forgotten and said again, and Space changes can be heard late.
  - The relaunch with three copies, each calling its own window focused: nothing in Margin.app's first second, then the shown window bound by its page, and the others as each Space is shown.
  - Fast back-and-forth switches with the pages lagging up to 0.6 s (40 runs): never wrong, and all bound once each was shown calmly.
  - No page saying: nothing bound. One page silent: nothing bound by the others' word while it's free.
  - A Private window with the same bounds, shown while the page shown before still says so for up to 3 s (40 runs): never bound to it. The log says why.
  - A wrong binding the pages contradict: dropped after 2 s with the other one, then both bound right and never wrong again.
  - The moved rule: a bound window offset 2.5 s and 5 s next to an unreported same-bounds window keeps its binding.
  - Elimination with a copy held and a window missing from the reads: never wrong.
  - Within one copy, settled focus still binds; a copy that heard a blur after its window's focus doesn't.
  - Focus moved through a 0.2 s blip of no front window binds; through a 0.6 s one it doesn't.
  - The new log lines.
  - 1,000 seeded runs of 20 switches among four same-bounds windows of three profiles (a Private window in 40% of runs): blips, the pages' word lagging up to 0.6 s (up to 3 s for the one shown before a Private window), 15% of pages never saying, words forgotten, Safari leaving the front, and Space changes heard from 0.1 s before to 0.8 s after the switch. Never bound wrong at any read. With every page saying and no Private window, every window is bound once each is shown calmly.

  The old "two copies, one called focused: settled focus binds after 5 s" check now checks the opposite on purpose: that across copies settled focus doesn't bind and says why, and that the pages then bind it. The two-profile set-ups that leaned on cross-copy settled focus now have their pages saying. The "both copies calling their window focused: nothing bound, nothing guessed" case is unchanged as the no-visibility case. `tests/link.test.js` checks that `visible`/`visibleAt` come only from the tab in front, are forgotten on activation and on a commit after the word, that `blurredAt` is recorded, and that the focus and flag lines go to Margin.app's log with ids and times only. The WebKit host's new `visibility` scenario checks the same through the real extension: a page in front says it's shown, another tab shown makes the window's word unknown and then that tab's, a tab behind is never the window's, and the new-tab page says it too.
- **This answers the open question from Phase 2 step 6** (what Safari's `focused` flags and `onFocusChanged` do across profiles): across profiles the binder no longer uses them, whatever they do. What's left to learn from Safari is whether its full-screen windows' pages go hidden when another Space shows, and how fast. TESTING.md "Telling same-bounds windows apart" asks for exactly that, from the new lines. If they don't, the rule never fires, and same-bounds windows of several profiles still wait for a focus change.
- **Open:**
  - The unbound twin of the moved case: an unbound window whose own window is offset for over 2 s, while a same-bounds window no copy reports keeps the bounds, can still be bound to that one by elimination once its own has settled elsewhere. Nothing tells them apart then short of a whole-list check that would block every full-screen window whenever Safari's Settings or a Private window is open.
  - The visible rule's timings (`visibleLag` 1 s, `visibleHold` 0.3 s, `hiddenDrop` 2 s) are guesses until Safari's numbers are in.

### Windows Margin.app doesn't draw over: no box

- **What happened.** In the 21:51 run, `#155` and `#153` were never bound (three same-bounds full-screen windows of three profiles), so Margin.app drew nothing over them for the whole run. Their pages were boxed all the same: the box is decided per copy (`appDraws`: linked or reconnecting), the first-paint registration covers every page, its layout was the settings' (260/8/8/8 with Keep open) since no window had been measured, `fixUp` leaves a window with no layout of its own alone, and a page whose window has no layout keeps its box. So the page sat beside Arcadia's grey with nothing drawn there: "an empty sidebar but the tab content of one of my tabs was there". Every normal (windowed) Safari window was boxed the same way, since the app draws over full-screen windows only (`windowedToo` is off until Phase 4). And `layoutFor` gave a known window another window's layout when only one existed.
- **The app says which windows it draws over** (`drawn { epoch, windowId, on }`, app/PROTOCOL.md; `Shell.swift` DrawnOutbox). It's about the binding, not whether the overlay is on screen: on while the window is bound and wanted (full screen, or `windowedToo`, with a theme). An overlay left in its window's Space, or concealed through a blip, stays on, and a Space change never sends anything. On goes at the bind, in the same pass before the overlay shows and before any `setLayout`. Off goes after a grace, one timer per window: 1.5 s not wanted (it covers the full-screen animation), 1.5 s never bound since the copy first reported it, 3 s unbound after being bound (as long as the binder keeps a window missing from a read), and at once for a closed window that was on. Only a change is sent. A line in the log says each one: `safari:…#N: told not drawn: not bound to one of the window server's since its copy reported it for 1.5 s`. A layout now goes only for a bound window.
- **The copy keeps it per window, apart from the layouts** (`appLink.drawn`, `background/link.js`). A new epoch's hello clears it together with the layouts of older epochs; going off clears both; a background starting again keeps only the windows `windows.getAll()` still lists. A copy never told behaves as before (an older app). The cleared layouts are kept as `lastLayouts`, which no page reads: the first paint's registration starts from them until the new epoch says something. Without that, the host's `first-paint-off` showed a relink registering the settings' layout and then, 2 s later, the app's again (one more registration piled up in WebKit per relink, and per Safari launch, since window ids change then).
- **What pages do with it.** `layoutFor` gives a window with `drawn` false a layout of zeros, so `settle` takes the box away (null still means "not measured yet: keep the box"). For a known window it gives only that window's own layout now; the one-layout fallback is only for a page that doesn't know its window yet, and never an undrawn window's. Open pages get the change through the existing paths: the tab in front `appLayout` (now with `drawn`), the others the `appLink` change as they're shown. No new page-world message.
- **The first paint.** `fixUp` puts `--margin-fp: off` into a page committing in an undrawn window. The page's world (`content/page/main.js`) now takes the box away when it reads `off` after an earlier look started the registered one: the host showed the registered CSS read at document start and the inserted `off` before the first frame, so without that the first frames were boxed until the content script spoke (the same was true before for a window whose own layout has no box). `chooseLayout` counts only this epoch's layouts of windows not `drawn` false. The settings' layout is used only before the app has said anything of a window this epoch; a drawn window not measured yet keeps the registered one; every window undrawn registers no box. Drawn changes go through the same `LAYOUT_SETTLE_MS`, so a binding that comes and goes doesn't pile registrations up in WebKit.
- **What this doesn't do.** It doesn't bring the sidebar back over a window the binder can't tell apart; the binding fixes above do that. With this, such a window shows its page as it is, with no empty gutter, instead of a boxed page beside nothing.
- **Tests.** `tests/link.test.js` (drawn per window, dropped on a new epoch and when off, a closed window's dropped, a restart keeps listed windows only; `layoutFor({ layouts: { 7: a } }, 9)` is null; drawn false gives no box), `tests/registered.test.js` (`chooseLayout` ignores undrawn and older-epoch windows; the settings' only before any word), `tests/undrawn-window.test.js` (`fixUp` puts in `off` for an undrawn window only, and a window's own layout once it's drawn again; a window not said of keeps the registered box), `tools/app-tests` (DrawnOutbox's timings; in `e2e-windows.swift`, the three same-bounds windows and the normal one told not drawn, never before 1.5 s, on as each binds and before its layout, nothing through four lagging Space swipes and three blips, with the overlay left in another Space in the own-Space run, off for a closed one; no layout for an unbound window), and the WebKit host's `undrawn` scenario (the open page loses its box, two fresh pages none from their first frame, before and after the registration follows, back in its box with no frame anywhere else, and fresh pages boxed from frame one after).

### Keys in a window Margin.app doesn't draw over

- **What happened.** At 21:52:42-47 Tirth pressed ⌘T four times in `#153`, which was never bound, and nothing happened: four `palette reached the extension (page) while the app holds it`. The app's hot keys are on only while the front window has a shown overlay, so the page heard ⌘T, but `content/core.js` decided by the copy's link state alone: it stopped the key and told the app (`keyHeard`), which only logged it, with a line that said the opposite of what was true. The background's commands did the same. Safari's own New Tab there was worse in code (no log shows it): the copy pushed `palette` and closed the new tab, and the shell gave the palette to an overlay that wasn't on screen (`#795 took the keyboard, but it isn't shown` at 21:53:03.996 is that, for a new window).
- **The copy decides per window now** (`drawsWindow`: linked or reconnecting, and not `drawn` false for that window). In a window the app draws nothing over: ⌘T heard by a page sends `newTabKey` (Margin's new-tab page, as when not linked); the `new-tab` and `open-palette` commands do the same; `toggle-sidebar` is logged in Margin.app's log (`toggle-sidebar heard in window N: Margin.app draws nothing over it, so there's no sidebar to show`), since there's nothing to toggle; `newTabHandOff` keeps a new tab Safari made and pushes nothing; `newTabPage` answers `app: false`. A window not said of yet is the app's, as before, so a new full-screen window's new-tab page still gets the app's launcher.
- **The app's safety net** (`Shell.swift`). A `palette` push for a window whose overlay isn't shown is held up to 1.5 s and given to the overlay as it shows (`palette push for …: given to its overlay as it showed`), else dropped (`… dropped: no overlay was shown there within 1.5 s`). The first line says why: no overlay, its window isn't bound, or not shown yet. The task asked to drop at once for a window with no bound overlay; held for everyone instead, because a new window's new-tab page pushes its palette about a second before its window is bound (`#795`: the push at 03.996, bound and shown at 04.938), and dropping it would lose the launcher's palette that shows today. A palette no longer opens in an overlay that isn't on screen. `keyHeard` is logged by the shell with what it knows: `safari:…#153: palette reached the extension (page): the app's hot keys were off: the front window has no overlay shown`, or that they were on (another combination, or Safari's own command). The overlay's own line no longer says "while the app holds it".
- **Tests.** `tests/undrawn-window.test.js` (the commands and the page's ⌘T open Margin's new-tab page in an undrawn window and push nothing; in a drawn one they're left to the app; the sidebar's key opens nothing; Safari's new tab kept there with no palette pushed, handed off in a drawn window), `e2e-windows.swift` (a palette for an unbound window held, then dropped, the hidden overlay never taking the keyboard; a palette for a bound window not shown yet given to it as it shows, its field taking the keyboard; the keyHeard line), and the WebKit host's `undrawn` (⌘T in the undrawn window's page opens Margin's new-tab page, which stays).

### The new-tab page follows the link when it misses a word

- **What happened.** Tirth's "then I opened another profile and it says Margin isn't connected or something". Not shown by the log. The reviews split: the copies in that run had one web page each at launch (`the open-tab repair, pass 0: 1 tabs, 1 asked`), so no new-tab page restored then can have shown it; but the third copy's later windows and any new tab could. What the code shows: `newtab.js` read `appLink` once, then registered its listener after that read's `await` (a write landing in between was lost), had no re-read when shown, though DECISIONS.md "One sidebar across pages" says the new-tab page has one, and decided by the copy's link state alone. So in a window Margin.app drew nothing over, a linked copy's new-tab page drew nothing (a blank tab); and a stale "Margin.app isn't running" stayed until something wrote `appLink` again. The other candidate, ShellProfiles.swift's "<Profile> isn't open in Safari", logs `profile switch:`, and the run has none.
- **Now** (`newtab/newtab.js`): it registers the listener first, reads, then reads once more; reads again on `visibilitychange` to visible, `pageshow` from the back/forward cache and `focus`; reacts to `drawn` as well as `state` (its window from `tabs.getCurrent`); and loads again when what it should show changes. Its Start reads `appLink` first and loads again if linked by then, instead of asking for the link. Linked, with its window `drawn` false, it shows the address field with "Margin.app hasn't placed this window yet", no Start and nothing about the app not running.
- **Tests.** `tests/newtab-page.test.js` (a "linked" written before the listener is caught by the second read; a hidden page missing the event loads again when shown, on pageshow and on focus; Start loads again instead of starting a linked app; linked but undrawn shows the address field and why, and loads again once drawn; four of the five fail on the code before), and the WebKit host's `undrawn` (the new-tab page ⌘T opened in the undrawn window says so, with no Start).
- **Open.** Ask Tirth which words he saw ("Margin.app isn't running" here, or "<Profile> isn't open in Safari" from a profile switch).

### The log says why a link went down, and the socket guard ends

- **What happened.** At 19:54:53 in the earlier run the app logged `link down (…): closed by the extension` and nothing else: no `dropped:` note from the copy, and no try of the kept link reached the app before the 19:55:00 ask. The extension handler's pid changed from 52855 to 63172 there, which points to the copy's background going away, but the log can't settle it. The app read every close frame as `close(1000, "closed by the extension")` and dropped its code and reason, so a copy's own drop, a background torn down (1001) and a plain TCP end looked the same. A socket that ended before its hello left no line, because `closed()` returned early. The listener's states other than `.ready` and `.failed` weren't logged. The copy noted nothing when its background started, nothing per try of the kept link, nothing when the native port went, and a note made while the port was down was lost (`note()` swallowed the error and still counted the line as sent for its 60 s filter).
- **The app's end** (`LinkSocket.swift`, `ExtensionLink.swift`). `FrameReader` reads the close frame's payload: no payload is 1005, a payload of one byte is refused, and the reason is clipped to 123 bytes, taken only as UTF-8 (never half a character), with control characters as spaces. The log says `closed by the extension (1000 no word from the app for 12 s)`, `(1001 going away)`, `(1005, no status code)`, and `(1006, no close frame)` for a TCP end with none. The WebKit host's `link-guard` run shows WebKit's own teardown of a background as `closed by the extension (1001 going away)`, so the next Safari log can tell that from the copy's own drop. A socket that ends before its hello is logged as `socket ended before its hello (after|before the upgrade, N ms): <why>`, which covers the 'no hello within 5 s' close (1008), a TCP connection closed before the upgrade, and a copy not in charge. A refused one is still logged once, as refused. The listener logs `.waiting(error)`, `.setup` and `.cancelled`. A `.waiting` that lasts 5 s (`Timing.listenWait`; after sleep or wake) cancels the listener and makes a new one. Its port changes, so copies holding the old one find their kept link refused and ask again. A `.cancelled` the app didn't ask for starts a new one after 1 s, as `.failed` does. The `hello` line adds `; its link was off before: …` when the copy says so.
- **The copy's notes** (`background/link.js`). Every line is ids, ports, times and reasons, never an address or a title. It notes:
  - `background started (kept link: yes|no, page state before: <appLink's state>, last stop: …)`. The last stop comes from storage.session `appLinkLast` (`{ what, why, at }`, written on every link, drop and stop), so `none since linked at …` means the background before was torn down while linked, and `none noted this Safari session` means a new session.
  - each try of the kept link and how it went: `kept link at the background's start (port P): …`, `kept link try n of 5 (port P): …`. The outcomes are `open in N ms`, `refused or closed at once (N ms, <code>)` under 100 ms, `closed after N ms before the welcome (<code>)`, `no welcome within N ms (slow)`, `not Margin.app (wrong welcome)`, and `sockets off`.
  - the native port disconnecting (with `runtime.lastError`) and coming back (`after N s, k disconnects since the last such note`), each at most once a minute.
  - `dropped: closed by the app (<code> <reason>)` instead of `dropped: closed`.
  - every close it makes now has a code and a reason: the copy's give-ups, its close after the app's `bye`, and its drops.
- **Notes while the port is down.** The newest 20 are kept and sent once the port is back (it's made again at the next ping, at most 20 s later), in order, each as `(at HH:MM:SS.mmm) <line>`, after a line saying how many older ones were lost. The 60 s repeat filter counts only lines actually sent. The 300-character limit applies to the line as sent.
- **A check that runs late.** The copy checks the link every 2 s. When a check runs more than 3 checks late (the background was paused, or the Mac slept), it notes `paused N s (a link check ran late); probing the app, judged from now`, sends the app a `tock` and judges nothing on that run. The link is judged on a `setTimeout(…, 0)` after it, so socket messages queued through the pause arrive first, and the app's quiet is counted from the probe at the earliest. A paused background no longer drops a live link the moment it wakes, and a dead app still drops within 12 s of the probe. Quiet is measured with `performance.now()` now (`now` in `createLink`'s deps), so a change of the wall clock can't drop or keep a link. DEAD stays 12 s.
- **The socket guard.** It turned a profile's link off "until another version of Margin". Every build is version 0.1.0 (`Margin 0.1.0` in each hello), so that meant forever, across Safari restarts and rebuilds. A background torn down between writing `appLinkOpening` and removing it was enough: an update, or Safari quitting during a reconnect. Not seen in these logs: every copy that said hello linked, and the guard's own lines (which do reach the log) never appear. It's a latent hazard, not a cause of Tirth's report. Now:
  - a note found left the first time turns sockets off for the rest of that Safari session only (storage.session `appLinkSessionOff { at, why }`), and counts the sighting in storage.local `appLinkHung { count, at }`;
  - found again in a later session, before any link opened: `appLinkOff { at, until: at + 24 h, count, why }`, honoured only until `until`. An old `{ v }` is removed, not honoured;
  - a link that opens says the sightings to the app in its hello (`socketsOff`: `the socket guard found an unfinished socket in N Safari sessions, last at …`) and clears the count;
  - each time it turns sockets off, it says so in Margin.app's log (`the socket guard turned the link off: …; no link for the rest of this Safari session`, or `… until <time>`), and each later start says `sockets off since …` again;
  - `appLink` carries `why: 'sockets-off'` with `offAt` (and `offUntil` for the 24 h one). The new-tab page says "Margin turned its link off at <time> after a socket never opened. It tries again when Safari next starts." (or "at <time>"), with a Try again button and no Start. Try again (`retryLink`, a person's choice) clears the notes and the count, turns sockets on and asks for the link at once.
  - `open()` puts the socket's handlers on (`follow`) before anything is awaited and only then removes the note, now awaited, so a link that opened leaves no note behind. It never awaits storage between `new WebSocket` and its handlers, because a welcome that came meanwhile would be lost.
  - Why session-first and not a build id: the build-id fix would churn the committed `dist/` on every build, or reset with every code change. The real hang only ever happened in a worker, and `canSocket()` keeps sockets out of workers since 2026-10-30. With the session-only first sighting, a real hang in a page background costs one session, then 24 h, never forever.
- **Settings.** Settings is drawn by Margin.app over the link, so with the link off there's no Settings to say it in. The new-tab page says it.
- **Tests.**
  - `tools/app-tests/tests.swift`: close frames of 1001 'x', empty (1005), with a copy's reason, one byte (refused), a reason that isn't UTF-8, one over 123 bytes, and one with a newline, and how each reads in the log.
  - `tools/app-tests/e2e.swift`: through the real link, with a socket done by hand in `fake-extension.mjs` (`rawConnect`, `rawClose`, `rawEnd`; a page's WebSocket can't send 1001 or an empty close). The log says `link down (…): closed by the extension (1001 x)`, `(1005, no status code)`, `(1000 no word from the app for 12 s)` and `(1006, no close frame)`. A socket ended after the upgrade with no hello, a TCP connection ended before the upgrade, and the 'no hello within 2 s' timeout are each logged as ended before their hello.
  - `tests/link.test.js`, eight new tests, all failing on the code before:
    - notes with the port down arrive in order with their times once it's back, the newest 20 kept;
    - the start and kept-link notes, with the last stop;
    - a late check with the app alive doesn't drop;
    - a late check with the app silent drops 0.4–0.9 s after the probe (DEAD 0.4 s there), not at once;
    - a left note in an empty session is off for that session only, links in the next, and says so in the hello;
    - seen in two sessions, off until +24 h, then it links, and an old `{ v }` off is removed;
    - the welcome is taken while the note's removal resolves 50 ms late;
    - Try again links.
  - The old test pinning "off for this version" is replaced: that behaviour was the bug.
  - The WebKit host's new `link-guard` scenario: a left note (written by hand, then Margin reloaded) turns sockets off, pages say `sockets-off`, the new-tab page says so with Try again, and Try again links.
- **Not tested:** the listener's `.waiting` restart. Network.framework can't be made to wait in a test here.

### Safari's profile names go to the copies that link

- **What happened.** Every `safari profiles:` line in every run (19:32, 19:57, 21:36, 21:51) mapped “Personal” to `s-5068e0e4-…` and “Test” to `s-be3f2b99-…`. The copies that said hello in those profiles were `s-8d454f81-…` and `s-4282ea5b-…`, the same ids in every run. So the two named profiles were shown as “Profile 2” and “Profile 3” with no Safari symbol, and the profile switch told Tirth to use “File › New Profile 2 Window”, which Safari doesn't have. Step 9's own check (the profile shown with `s-8d454f81-…` must be the one that copy runs in) failed from the start and nothing flagged it. This is only about names, symbols and that hint. Window binding and the link never use this mapping, so it does not explain the sidebar going away or "not connected".
- **Why.** Each named profile's `Extensions.plist` has two entries of Margin's. `io.tirth.margin.Extension (UNSIGNED)` is the ad-hoc build of 2026-10-24: `RemovedDate` 2026-10-30, `Enabled` false, `ProfileIdentifier` 5068E0E4… (Personal) and BE3F2B99… (Test). `io.tirth.margin.Extension (VSNN5UW99X)` is the signed build, added 10-29, live, with 8D454F81… and 4282EA5B…. Safari keeps a removed extension's state. `marginProfile` took the first matching key in sorted order, and "(U" sorts before "(V", so it always took the removed one. Only the plists' structure and Margin's own entries were looked at, never another extension's settings.
- **The fix** (`SafariProfiles.entries`). Every Margin entry in the profile is collected (the bundle id alone, or `<bundle id> (<team>)`), and the one used is chosen in this order:
  1. never a removed one: a `RemovedDate` later than its `AddedDate` (and later than its `EnabledModificationDate`, if it has one). An entry added again with an old `RemovedDate` left on it counts as there.
  2. the key with Margin.app's own team, `(<team>)`, or `(UNSIGNED)` for an ad-hoc build. The team is read once from Margin.app's own signature with public Security API (`SecCodeCopySelf`, `SecCodeCopyStaticCode`, `SecCodeCopySigningInformation` with `kSecCSSigningInformation`, `kSecCodeInfoTeamIdentifier`). The extension inside is signed by the same team. It's `SafariProfileWatcher.Options.team`, so tests set it.
  3. one whose copy Margin knows from a hello (`browser.json`'s profiles and the linked copies, asked as each read starts).
  4. an enabled one, then the newest `AddedDate`, then the key.
  None live means no copy, and the profile keeps Margin's typed name. Nothing pairs a profile with a copy by elimination. `Enabled` alone decides nothing: Safari leaves it true on some removed entries (the `margin-move-test` entries on Tirth's Mac have `Enabled` true and a `RemovedDate`).
- **Nothing wrong was stored.** `browser.json` has no `browserId` for `s-8d454f81-…` or `s-4282ea5b-…` (the wrong ids never linked, so they were never saved), so the right names show at the first read after the update.
- **The log.** Each profile now says which entry it came from and how many Margin has there: `“Personal” (A8C3B0E9-…, person.lanyardcard.fill) → s-8d454f81-… (VSNN5UW99X, 2 entries)`. A profile with entries but none live says `no copy yet (1 entry, none live)`. Ids and team ids only, never another extension's key. After every read (not only a changed one), each linked Safari copy with an `s-<uuid>` that no profile in that read maps to is logged once: `safari: copy s-… isn't any Safari profile's as read (N profiles, copies …)`. It's said again only after a read has mapped it in between. Nothing is said before a read, nor without Full Disk Access, nor while the database is busy.
