A Safari copy's hello asks for a read 0.3 s later, so a new copy is checked within about a second.
- **Tests** (`tools/app-tests/safari-profiles-tests.swift`, FakeSafari fixtures only):
  - Tirth's layout: `(UNSIGNED)` removed and off with id A, `(VSNN5UW99X)` live with id B, and a `move-test` entry. With team VSNN5UW99X the result is B, and with no team it's still B. On the old code the read gave A (the sorted first).
  - Only a removed entry gives none.
  - Two live entries: no team gives UNSIGNED's, and the team gives the team's.
  - `Enabled` true with a `RemovedDate` beside a live entry gives the live one, and alone gives none.
  - An entry added again (or turned on again) after an old `RemovedDate` is taken.
  - With neither of two live entries the build's team, the known copy wins, else the newest, and an enabled one beats a newer one that's off.
  - A read of both named profiles laid out as on Tirth's Mac gives Personal → B and Test → its own live id. The log line says `(VSNN5UW99X, 2 entries)` and never names the removed id or another extension. `useBrowserNames` then gives `s-8d454f81-…` “Personal” and saves its `browserId`.
  - The "isn't any profile's" line: nothing before a read, once after one, nothing without the permission, and again after a read mapped it in between.
  - The UNSIGNED-only test stays as it was.

### A profile's spaces follow it across a signing change

- **What could happen.** Not what happened in these runs. The data sets match the hello ids exactly (`data/safari/` holds `s-4282ea5b-…`, `s-8d454f81-…` and `s-default`), and nothing was split or lost. But the plists show it already happened once: the unsigned build of 10-24 had ids 5068E0E4… and BE3F2B99…, and the signed build got 8D454F81… and 4282EA5B… for the same profiles. That was before Margin.app's data store existed, so nothing was lost then. Safari keys an extension's state by `"<bundle id> (<team or UNSIGNED>)"` and makes a random `ProfileIdentifier` for each key and profile. So a build signed by another team, or `npm run safari -- --unsigned` over a signed install, gives every named profile's copy a new `s-<uuid>`. `takeProfile` takes it (acked and rev reset), the app has no set for it and asks for the copy's, and the copy's fresh defaults become that id's set. The real spaces stay under the old id, listed in `across` as another unlinked “Personal”. Only the default profile is safe (always `s-default`).
- **`npm run safari` asks first** (`scripts/signing-check.sh`, sourced by `safari.sh`). It compares the installed copy's `codesign -dv` `TeamIdentifier` (`not set` for ad hoc) with the team about to be used, or `not set` with `--unsigned`. When they differ, it warns that named profiles' spaces start fresh and asks `Build and install it anyway? [y/N]`. Anything but y stops it before `xcodebuild`, with nothing installed. `--yes` goes on without asking. Nothing installed, or nothing codesign can say, goes on as before.
- **The app carries the set over** (`DataSync.carry`, `BrowserProfiles.carrySource`/`mightCarry`/`carry`, `ShellProfiles.swift` `needChoice`/`carryOver`). The anchor is Safari's own id for the profile. `browser.json` keeps each Margin profile's `browserId` from an earlier read, and the read maps the new id to the same Safari profile. With the previous section's fix, the first correct read after this update saves `browserId` for `s-8d454f81-…` and `s-4282ea5b-…`. A profile never read correctly before a signing change can't be carried.
  - **Which set.** The source for a new id N is the profile O with `browserId` = the Safari profile the last read maps N to. O must be an `s-<uuid>` (never `s-default`), not mapped by that read, not linked, and must have a set. With two such, the one linked last.
  - **When.** At N's `data.hello`, if N has no set: if the read already maps N, at once. If no read maps N yet and some unlinked profile with a `browserId` has a set, the hello is held up to 3 s (`DataSync.holdFor`), and Safari's profiles are read at once. The new entry in Safari's plist can come after the copy's first hello, which is why there's a hold. The hold ends when a read maps N (carried over, or let go if nothing is to be carried) or after 3 s, when data.need goes as before. After each read, every linked Safari copy is checked again. After a new copy's first set is stored from its snapshot, it's checked again too: a set still at rev 1, that is, untouched since, can be replaced.
  - **Never** when N's set has changed since its first one (rev past 1: a copy that already sent changes), while N's copy's snapshot is on its way, while O is linked, or with no read (no Full Disk Access: today's behaviour, a fresh set).
  - **What N gets.** O's set, with O's `acked`. N's own items that O's set doesn't have (from its first set, or its held hello's queue: the fresh copy's defaults) become deletions, newer than they are, so N's copy ends up with O's set alone. Items both have stay O's: a fresh copy's defaults (its settings) are newer and would otherwise win. The held hello is then answered as a relink with nothing queued, so the copy gets the whole set and an ack. A set replaced after its first is sent to the copy as a snapshot.
  - **The old set.** It's put away, not deleted: its folder is renamed to `data/<browser>/.archived/<profile>-<ms>/`, where `profiles()` never looks.
  - **Names and order.** O's name, emoji and color (the color it was shown with) and its places in the one order go to N, and O is forgotten (hidden), so `across` has one “Personal”. The log says `safari: s-old → s-new (Safari profile X): data carried over (N items, M of the new copy's own left out; the old set put away)`.
  - **The switch back** (the old build again: Safari adds its entry again, with the old id) carries the set back the same way, so it never ping-pongs onto a fresh set.
- **Not known:** whether a build signed otherwise also gets fresh extension storage. If Safari keeps it, the copy's queue holds the real items, and the ones with ids in O's set stay O's (the same items, normally with the same times), so nothing is merged twice. Changes that copy made while unlinked are taken from O's set's version. That's acceptable for a signing change, which Tirth makes by hand.
- **Tests.**
  - `tools/app-tests/data-tests.swift` (`dataCarry`):
    - a hello held, then carried: the set, the defaults deleted, the relink's snapshot and ack, the old folder put away, the log line;
    - not from a linked profile, and not twice;
    - the hold running out gives data.need;
    - with no read, data.need at once;
    - a first set from the copy's snapshot carried over by `onFirstSet`, the copy getting the carried set and an ack with its rev;
    - a copy that sent changes since its first set gets no carry;
    - not while its snapshot is on its way;
    - the switch back.
  - `safari-profiles-tests.swift`:
    - `carrySource` and `mightCarry` before and after the read that maps the new id;
    - neither while the old one is linked, with no set, for `s-default`, or with no read;
    - `carry` moving the emoji, color and places and leaving one Personal.
  - `tools/app-tests/e2e-data.swift` (`carryScenario`): the real link and shell watching a stand-in Safari folder (`fake-safari.swift`, now shared by the unit and end-to-end builds).
    - Personal's copy links and its set is stored.
    - A new copy's hello comes before the plist changes and is held, with no data.need.
    - The plist's team entry is removed and an UNSIGNED one added. The read maps Personal to the new id, and the copy gets Personal's set (a renamed space included).
    - The old set is put away, with one Personal and the log line.
    - The switch back: the plist read first, then the old copy's hello gets the set at once.
  - `tests/signing-check.test.js`: the same team says nothing; unsigned over signed, and signed over unsigned, warn and ask, and no answer stops; another team: y goes on, n stops, `--yes` goes on without asking; nothing installed, or nothing from codesign, goes on.

### Fast tab switching: one target per chain, a highlight that never goes back

- **What Tirth saw.** "Tab switch still flickers when I'm moving through tabs fast through shortcuts." Part of that is the sidebar's highlight. The code let it go back to a tab already passed, and lag behind the keys. The page itself blinking as Safari swaps tabs is Safari's own, and this doesn't change it.
- **Why the highlight went back.** The copy's `switching` push for the next press reached the overlay before the state change of the press before it. That change waits for Safari's storage event, 100 to 120 ms after the write, plus the link's 16 ms flush. Each `start()` took the state's tab in front as the switch's start, so when the late change for tab X came in during the switch to Y, X counted as "another tab came to the front", the switch ended, and the sidebar drew X until Y's change came: 1, 2, 3, 2, 3. A switch to the tab the state still had in front (next then back) called `end()` outright, and the passed tab's late change then drew it: 1, 2, 1, 2, 1. The same `end()` was in a click on that row and in a click on its space. real.txt has 7 of 66 chained key switches where the next press was asked 11 to 123 ms before the previous one's change went out. The log can't show whether each one bounced, since its lines close on the first delta after the activation.
- **Why it lagged.** Every press waited its turn in one queue: the previous press's `tabs.update` and its answer, the window check, its state write, then a `tabs.query`, and only then its `switching` push. Each press was a whole switch, so a held key took Safari through every tab, one round trip each, and the highlight moved one tab per round trip.
- **The chain (shared/switching.js).** With `front` (Margin.app's overlay), a switch keeps a chain record: `from`, the tab in front when no switch was under way, and `passed`, the earlier targets. `end()` clears it.
  - The state with `from` or a passed tab in front is the chain's echo: the highlight stays on the newest target.
  - A passed tab counts as an echo for 300 ms (`PASSED_MS`) after the state first has it in front. After that it's a real switch the chain didn't make, such as Safari showing a neighbour after the target closed, and the highlight follows it.
  - A target the state already had in front when it was asked for (back to the chain's start, or to a passed tab already shown) lands only once the state has had another tab in front since then, or `SETTLE_MS` after its answer.
  - Any other tab in front ends the switch at once, as before.
  - New `toFront(tabId, request)`: with a chain under way it goes on to that tab; otherwise it ends. `ui/runtime.js` uses it in `switchTo` (the copy's push), for a click on the tab in front, and for a click on its own space. The `landed` option is gone; the logic lives in the module.
- **The keys (background/navigation.js).**
  - **The target at the press.** It's the chain's last target within `NAV_CHAIN_MS`, else the tab the cached state has in front. Only when neither is known (the state not synced yet, or a space switch ahead in the queue) is it worked out at its turn with `tabs.query`, as before.
  - **The overlay is told at once.** `switching { tabId, switchId: 'nav-<seq>', chain, seq }` goes to the window's overlay through `appLink.push(windowId, …)` (`tabs.js tellApp`). It's within 5 ms of the press in the tests. The page in front is still told as the switch starts (`tellPage` with `app: false`).
  - **One switch with Safari per window.** Presses that come meanwhile wait, and a newer one of the same kind replaces the one waiting (latest wins). The first press goes at once, and a press after the switches are done is never skipped.
  - **Every `switching` gets a word with its `switchId`.** That's done or failed, and a press that was replaced gets a done. The overlay passes `switchId` to `backgroundSwitches().begin`, so only its own word settles it.
  - **No waiting on the state write.** The activation's write is still made (a tab handed off is left out of Safari's own), but it isn't waited for.
  - **A press back to the tab the last switch showed** sends nothing to Safari when Safari's last activation there was that tab.
  - **Spaces work the same way.** One `showSpace` at a time per window, the newest waiting. Each target is worked out at the press, from the chain's last space (`cycleSpace` now takes `from`). The peek comes once the presses are done.
  - **A chain's own activations don't restart it.** `background.js` calls `navActivated`, which leaves the chain alone for a tab one of its presses asked for (within 1.5 s) or a tab of a space it went to. Before, the activation of a tab still with Safari reset the chain while a newer press waited, so the next press counted from the wrong tab.
  - **One press heard twice counts once.** The app's `navigate` used to come in as `page`, the same source as a page's keydown, so a press both heard counted twice. It's now `app`, and the 150 ms check drops the second.
- **Held space keys.** Tirth's 2026-11-05 decision stands: they repeat. `AppShortcuts.onCommand` now says whether a call is a repeat, and `Shell.run` passes it to `spaceKey`. The new `BrowserProfiles.spaceStep` decides:
  - a space of the window's own profile: `.own`;
  - another profile's space, or on from an aim that's already another profile's: `.across` for a press, `.held` (nothing) for a repeat.

  So a held ⌃⌘→ goes through the profile's own spaces and stops at the next profile, and a press starts at most one switch of profile and its full-screen windows. A press on from an aim that's already another profile's goes across to that profile's next space. Before, it sent the copy of the window still in front a `setSpace` for another profile's space. The page path still drops held space keys (`shared/shortcuts.js`, whose comment now says it's that path only).
- **What it doesn't do.**
  - Safari's own blink as it swaps the page isn't changed.
  - A press still shows each tab Safari gets to while a key is held: with 120 to 180 ms updates, 12 presses 83 ms apart made 7 or 8 updates in the tests, not 12.
  - The 300 ms for a passed tab is a guess. A Safari slower than that between two activations' changes would draw the passed tab for a moment. (Fixed in "Review fixes" below: the 300 ms start only once the newest target's request is answered.)
- **Tests.**
  - `tests/switching.test.js`:
    - a chain where the previous change comes after the next `switching` draws 1, 2, 3;
    - next then back, with the word for 1 and a change that isn't the front's before 2's change, draws 1, 2, 1;
    - a click on the start row during a key chain;
    - the target closing with Safari showing a passed tab, followed within 300 ms;
    - a tab outside the chain ends it at once;
    - a new chain after one is over.
  - `tests/switch-plain.test.js` (the real background, a fake Safari with 14 tabs):
    - 12 presses 83 ms apart, with 120 to 180 ms updates, each told to the overlay within 5 ms. Safari got fewer than 12 updates, all forward and the last on the 12th target. The highlight never went back, every `switching` had its word, and nothing came after;
    - a press after the switches are done goes at once;
    - the app's key and a page's keydown for one press count once (this failed before: two switches);
    - a chain's own activation doesn't restart it (this failed before, with the old reset: 14, 13, 13);
    - six space presses land on the sixth press's space with fewer than six switches.
  - Swift `tests.swift`:
    - a held key marks its repeats;
    - a held next-space with three profiles repeats eight times, from a profile's first space stays in that profile, and from its last makes one switch of profile;
    - `spaceStep`'s cases.

    With `.held` taken out, this gives six and seven switches.
  - WebKit host `fast-keys`: four app key presses while Safari is slow with the first. The first and last tabs come into view once each, the two between never do, and each push has its `switchId`. Against the code before: the two between came into view, and no push had a `switchId`.

### The overlay's keyboard and its page's focus stay in step

- **What happened.** At 21:53:03.690 the overlay for `#795` (a new Personal window with Margin's new-tab page in front) was made. At 03.996 the log says `#795 took the keyboard, but it isn't shown`, the page was drawn at 04.189, and the overlay was shown at 04.938. Nothing made the panel key then. The next `took the keyboard` is at 17.591, so the keyboard was given back in between with no line. real.txt has the same at 19:58:12 for `s-default#73`, which was never shown. No `palette opened` line goes with either, so the field was the new-tab launcher's, which `palette.js` focused as the theme first drew, before `ready`. The panel only becomes key in `takeKeyboard`, and only if the overlay is shown, and `show()` never looked at the keyboard. So the launcher looked focused while the keys went to Safari.
- **Two more ways to the same thing.** Every read that doesn't show an overlay calls `hide()`, and `hide()` gave the keyboard back without telling the page. The page kept its field focused, and `overlay.js` doesn't send `focus` on twice, so after a put-away and a show (out of and into full screen, a binding dropped and made again, a Space change with `ownSpace` off) the palette or launcher looked focused with no keys. And the launcher's focus at the first drawing came at every Margin.app start, relink and page reload over a window with a new-tab page in front, in a window the person may not be looking at.
- **Now** (`Overlay.swift`):
  - The overlay keeps what its page last said (`pageWantsKeyboard`, with when) apart from whether it has the keyboard (`hasKeyboard`). The keyboard is taken only while the overlay is shown. A `focus` on while it isn't is noted (`…: a field of its page has the focus, but the overlay isn't shown: the keyboard waits for it to show`) and waits.
  - As an overlay goes from not shown to shown, the shell calls `appeared(front:focusLauncher:)`. A field focused while it was hidden gets the keyboard (the web view made first responder, the panel made key) only if this is the overlay over the front app's front window (`Shell.frontOverlay`) and the focus is at most 2 s old (`focusFresh`). Otherwise the page is told to let go.
  - Letting go is a new push, `blur`: the page closes the palette, blurs what has the focus and says `focus` off. A shown overlay put away with a field focused gets it; one that was never shown doesn't, since what its page focused is waiting for the show. A press anywhere while a hidden overlay's field is focused sends it too.
  - Each time the keyboard goes back the log says why: `… gave the keyboard back: put away: <why>`, `… its field let go of the focus`, `… a press on the page or another app`. When the page is told to let go without having had it: `…: its page told to let go of the focus: shown, but not over the front window` (or `shown N ms after its field took the focus`, `a press while it wasn't shown`).
  - An overlay concealed through a blip (a window missing from one read) keeps the keyboard as before. If its window's binding drops, `hide()` gives it back and tells the page.
- **The launcher's focus** (`ui/behaviors/palette.js`, `ui/host-app.js`). A host can say `focusLauncherAtMount: false`; the app's host does. Then the launcher isn't focused as the runtime first draws. A launcher that comes later (a new tab in the window) is still focused as it shows. The app says when to focus it: the new `shown` push, sent as the overlay shows, has `focus` true only over the front window and only for a window its copy heard made within 10 s (`Shell.launcherFocusWithin`, from the window's `createdAt`). That's ⌘N and New Window. Margin.app starting, a relink, or a page reloaded after its process died leave the keys where they were.
- **What this changes that you can see.** A palette open as its overlay is put away closes (before, it stayed open with no keys and needed a click). The launcher over a window that was already there isn't focused when Margin.app starts or links again; click it, or use the palette's key.
- **Tests.** `tools/app-tests/e2e-keyboard.swift`, against the real overlay page and Arcadia with a new-tab page in front:
  - at the start and after a relink, the launcher isn't focused and no keyboard is taken;
  - a new window in front: the launcher focused and the keyboard taken, after the show;
  - a field focused while hidden, three reads that keep it hidden, then shown over the front window within 2 s: `keyOnShow` 1 and no `blur`;
  - put away with the palette open: the keyboard back, `blur`, the palette closed and nothing focused, and still nothing focused when it's shown again;
  - focused while hidden and shown behind the front window, or shown after more than 2 s: no keyboard, `blur`, nothing focused;
  - a press while it's hidden lets go at once.

  Against the code before (with stand-ins for the new counters), 23 of its 32 checks fail. `tests/theme/arcadia.test.js` (WebKit and Chromium): with `focusLauncherAtMount` false the launcher isn't focused at the first drawing or a later one; `focusLauncher()` focuses it, not while the palette is open; a launcher that comes after the first drawing is focused. It fails on the code before.
- **Not done.** The harness can't make a panel key, so whether Safari really sends the keys to the panel at the show is for Tirth to check (TESTING.md). The 2 s and 10 s are guesses.

### Closing windows, dropped links and Safari's own sidebar

- **The own-Space fallback fired at a close.** At 21:57:20.337, as Safari quit, window 18278 read as off screen while its overlay read as on screen. The shell took that as macOS keeping the overlay in the Space showing and made every overlay join every Space for the rest of Margin.app's launch. 73 ms later 18278 was gone from the list: Safari orders a closing window out a read or so before it leaves the list. The earlier logs show the same off-screen-then-gone order for single ⌘W closes (19:30:51, 19:33:58, 19:37:19). It came after everything Tirth reported in that run, so it didn't cause it, but a Safari reopened within Margin.app's 10-minute linger got the every-Space overlays (put away and shown again on each Space change).
  - **Now** (`Shell.swift` `suspectOwnSpace`, `judgeOwnSpace`). The first such read still puts the overlay away at once, but only records a suspicion (the overlay, the window number, when) and logs what it read: `…: its overlay (window N) reads on screen while its window M is listed off screen (the Space changed N ms ago): put away; looked at again in 300 ms …`. The fallback comes only from a later read, at least `ownSpaceWait` (0.3 s) on, that still lists the window as off screen, with no Space change since, the link up, the window still its copy's and still bound to that number. The suspicion is dropped, with a `no own-Space fallback: <why>` line, when the window leaves the list, comes back on screen, is no longer its copy's or bound there, the Space changes, or the link goes down.
  - The fallback no longer lasts for the launch. Once no link and no overlay is left (Safari quit, every copy gone), `ownSpaceResetIfIdle` puts `ownSpace` back to the setting and says so. Overlays made after that join only their window's Space again.
  - A harness option, `harnessOwnSpaceCheck`, runs the check (and the one 0.25 s after a placement) in the harness, whose window source can now say an overlay reads as on screen while its window doesn't (`ScriptedWindows.overlayOnScreen`).
- **A dropped link's overlays were frozen.** A reconnecting link isn't in `links`, so its overlays were neither shown, hidden nor closed by the reads, and when the only link dropped `started` went false and the reads stopped altogether, the poll with them. So for the grace (6 s) a dimmed overlay could stay over a window that had left full screen, or over nothing after Safari quit. After a relink while Safari wasn't in front, the reads stayed off until Safari came to the front (19:44:10 to 19:45:00 in the earlier log).
  - **Now.** `started` stays true and the poll keeps running while a dropped link's overlays are kept. The reads include that link's browser, from the bundle id and the pid of its last hello (`Kept`), but the keys don't (nothing would take them). Each kept overlay is checked against the window it's over (`followKept`): put away if that window is gone from the list, off screen without being left in its own Space, moved or resized (out of full screen), or no longer fills its display. The log says `overlay put away (…): <why>, while its link is reconnecting`. A link that drops while its browser isn't in front has its overlays put away at once (still kept for the grace; the relink shows them again).
  - **Not done as the plan had it.** The plan said to hide them at once also when the only link drops. With Safari in front that's the common single-profile drop (an update reloading the extension), and hiding would make every such relink blink. Since the reads now go on and each kept overlay follows its window, I kept them shown there. A 1 s relink of the same copy with the windows as they were puts nothing away and shows nothing again (tested).
- **Safari's own sidebar moved Margin.** The earlier run (19:37:14.956-18.706, and twice more at 19:39:59-19:40:03) has a `Safari takes part of its window` line at nearly every step of Safari's sidebar sliding in: x 19, 213, 238, 239, 240, back 177, 54, 2, then 115 … 240 … 2, 0 (width 1511). The page in front reports each step as a `viewport` with the missing width guessed to be on the left, and `overlayFrame` carved the overlay to each one, so Margin's sidebar slid 240 pt right and back. A settle time doesn't help: the sidebar stays open 0.4 to 1.1 s.
  - **Now** (`Shell.overlayFrame`, `dockedLeft`). Decided by geometry. A part on the left is left under Margin's sidebar (the overlay stays over the whole window) when Margin's sidebar is docked (its page wants no pointer positions) at least that wide (its layout's left inset, measured from the overlay's own edge, so it doesn't flip with the carve-out). The page's box keeps its place: `boxFromLayout` takes the viewport's offset off the layout's left inset (tests/link.test.js "Safari's sidebar open"). A hidden Margin sidebar, or a part wider than the docked sidebar (a left-docked inspector, Safari's sidebar kept open past it), is carved out at once, as before. Bottom and right parts are as before. An edge within 2 pt of the window's is the window's (the slide ends 1 or 2 pt off).
  - **Not as the plan had it.** The plan also wanted the page to have only guessed where its area starts (no pointer origin). A pointer moving over the page while Safari's sidebar slides away measures every step (`learnOrigin` runs for each new size), so that condition would carve out the closing half of the slide again. Whether it was measured doesn't tell Safari's sidebar from an inspector either, so the decision doesn't use it.
  - **The page side.** `content/core.js viewportNow` adds `measured: true` when a pointer event gave the origin; `handlers.js` passes only `true` on, and `link.js` carries it in the window's report. It's for the log (below). app/PROTOCOL.md documents it. The rAF tell stays.
  - **The edge guard.** It stays placed while no left part is carved out. Why it didn't stop the reveals isn't known yet (moved events may go to Safari's key window and not by hit test). Each time a part on the left first appears, the log now says where the pointer and the guard were: `…: a part on the left, N pt (guessed), appeared: the pointer at (x, y) in the window, the edge guard x 0, 4 pt wide; left under Margin's docked sidebar (260 pt)`. That's for Tirth's Safari.
- **Margin.app's windows after Safari quit weren't overlays.** The 1512×33 windows at 0,0 and the 64×64 at 0,482 are what AppKit gives every app (Finder, Chrome, Notes have the same), and 760×812 at 376,50 is the set-up window. The run had been opened by hand, so it was a regular app and checkIdle kept it running while the set-up window was open. Nothing changed there. The log now says how it started (`Margin.app opened by hand: set-up window shown`, `… started for a link (the extension's note): no set-up window`, `… started with --background: no set-up window`), and once, while no copy is linked, that the set-up window is keeping it running.
- **Tests.** `tools/app-tests/e2e-overlays.swift` (`overlaysScenario`, and `sidebarSlideScenario` in its own shell), against the real overlay page and Arcadia:
  - the own-Space check: off screen for one read and gone the next, a ⌘W while the other window stays, and Safari quitting (its last window ordered out, then gone, the link dropped) don't fall back; a window listed off screen for 0.3 s with its overlay on screen does; after every link and overlay are gone, a new overlay joins only its window's Space;
  - a dropped link: the reads go on; a 1 s relink of the same copy puts nothing away and shows nothing again; a window out of full screen during the grace, and Safari quitting, put the kept overlay away within a read, not after the grace; a drop with another app in front puts them away at once; after the grace no overlay or panel is left (weak references);
  - Safari's sidebar: the logged x steps replayed at their logged times over a docked sidebar keep the overlay over the whole window, send no setLayout and keep the guard; a measured part under it isn't carved either; a 500 pt left part and one with Margin's sidebar hidden are carved out; a bottom inspector as before. (The harness's web views draw no frames, so each step tells the page it was resized, as a drawn page would hear.)

  Against the code before (the same checks with the old rules put back), 19 of the overlays scenario's 45 checks fail, and 5 of the Safari sidebar scenario's 17 (the frame rule put back alone). Unit: `overlayFrame` at each logged step, the 1 pt and 2 pt edges, the 500 pt part and one 1 pt wider than the docked sidebar, a docked sidebar with a bottom inspector, and `viewportMeasured` parsed (tests.swift); `measured` through the handler and the link (tests/app-feedback.test.js, tests/link.test.js).
- **Not done.** Whether Safari's sidebar comes out with the pointer over the guard is for Tirth's Safari. With Margin's sidebar docked, Safari's sidebar opened on purpose (⌘⇧L) now stays under Margin's, and so would a left-docked Web Inspector no wider than Margin's sidebar; neither moves Margin. The 0.3 s is a guess; a real fallback now waits that long (the overlay is put away meanwhile).

### The log for Tirth's next Safari session

- **What was asked.** A list of lines for Margin.app's log (os_log, subsystem `io.tirth.margin`), so Tirth's next session settles what the tests can't: why the window server's list blips, why a window isn't bound, what Safari's focus flags and page visibility do across profiles, how links end, and whether a fast key run ever moves the highlight back. Ids, counts, times and rule names only.
- **Already there.** The groups above had added most of it, so those stay as they are: the binder's missing-window and collapsed-read lines, settled focus's refusals (once per change), the rejecting rule of most focus events, the front window's changes (once a second), the visible rule's refusals; the copy's focus events and per-report flags; every link line (dispatch results, the replaced app, close codes both sides, a socket ended before its hello, the listener's states, the copy's start, kept-link tries, pauses, the native port, held notes with their times, the socket guard); `safari profiles` with each profile's entries and the copy chosen, and the copy no profile maps to; drawn on and off with why, the hot key's state when the copy heard a key, the palette push held or dropped, every hand-back of the keyboard and the show's decision; the own-Space suspicion, its cancel and fallback; the left part with the pointer and the edge guard (it says when the carve-out isn't applied: `left under Margin's docked sidebar (N pt)`); the launch path. `scripts/app-swap.sh` says `quit the running Margin.app (pid N)` and `started the new Margin.app in the background` (for "opened the new build").
- **Added, the binder** (`WindowMatcher.swift`):
  - A collapsed read's line now ends with what the last read that told something had (`the last read that told something had J at layer 0`) and what the source gave (`a list`, `an empty list`, `nil`). The line's start stays as it was, so a grep from before still finds it.
  - The end of each run of reads that told nothing: `safari: the window server's list has the browser's windows again after X ms (N reads told nothing)`.
  - A bound window that the log said was missing, listed again: `safari:…#K: window N back after X ms`. Only for windows whose missing read was logged, so one pair of lines per blip.
  - The focus events that ended with no line: one its copy no longer reports or that's minimized, one whose front window was a closed window's, and one that agrees with its binding (`… agrees with its binding (window N came to the front by …)`). A focus event whose window is missing from this read is weighed again when it's back, so it still says nothing until then.
  - The visible rule not weighed at all (the browser not in front, links held): `safari: the visible rule not weighed: …`, once per change of reason, only while a window is free.
- **Added, the copy** (`background/link.js`): each window shown or hidden, as its tab in front says, one line per change: `window N hidden at HH:MM:SS.mmm (content script, tab T, heard X ms later)`. The source is what said it: `content script` (`content/core.js`), `new-tab page` (`newtab/newtab.js`), or `unknown` (the message now carries `from`, and `handlers.js visibility` maps it). It's looked at 150 ms after a word, against the window's tab in front from the last report, so a tab left behind as another is shown (it says hidden first) isn't taken for the window being hidden. "Heard X ms later" is how long the word took to reach the background. Together with `the active Space changed`, these say whether a full-screen window on a Space that isn't showing says hidden, and how fast.
- **Added, the app's start** (`AppDelegate.swift`): once an older Margin.app it asked to quit is gone, `ended an older Margin.app (pid N) in X ms`.
- **Added, fast switching:**
  - `background/navigation.js`: one line per key run. A run is a window's presses until 400 ms after the last one's switch is over with none waiting. The line has its tab and space presses, how many switches Safari was asked for, how many presses a newer one took the place of, presses heard twice, presses already in front, failures, and how long from the last press to landing (Safari's activation of its tab, or its answer). It also counts how many times the overlay was told an older press's target after a newer one's. That must be 0. Example: `key run in window 3: 10 tab presses, Safari asked 4 times, 6 skipped (a newer press took their place), landed 183 ms after the last press (Safari's activation); highlight told backwards 0 times`.
  - The overlay's own highlight (`shared/switching.js` `onRun`, `describeSwitchRun`; `ui/runtime.js` `onSwitchRun`; `app/overlay/overlay.js`): each chain as it ends, if it had two targets or more or didn't end on its target: `switch chain: N targets highlighted, ended on its last target X ms after the last; the highlight went back 0 times`. "Back" means the chain ended with the state's tab in front being the tab it started from or one it passed (the flicker), not a failure and not another tab coming to the front.
  - The blink in the page area is Safari's swap. Whether a recently shown tab gets a dark frame is read from WebKit's own lines with `/usr/bin/log` for one fast run, read only (TESTING.md has the command).
- **Tests.**
  - `tools/app-tests/window-tests.swift`: the blips case checks the back line after 42 ms and after the read without any window (99 ms), each collapsed line's new end (`3 at layer 0`, `a list`, `an empty list`), and one end-of-episode line for each. A new case checks a focus event agreeing with its binding, one for a window its copy no longer reports, and the visible rule's line while the browser isn't in front, once each time. Against the binder before, 7 of these fail.
  - `tests/link.test.js`: the window's shown and hidden lines with their source, tab and lateness, a tab behind and the same word again giving none, and a content script's word.
  - `tests/switch-plain.test.js`: the 12 fast presses give one key-run line whose counts match Safari's updates (asked, and 12 less that skipped), never backwards, and the overlay's chain ends with all 12 targets, on the last, never back. One press heard twice gives `1 press heard twice, counted once`. `handlers.visibility` maps `from`.
  - `tests/switching.test.js`: chains that land, go back to a passed tab past `PASSED_MS`, end on a tab outside the chain, or fail; the line's wording.
  - WebKit host: `visibility` checks the copy's `window N shown at … (content script, …)` line, and that tab A left behind saying hidden gives no ` hidden at ` line. `fast-keys` checks the run's line (4 presses, Safari asked 2 times, 2 skipped, never backwards).
  - The overlay's chain line isn't checked end to end in `tools/app-tests`. Tried there, the harness's overlay pages (off screen) started no chain from the copy's pushes, so the line and the chain's counts are checked in the node tests instead.
- **Not changed:** no behaviour. The `visibility` message's new `from` is for the log only.

### Review fixes (2026-11-06)

A review of the branch's diff made eight findings, seven of them distinct (two reviewers found the same `linksSeen` problem). Each was checked first, and all were real. Five were in the code and are fixed, with tests that fail before the fix and pass after. Two were in the tests: a check that could never fail, and a randomized run that checked bindings in only about a third of its runs. Those are fixed in the tests, and the behaviour they cover was right.

- **A copy gone for good no longer holds up full-screen elimination for the rest of Margin.app's run** (`WindowMatcher.swift`). "Every copy seen this session still reports" kept every link that ever reported, and the binder lives as long as Margin.app does. So after one profile's copy stopped for good (the socket guard, its background torn down, its last window closed, or Safari opened again with fewer profiles), no full-screen window was bound as "the only free window with its bounds" until Margin.app quit. A lone full-screen window then waited for its page or a focus change, and its copy was told "not drawn" after 1.5 s: the sidebar gone. Now each link has the time it last reported (kept fresh while it's held). It counts as away for `Timing.away` (5 s) after that, then it's forgotten. The list is cleared when the browser isn't running. A gone copy's windows that are still open are counted by their bounds (`serverHaving`), and missing ones by `missingFree`, as before. The 5 s keeps the case the rule was made for, a copy held and then let go while another's window is missing from the reads, never bound wrong. Tests: a copy gone with its window closed, then a new full-screen window bound after 5 s (it failed before: never bound); Safari quit and opened with one profile, bound at once (failed before). The older "B's copy seen and not reporting: still not" case still holds.
- **The front window and the Space change seen in one read are one transition** (the visible rule). A Space change was merged only into a front change seen before it. When one read saw both, the Space change came first, so the front change became a transition of its own, and the rule's threshold moved to the Space change plus 1 s. WebKit says the page is shown during or at the end of the slide, before that, and pages don't say it again. So the window was never bound by its page. In Tirth's log at 21:53:03 the front window changed 166 ms before macOS said the Space changed, and the Shell reads at once on that notice, so one read sees both. Now a front change whose "may have begun" time (the read before it) is at or before a Space change heard without one is folded into that transition, from the earlier time. A front change seen in a read after the one that saw the Space change is still a transition of its own, so a word between them isn't taken for it. Tests: both orders, in one read and across reads that told nothing (both failed before).
- **The randomized "pages" run now checks every run.** It checked bindings only in runs with every page saying and no Private window. Those are 293 of the 1000 runs. The other runs checked only "never wrong", and bound nothing at the end, because they ended with no focus change. Now those runs end with each window shown with its focus change, as Safari gives one when a window comes forward, and every full-screen window must be bound: 4000 of 4000 (1172 by the pages alone, 2828 with focus changes). The 40 Private-window runs end the same way and must bind all three reported windows. A new deterministic case covers three profiles with one page silent: the two that speak are bound as each is shown with its focus change. With a Private window, all three are bound. Behaviour is unchanged: with a silent page or a same-bounds Private window, the pages alone still bind nothing (never guessed), and a focus change does.
- **"No binding dropped for a window missing under 3 s" is measured.** It matched the drop line's words, and the line always says "(not listed for 3 s)", so the check could never fail. The test world now keeps when each window was last listed, and every drop for the window server must come at least `absent` after that. With `missing()` cut to 0.3 s, it fails in 40 of 50 runs (`window 102 dropped 1.10 s after it was last listed`).
- **A tab switch sends one or two `windows` reports, not three or four, and none of them wake the app's reads unless a window is unbound.** Each switch cleared the window's word and sent a report, then the new page's `visibilitychange` sent one and its answer to `reportVisibility` another (a new `visibleAt`). The old tab's "hidden" could also be sent as the window's, while the copy's last list still had it in front. Each report was pushed to every overlay of the link and set off a 1 s burst of window server reads and binder updates on Margin.app's main thread, the thread that handles the keys and the highlight. And each report's flags went to the log over the native port, with times that beat the dedupe. Now:
  - the same word again from a tab keeps its first time and sends nothing (`visibleAt` is "since when it has said so" now; PROTOCOL.md);
  - `tabActivated` puts the new tab in front in the copy's last list at once, so the tab left behind never speaks for the window;
  - the app passes a report that changes only `visible`/`visibleAt` to no overlay, and reads the window server for it once (not a burst) only while one of that copy's windows is unbound (`Shell.onlyVisibilityChanged`);
  - the flags line is written when the flags change (not their times) and at most once a second, as they are once the second is over.

  The `reportVisibility` ask on activation stays: it covers a page whose `visibilitychange` came before the activation and was cleared with it. Tests: `link.test.js` (the old tab's hidden never sent, the same word not sent, a change sent with its time, the flags line at most once a second), Swift `onlyVisibilityChanged`. The WebKit host's `visibility` scenario now reads the reports themselves (the link server's `windows` line carries each window's tab in front and word) instead of the flags line, which no longer has a line per report. Its checks are the same.
- **The highlight no longer goes back when Safari takes longer than 300 ms with a switch** (`shared/switching.js`). A passed tab in front counted as the chain's echo for `PASSED_MS` from when the state first had it. A Safari switch slower than that (450 ms) ended the chain on a passed tab while the newest target was still being carried out: `[1,14,13,…,5,14,4,3,9,4,3]`. Now the 300 ms start only once the newest target's request is answered. Until then a passed tab is the echo for up to `SETTLE_MS` (2 s), so a lost answer doesn't hold the highlight longer than that. Tests: the 12 fast presses with Safari at 350 to 600 ms a switch, and one at 1 s: the highlight never goes back and ends on the last press's tab, in one chain (it failed before). A unit case: held until the answer, then `PASSED_MS`, then followed; with no answer, followed after `SETTLE_MS`.
- **⌘T in the moments after a binding goes still opens something.** The copy hands ⌘T off while it thinks the app draws the window. That's until the app says "not drawn": 1.5 s for a window never bound, 3 s after a binding goes. It closes Safari's new tab and pushes the palette. The app held it 1.5 s and dropped it, and the user got neither a tab nor a palette. Now, when the app drops a held palette, it tells the copy "not drawn" at once (`DrawnOutbox.notShown`, past the grace). For ⌘T's hand-off (`newTab`) it also asks `paletteDropped`. The copy then opens Margin's new-tab page in that window and, for 10 s, keeps Safari's new tab and has its new-tab page show its own notice and address field, so the hand-off can't loop. That ends sooner when the app says it draws the window again, but not when it says so within a second of the drop: a binding that holds while its overlay can't show says it at once, and that mustn't start the hand-off and its drop again. The e2e check that locked in the silent drop now requires the `paletteDropped` request. `undrawn-window.test.js` plays it through the real background: the hand-off, the app's word, Margin's new-tab page opened, the next ⌘T's tab kept, still kept after a `drawn` at once, and handed to the palette after one a second later. Cost: about 1.5 s from ⌘T to the new-tab page in that case, the palette's hold.
- **Not changed:** the visible rule's gates (a silent page or an unreported same-bounds window still blocks it), the 1.5 s palette hold, the `drawn` graces otherwise.

## Margin 2: iCloud Tabs (2026-11-05)

On `feat/icloud-tabs` (from `feat/margin-app` at e3725be). Tirth asked for it on 2026-11-05: "It should show up in the sidebar as a live folder above the new tab stuff, and should have a good UI/UX as well, and also show up in the command palette for now." Margin shows the tabs open in Safari on the person's other Apple devices, read from Safari's own file by Margin.app, in a live folder in each Safari window's sidebar and as a section of the palette.

**What Safari keeps** (read on Tirth's Mac with Margin.app's permission: the schema, row counts, metadata keys, the device types and the position blob's format; never a title, an address or a device's name):
- `~/Library/Containers/com.apple.Safari/Data/Library/Safari/CloudTabs.db`, SQLite in WAL mode: `cloud_tab_devices` (4 rows), `cloud_tabs` (21), `cloud_tab_close_requests` (0), `metadata` (only `use_manatee_container` and `server_change_token`).
- **This Mac's own record:** nothing in `metadata` (or in Safari's preferences: its device identifiers have moved to the keychain, `DidMigrateDeviceIdentifier …`) says which device is this Mac. Two device rows have this Mac's name (its computer name) and its model (`com.apple.macbookpro-14-2021-space-gray`, a 14-inch 2021 MacBook Pro, `MacBookPro18,3`), both with no tabs. So a device is taken to be this Mac when it's a Mac named as this Mac is (`SCDynamicStoreCopyComputerName`), and a device with no tabs isn't shown either (as in Safari). Limit: a Mac renamed keeps its old name in Safari's row until Safari writes it again; another Mac with exactly this Mac's name would be hidden too.
- **The position blob:** zlib (78 DA header, deflate, Adler-32) around JSON, `{"sortValues":[{"changeID":0,"sortValue":-689,"deviceIdentifier":"<uuid>"}]}`. Every tab's `deviceIdentifier` was different (one per insert), and on one device the values ran 201, 1201, 1424, 2201, 2424, … : a fractional-index position, compared value by value (the number, then the identifier), a shorter list before a longer one it begins. A tab whose position doesn't read goes after those that do, newest viewed first (`last_viewed_time`).
- **`device_type_identifier`:** `com.apple.iphone-13-pro-9`, `com.apple.macbookair-15-2025-silver`, `com.apple.macbookpro-14-2021-space-gray`: Apple's model identifiers. `iphone`/`ipod` → iPhone, `ipad` → iPad, `vision`/`realitydevice` → Vision, `mac` → Mac, anything else a generic device (globe icon).
- **Times:** `last_modified` is rounded to the local day (all four were at a local midnight); `last_viewed_time` is to the second. "updated …" uses the newest tab viewed when that's newer, to the minute ("updated 5 min ago"), else the record's day ("updated yesterday").
- **Ephemeral and duplicate names:** none on Tirth's Mac (both flags 0, even for the two rows with the same name). An ephemeral device is shown like any other if it has tabs; a name that's Safari's duplicate (its flag) or the same as another shown device's gets the kind after it ("Studio (Mac)", then "Studio (Mac 2)").
- **Profiles:** there's one `CloudTabs.db` for all of Safari; no profile folder has one. Named profiles do have their own per-device rows in `SafariTabs.db` (`bookmarks`, `subtype` 3 for this device and 4 for others, under the profile's row, each holding windows of tabs), but on Tirth's Mac the other devices' entries there were nearly empty (one blank tab). **Decided:** the folder shows `CloudTabs.db`, the same in every Safari window whatever its profile. It's what "iCloud Tabs" means on every device, and it's predictable. Reading each named profile's own other-device lists from `SafariTabs.db` is a possible later step, once its format is seen with real data.

**How it's built:**
- **The app reads, never the extension** (`app/shell/CloudTabs.swift`). `SafariFiles.swift` now holds what the profile names and iCloud Tabs share: the access check (a read attempt), the read-only open (`SQLITE_OPEN_READONLY`, `mode=ro`, `immutable=1` when there's no `-wal`, 200 ms busy timeout; Margin never writes, vacuums or checkpoints), and the watcher (kqueue on the database and its `-wal`, FSEvents on the folder, a read at most once a second, the outcome handed on only when it changed), which `SafariProfileWatcher` now uses too. iCloud Tabs' watcher also skips a read when both files have the size, time and inode of the last good read.
- **To the overlays directly** (`ShellCloudTabs.swift`): in each Safari overlay's hello and as `{ type: 'cloudTabs' }` when it changed, the way `across` goes (`app/PROTOCOL.md` "iCloud Tabs"). The reads run only while a Safari copy is linked with the setting on.
- **In the UI:** `extension/shared/cloud-tabs.js` checks what came (ids, web addresses only, caps) and has Margin's words; `ui/view-model.js` builds `cloudTabs` (structural sharing keeps every unchanged row the same object, so a live update redraws only what changed and the list keeps its scroll); the runtime makes the model again once a minute while devices are shown, for "updated … ago".
- **Theme API:** a new slot, `<m-cloud-tabs>`, showing a new part, `cloud-tabs`, while `cloudTabs` is set. The part draws the whole folder on its own, so it can later be shown elsewhere (the "icon beside the sidebar's toggle" idea, `PLAN.md` §13) with no theme change. It's a guarded part: every word in it is Margin's, since its one button asks for a permission. New actions: `cloud.toggle`, `cloud.toggleDevice`, `cloud.open`, `cloud.copy`, `cloud.access`; palette rows gained `section` and `sectionStart`. Documented in `extension/themes/README.md`.
- **Look:** Arcadia draws it as one of Arc's folders with a sky-blue cloud in the folder icon's place, a count and a turning chevron, quiet device headings (icon, name, "updated …" in small grey), two-line rows (title over site), a divider under it. Neo-brutalism draws a bordered card with a sky-blue head and a cloud, each device a black tag with its icon and name (white when folded, dashed when stale), two-line rows with the site in small caps, and a yellow pin badge.
- **Where:** above the new-tab row, outside every drop zone (Arcadia: between the pinned area's divider and the tab list; Neo-brutalism: between the folders and the today zone). No `m-drag` or `m-drop` in it.
- **Collapse:** the folder and each device fold; kept in settings (`cloudTabsOpen`, `cloudTabsClosed`: Safari's device ids, devices gone are dropped as one is toggled), so it lasts and is the same in a profile's windows; shown at once, before storage comes back.
- **Click:** opens the address as one typed in the palette does: `newTab { windowId, url }` (this window, its space), or in place over Margin's new-tab page. There are no Cmd-click or middle-click conventions in the sidebar to follow, so none were added.
- **Menu:** Open and Copy Link (`copyText`, the app's pasteboard). Open in New Window isn't offered: `newWindow` takes no address, and adding one would change an existing request.
- **States:** no other devices with tabs ("No tabs are open on your other devices."), no Full Disk Access (one row and a button that opens System Settings at Full Disk Access, the URL the set-up window's step opens, now shared as `SafariFiles.fullDiskAccessURL`), no file ("No iCloud Tabs yet…"), a database that isn't Safari 27.2's ("couldn't read"). A device not used for 7 days is dimmed.
- **Icons:** Margin's stored icon for the site's origin (`state.favicons`), else a letter on a color. No request is made for them.
- **The palette:** iCloud tabs show only once something is typed (before typing: none, as Tirth didn't ask for recent ones). They come as their own section **after Margin's own rows (open tabs, pins, actions, the archive) and before "Search for …"** (an address typed still puts "Open …" first). Why there: the palette ranks Margin's own kinds on one score, and a section has to stay together; putting it after them never pushes the person's own tabs down and leaves the existing order exactly as it was, while a match on another device is still more specific than a web search. One run per device, headed "On <device>", the device with the best match first (as good a match: the one used most recently), at most 5 per device and 8 in all. A page already open in this window isn't offered again from another device.
- **Settings:** "Show iCloud Tabs: the tabs open on your other devices, in the sidebar and the command palette", on by default, right after the frame setting (Settings has no sidebar heading; adding one would move the existing fields). Only in Margin.app's Safari windows. Off, the app stops reading.
- **The set-up window's** Full Disk Access step now says Margin reads the tabs open on the person's other devices too (its title: "Show Safari's profile names and iCloud Tabs").
- **Privacy:** the app's log says counts only; tests use databases they make in a temporary folder and never read Safari's path (the folder is an option; nil in the harness); the scratch copies used for the research were deleted.

**Limits:** read only (Margin never writes Safari's files); no closing a tab on another device (`cloud_tab_close_requests` is Safari's); no favicons from Safari (the database has none); this Mac found by its name; profiles all see the same iCloud Tabs; Safari only for now (Chrome and Edge in Phase 5 through `sessions.getDevices()`, `PLAN.md`).

**Not done:** a WebKit host scenario (the host runs the extension, not the app's overlay, and iCloud Tabs never pass through the extension; the app's end-to-end test draws the folder in the real overlay instead); recent iCloud tabs in the palette before typing; the icon beside the sidebar's toggle (`PLAN.md` §13, later).

**Tests.** Unit (`npm test`) 696 (15 new: `tests/cloud-tabs.test.js` 14 and a guard test; three tests that list the actions, guarded parts and features extended). Theme (`npm run test:theme`) 108 (32 new: `tests/theme/cloud.test.js`, both themes in WebKit and Chromium: the folder above the new-tab row and outside drop zones, clicks, the menu, collapse, a live update touching one row's text and keeping the scroll, the no-permission row, the setting, the palette's section and Enter). App (`npm run test:app`) 513 unit checks (`tools/app-tests/cloud-tabs-tests.swift`: order, fallback, this Mac left out, empty devices, web pages only, kinds, a busy database, no permission, a missing file, Safari closed with nothing made beside the file, a file that isn't Safari's, a database changed while open, the watcher following the log with few reads, a read skipped when nothing changed, the permission withdrawn and given back, what's sent and what's logged) and 185 end-to-end (11 new, `e2e-cloud.swift`: a stand-in file drawn live in the real overlay, a row's press asking the copy for a new tab, the setting stopping the reads, the Full Disk Access row and its button), no compiler warnings with the Xcode target's settings.

**Open, for Tirth:** the `TESTING.md` checks ("Margin 2: iCloud Tabs"), first that this Mac isn't listed and that each device's order matches Safari's.
